“koḷḷiyilē nērttiyāṉa oru pāpilōṉiya cālviyiyum, 200 cēkkal veḷḷiyiyum 50 cēkkal niṟiyāṉa oru poṉ pāḷattiyum nāṉ kaṇṭu avikaḷi iccittu eṭuttukkoṇṭēṉ.”
eṉṟu ākāṉ colliyirukkiṟatip pārkkumpoḻutu, mikunta pūrva kālattilēyē mika alaṅkāramāṉa cāyaṅkaḷuṭaṉ nāṇayamāṉa vēlip pāṭuṭiyatāy maṟṟa tēcattavar virumpum paṭiyāṉa viliyuyarnta vastiraṅkaḷ neyyappaṭṭu vantaṉaveṉpatu veḷiyākiṟatu.
mēlum, paṭṭaṇattiṉ naṭumattiyil vāṉamaḷāviya uyarattuṭaṉum mikunta vistārattuṭaṉum ākāyattil toṅkuvatupōl kāṇappaṭum oru tōṭṭamiruntaJ. ovvoṉṟum 50 aṭi uyaramuṭiya eṇṇiṟanta ārccu (vaḷivu) kaḷāl tāṅkappaṭṭa mēlmāṭiyum, ataṉmēl cuṟṟi oru aṅkaṇamiṭṭu ārccukaḷāl tāṅkappaṭṭa iṉṉoru mēlmāṭiyum ippaṭiyē paṭippaṭiyāy oru aṅkaṇam kuṟittu anēka māṭikaḷuḷḷatāṉa oru piramāṇṭamāṉa kaṭṭaṭam kaṭṭuvittāṉ. akkaṭṭattiṉ mēlmāṭiyil cuṟṟi viṭṭirukkum ovvoru maṭṭattilum pōtumāṉa kipiṭiccuvarkaḷ eḻuppi, atiliruntu taṇṇīr oḻukātapaṭi īyattakaṭṭiṉāl mūṭi, atu niṟiyap periya maraṅkaḷum vērviṭumpaṭiyāṉa āḻattiṟku nalla maṇkaḷikkoṭṭi, atil tūratēcattiluḷḷa yāvarum kaṇṭukaḷikkum paṭiyākap palatēca virukṣaṅkaḷiyum kaṉitarum maraṅkaḷiyum puṣpacceṭikaḷiyum koṭikaḷiyum nāṭṭivittāṉ. ippaṭiyē oṉṟukkumēl oṉṟāy uyarnta ovvoru taḷattiṉ maṭṭaṅkaḷilum mēlmaṭṭattilum mikavum alaṅkāramāyc ceyyappaṭṭiruntaJ. ivikaḷi oṉṟiṉpiṉ oṉṟāy ēṟippārppataṟku aṉukūlamāṉa paṭikaḷum kaṭṭappaṭṭiruntaṉa. ivvuṉṉatamum vistāramumāṉa tōṭṭam pārppataṟku maraṅkaḷ aṭarnta oru ciṟu kuṉṟippōl mika alaṅkāramāyt tōṉṟumām. ākāyattōṭṭattiṉ kīḻppākam mikavum palamāy amikkappaṭṭiruntaJ. ovvoṉṟu nālaṭikkunālaṭi kaṉamuḷḷatum 16 aṭikkumēl nīḷamuḷḷatumāṉa anēka kal uttiraṅkaḷuṭaṉ piramāṇṭamāṉa kampaṅkaḷāl tāṅkappaṭṭiruntaJ. ovvoru māṭiyilum mikavum vistāramāṉa aṟikaḷ ērāḷamāyiruntaṉa. avikaḷiṉ kīḻēyuḷḷa mutal māḷikiyil, jaṉaṅkaḷ yāvarumkaṇṭu āṉantappaṭakkūṭiya nāṭaka cālikaḷum, kaṇkākṣicālikaḷum, puttakacālikaḷum, viḷiyāṭum iṭaṅkaḷum, iḷippāṟum iṭaṅkaḷum, kaṭikaḷum, iṉṉum palavitamāṉa cantōṣaṅkaḷukkuriya iṭaṅkaḷum amikkappaṭṭiruntaṉa. ataṉmēluḷḷa māṭikaḷil, rājya nirvākattukkuriya kaccērikaḷ oṉṟilum nītistalaṅkaḷ maṟṟoṉṟilum rājāṅkattukku upayōkamum anēkavita āṭamparamumāṉa stalaṅkaḷ vēṟoṉṟilumāka amikkappaṭṭiruntaṉa. atu, kālā kālaṅkaḷil tōṭṭakkaccēri ceyyavum āṉantam koṇṭāṭavumkūṭiya stalamāyiruntaJ. ivvuṉṉatamāṉa tōṭṭattukkut taṇṇīr tūkkik koṇṭupōvatu kūṭiya kāriyamāyillātatiṉāl āṟṟiliruntu taṇṇīr iṟippataṟkāka oru piramāṇṭamāṉa yantiram (nupēinē) iruntatākac collappaṭukiṟatu. ippaṭṭaṇam mika vistīraṇamum jaṉa aṭarttiyu muṭiyatāyiruntatiṉāl, oru pakkattil naṭakkum kāriyam maṟṟoru pakkattukku ilēcāyt teriyātām. ipperiya paṭṭaṇattit taṅkaḷ vācastalamākkikkoḷḷa anēka viyāpārikaḷum pirapukkaḷum aṅku vantucērntārkaḷ. atu, ulakattiluḷḷa ellāp paṭṭaṇaṅkaḷilum mikavum periyatākavum atikat tiraviyamuṭiyatākavum palamuḷḷatākavumiruntaJ. aṅkuḷḷavarkaḷ ellā vittikaḷilum kittoḻilkaḷilum mikac ciṟantavarkaḷāyiruntārkaḷ. aṅkē koli, kaḷavu, vipacāram mutaliya tīmikaḷum atikamāyiruntaṉa. pāpilōṉ rājaṉ, pakkatturājyaṅkaḷil kālākālaṅkaḷil paṭiyeṭuttu attēcattiṉ poruḷkaḷiyum jaṉaṅ kaḷiyum taṉ paṭṭaṇattil koṇṭuvantu nirappuvatu vaḻakkam. atupōlavē, pakkattiluḷḷa yāvarum, ippāpilōṉ rājyattiyum ataṉ makimiyiyum tāṅkaḷ aṭiyavēṇṭumeṉṟu ācippaṭṭuk koṇṭiruntārkaḷ.
caṟṟēṟakkuṟiya 2000 varuṣaṅkaḷāka nilittirunta pāpilōṉ rājyatti, pāpilōṉiyar taṅkaḷukkuc ceyta koṭumikaḷi niṉittu ati aḻittu akkiṉiyiṉāl koḷutti ati nācamākkavēṇṭumeṉṟu, palar tuṭittukkoṇṭiruntārkaḷ. pāpilōṉiyar pīlas eṉṉum teyvattiṟku oru periya uṟcavam naṭattikkoṇṭirunta camayattil acīriyā tēcattu rājaṉākiya kōrēs eṉpavaṉ, iṟṟikku 2454 varuṣaṅkaḷukkumuṉ ataṉmēl paṭiyeṭuttu aipirāttuāṟṟiṉ taṇṇīrip paṭṭaṇattukkuḷ tiruppip perukaviṭṭum akkiṉiyiṉāl koḷutti nācamākkiyum anēka jaṉaṅkaḷi veṭṭiyum araṇmaṉi vācal varukiṟavariyum pāpilōṉ rājaṉ aṟiyātiruntāṉ. ataṉmēl, rājaṉiyum koṉṟu paṭṭaṇattit taṉ vacappaṭuttikkoṇṭāṉ. nāḷ cellaccella ataṉ makimiyum kuṟintu paṭippaṭiyāy muṟṟilum aḻintu, maṇmēṭukaḷāyk kaṟkuviyalkaḷāyt tuṣṭa mirukaṅkaḷum jīvajentukkaḷum ceṭikaḷum niṟinta catuppu nilamāy nāḷatu variyum kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. yāttiri ceykiṟavarkaḷil ataṉ pūrva carittirattiyum ataṉ ciṟappiyum kēṭṭiruntavarkaḷ atiṉ naṭuvil pōkumpoḻutu piramippiṉālum āccariyattiṉālum oruvitat tikil piṭittavarkaḷā vārkaḷām. āntikaḷiṉ alaṟalum narikaḷiṉ ūḷiyum tuṣṭa mirukaṅkaḷiṉ kūccalum mikap payaṅkaramāyirukkumām. yāttirīkarkaḷ pūrvakālattiṉ nākarīkattik kuṟikkum cila aṭiyāḷaṅkaḷiyum kallil veṭṭappaṭṭa eḻuttukkaḷiyum taṅkaḷ tēcattiṟkuk koṇṭu pōvārkaḷām.
11. niṉivē nakaramum ataṉ ciṟappum ataṉ aḻivum
immakā pāpilōṉ eṉṉum nakarattiṟkum peritāyirukkavēṇṭu meṉṟu tikarīs āṟṟil nimirōttiṉāl kaṭṭappaṭṭa niṉivē eṉṉum nakaram, mūṉṟunāḷ payaṇam naṭantu cellakkūṭiya vistāramuṭiyatāka iruntateṉṟu collappaṭukiṟatu. 19 mil nīḷamum 11 mil akalamum 60 mil cuṟṟaḷavumuḷḷatāy āṟu laṭcattiṟku mēṟpaṭṭa  jaṉaṅkaḷuṭiyatā yiruntaJ. nūṟu aṭi uyaramum, mēl uyarattil mūṉṟu vaṇṭikaḷ pakkam pakkamāka ōṭakkūṭiya vicālamum, oṉṟoṉṟu 200 aṭi uyaramuḷḷa 1500 kōpuraṅkaḷuṭiya kōṭṭiyumiruntaJ. kōṭṭikkuḷḷāka uḷkōṭṭi iraṇṭum akaḻ iraṇṭumāka mikavum araṇippāyk kaṭṭappaṭṭiruntaJ. kiṟistuvukkumuṉ 862-m varuṣattil yōṉā piracaṅkikkappōṉa nakaram ituvē. immakānakaramum, kiṟistuvukkumuṉ 753-m varuṣam akāsvēru rājaṉāl, ippōtu kāṇappaṭum cila cuvarkaḷum akaḻkaḷum tavira, maṟṟa yāvum kuppi mēṭākkappaṭṭaJ.
12. akāsvēru rājaṉum rāja stirīyum ceyta viruntukaḷiṉ ciṟappu
akāsvēru rājaṉ intutēcamutal ettiyōppiyā tēcam varikku muḷḷa 127 nāṭukaḷi aracāṇṭāṉeṉṟu colliyiruppatōṭu, mikavum campiramāṉa viruntu taṉ atikārattiṟkuḷpaṭṭa nāṭukaḷiṉ atipatikaḷukkum pirapukkaḷukkum ūḻiyakkārarkaḷukkum 180 nāḷ ceytāṉeṉṟum, atu muṭintapiṉ taṉ rāja araṇmaṉiyiluḷḷa ciṅkārattōṭṭa maṇṭapattil taṉ araṇmaṉikku vantirunta periyōr mutal ciṟiyōr varikkumuḷḷa yāvarukkum ēḻunāḷ viruntu ceytāṉeṉṟum, appaṭiyē rājastirīyākiya vastiyumstirīkaḷukku oru viruntu ceytāḷeṉṟum collappaṭukiṟatu. estariṉ carittiram 1-m atikāram 6-m vākkiyattil maṇṭapattiṉ ciṟappip piṉ varumāṟu kāṇalām.
“aṅkē veṇkalattūṇkaḷiṉ mēluḷḷa veḷḷi vaḷiyaṅkaḷil melliya nūlum civappu nūlumāṉa kayiṟukaḷāl veḷḷiyum pacciyum iḷanīlamumākiya toṅkutirikaḷ vitāṉittiruntatu; civappum nīlamum veḷḷiyum kaṟuppumāṉa kaṟkaḷ patittirunta taḷavariciyiṉmēl poṟ carikiyum veḷḷiccarikiyumāṉa mettikaḷ vikkappaṭṭiruntaJ. poṉṉāl ceyyappaṭṭa nāṉāvita pāttiraṅkaḷilē pāṉam koṭukkap paṭṭaJ.”
iṉṟikku 2435 varuṣaṅkaḷukkumuṉ naṭanta oru tōṭṭak kaccēriyiṉ (Garden Party) ciṟappum collappaṭṭirukkiṟatu. ivvaḷavu ciṟappup poruntiya akāsvēruviṉ rājyamum cūcāṉ paṭṭaṇattuṭaṉ piṉṉoru kālattil aḻintupōyiṟṟu.
ivi yāvaṟṟiyum nām kavaṉikkiyil, jalappiraḷayattiṉpiṉ nimirōt mikavum pirapalamāṉa paṭṭaṇaṅkaḷik kaṭṭiṉāṉ eṉpatum, pirapalamāṉa cila paṭṭaṇaṅkaḷ avaṉ rājyaṅkaḷil talinakarkaḷā yiruntaṉaveṉpatum, nammuṭiya kavaṉattiṟku varāmalpōkā. ap pūrvakālattilē, taṟkāla nākarīkamuṭiyōrum mikavum viyantu pārāṭṭakkūṭiya kāriyaṅkaḷ anēkam iruntatākak kāṇalām. pāpilōṉatu vistīraṇamum, ataṉ amippiṉ oḻuṅkum, toṅkutōṭṭamum, periya yantiramum (nupēinē), eṇṇiṟanta pittaḷik katavukaḷum, piramāṇṭamāṉa ēriyum, 60 muḻa uyaramum 6 muḻa akalamumuḷḷa poṟciliyum, akkālattilirunta vāttiyakkaruvikaḷum, avikaḷi upayōkitta muṟiyum, 70 muḻa uyaramuḷḷa kōṭṭiyum, aticcuṟṟiyuḷḷa akaḻiyum, mikunta viyappittarāmalpōkā. akkālattil mikavum ērāḷamāṉa jaṉaṅkaḷukku 6 mātam allatu 180 nāḷvarikkum toṭarntu ceyyappaṭṭa rājaviruntum araṇmaṉit tōṭṭaviruntum ammaṇṭapattiṉ ciṟappum stirīkaḷukkeṉṟu rājastirīyāl ceyyappaṭṭa viruntumpōlat taṟkālattil nām kāṇpatu ilēcāṉa kāriyamō? jalappiraḷayattiṟkumuṉ 900, 930, 960 eṉṉum nīṇṭa āyuḷōṭu irunta jaṉaṅkaḷum rākṣatarkaḷum palavāṉkaḷum piraḷayattiṉāl aḻintapiṉ, maṉitar 120, 100, 80, 70 eṉṉum kuṟukiya āyuḷuṭiyavarkaḷāṉārkaḷ. piraḷayattiṟkup piṉṉuḷḷavarkaḷē ivvaḷavu pirapalamāka iruntiruppārkaḷeṉṟāl, cumār āyiram āyirattukku mēṟpaṭṭa varuṣam āyuḷpeṟṟa vīrarkaḷum rākṣatarkaḷum kaṭṭiya paṭṭaṇaṅkaḷ evvaḷavu periyavikaḷum ciṟappuṭiyavikaḷumāka iruntirukkavēṇṭum? ulakattil mutalmutal piṟanta oru maṉitaṉ oru paṭṭaṇattikkaṭṭi ataṟkut taṉ makaṉpeyari yiṭṭāṉeṉṟu colvatu, caṟṟu yōcaṉikkiṭamāyirukkiṟatu. jalappiraḷayattiṟku muṉṉuḷḷa kālatti oru yukattiṉ kaṭiciyāka vittukkoḷvōmāṉāl, avarkaḷukkup paṭṭaṇaṅkaḷum, nīṇṭa āyuḷum, kiṉṉaram nākacuram mutalāṉa vāttiyaṅkaḷiṉ viruttiyum, irumpu pittaḷi mutaliya kittoḻilkaḷiṉ mikutiyum iruntaṉaveṉṟu collattakutiyāyirukkum.
13. jalappiraḷayattiṟku muṉṉuḷḷa ēṉōk,
nōvā eṉṉum uttamarkaḷiṉ tapaR.
mēlum, maṉitarkaḷ jalappiraḷayattiṟkumuṉ eppaṭi atika āyuḷum tiṟamiyumuṭiyavar kaḷāyiruntārkaḷō,appaṭiyē teyva paktiyilum mēmpāṭaṭintiruntārkaḷeṉṟu teḷivāy aṟikiṟōm. ulaki veṟuttut teyvattōṭēyē cañcarittukkoṇṭiruntu uyirōṭē paralōkattukku eṭuttukkoḷḷappaṭṭa ēṉōkkiṉ tapacum, akkālatti liruntavarkaḷukkuḷḷē nītimāṉum uttamaṉumāyt teyvattōṭē cañcarittukkoṇṭiruntu, 300 muḻa nīḷamum (taṟkāla muḻamalla) 50 muḻa akalamum 30 muḻa uyaramumāṉa mūṉṟu aṭukkuḷḷa pēḻiyeṉṉum kappalicceytu koṇṭu, maṟṟavarkaḷ pāvavaḻiyi viṭṭu maṉantirumpap piracaṅkamceytu, jalappiraḷayattiṟkup piṉṉuḷḷa jaṉattiraḷukku mutalvaṉāṉa nōvāviṉpaktiyum, viḷaṅkiyatu antap pūrvakālattilēyē.
III. jalappiraḷayattāl aḻikkappaṭṭa tamiḻ nāṭukaḷum kalikaḷum.
1. jalappiraḷayattiṟku muṉṉuḷḷa carittiraṅkaḷippaṟṟik kāla niccayam ceyvatu caṟṟup pirayāciyeṉpaJ.
jalappiraḷayattiṟku muṉṉuḷḷa carittiraṅkaḷi nuṭpamākac collap pōṉāl, taṟkālattavarukkup piriyamāyirāJ. avikaḷ paḻamiyāṉa katikaḷāka eṇṇappaṭumēyoḻiya, uṇmiyāy naṭantaviyeṉṟu oppuk koḷḷappaṭuvatu caṟṟukkaṭiṉamākum. uṇmiyi aṟiyap pirayācippaṭum carittira ārāyccikkārar, pūmiyil putintirukkum piramāṇṭamāṉa pirāṇikaḷiṉ elumpukaḷikkoṇṭum maṉitarkaḷiṉ elumpukaḷik koṇṭum, kallil veṭṭappaṭṭa eḻuttukkaḷikkoṇṭum, pūrva cācaṉaṅkaḷikkoṇṭum, kaṇṭeṭukkiṟa paṇaṅkaḷiṉ muttirikaḷik koṇṭum, putintirukkiṟa nakaraṅkaḷikkoṇṭum, iyaṟkiyikkoṇṭum mēṟcolliyavikaḷip pārkkilum atikakkālaṅkaḷic colluvārkaḷ; atuvum uṇmiyē. intiyāvil atipūrvamāka eṇṇappaṭum ettaṉiyō paṭṭaṇaṅkaḷum kōṭṭikaḷum, oṉṟaṉpiṉ oṉṟāyt tōṉṟiyaḻintu, ikkālattil maṇmēṭukaḷāyk kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. avikaḷiṉ pērumpirastāpamum, akkālattavar parākkiramamum, kalikaḷiṉ ciṟappum, paktiyiṉ uyarvumpaḻaṅkatiyāyc collappaṭṭuvarukiṉṟaṉavē yaṉṟi, nitaricaṉamāka oṉṟiyum kāṇōm. itu kālattiṉ iyaṟki.
intiyāvil, piraḷayattukku muṉṉuḷḷakālattil, parākkiramamuḷḷa rākṣatarkaḷum, tapacil ciṟanta riṣikaḷum, cattiyamum nītiyum tavaṟāmal āṇṭukoṇṭirunta rājākkaḷum, mikunta parākkiramamuḷḷa pōrvīrarum, poruḷiṉ arumiterinta varttakarkaḷum, kittoḻililtērnta toḻilāḷarkaḷum, kalikaḷ ovvoṉṟilum tērnta vitvāṉkaḷum iruntārkaḷeṉṟu nām atikamākak kēḷvippaṭukiṟōm. intiyāviṉ pūrva carittiraṅkaḷukkut tiṭṭamāṉa kālavariyaṟi illātatiṉālum, avikaḷil mikuntakaṟpaṉikaḷ kalantiruppatiṉālum tiṭṭam collak kūṭāta niliyil niṉṟu tattaḷikkavēṇṭiyatāyirukkiṟatu. āṉālum, pūrvamāṉa cila nūlkaḷil aṅkaṅkē collappaṭum cilakāriyaṅkaḷiṉālum uṇmiyi oruvāṟu aṟiyavum niccayikkavum kūṭiyatāyirukkiṟatu. ippaṭi niccayikkakkūṭiyavikaḷippaṟṟiyum oṉṟukkoṉṟu ovvāmiyāṉa apippirāyaṅkaḷuṭaṉ oruvāṟu camātāṉattukku varavēṇṭiyatā yirukkiṟatēyoḻiya, uḷḷattu uḷḷapaṭi niccayikka iyalātatāyirukkiṟatu. appaṭiyiruntālum, ulakattiṟkup potuvāṉa cila campavaṅkaḷik koṇṭum nūlkaḷikkoṇṭum intiyāviṉ pūrvīkattippaṟṟi nāṉ colvatu, uṇmiyivicārikkum vivēkikaḷukku avikaḷi ñāpakap paṭuttak kūṭiyatāyāvatu irukkumeṉṟu eṇṇic cila vārttikaḷic collattuṇintēṉ.
2. tirāviṭa tēcat taracaṉākiya cattiyavirataṉum jalappiraḷayamum.
pākavata vacaṉam, eṭṭāvatu skantam, 24m attiyāyam.
“pōṉa kalpattiṉuṭiya antattil pirammāviṉuṭiya nittiri yiṉāl uṇṭāṉa nimittika meṉṟu collappaṭṭa piraḷayam uṇṭāccuJ. antap piraḷayattil pūmi mutalāṉa lōkaṅkaḷellāñ camuttirattil muḻukippōccuJ. appōtu kālavacattiṉāl nittiriyaṭintu cayaṉikka vēṇumeṉkiṟa icciyiyuṭiyavaṉāṉa piramatēvaṉuṭiya mukattiṉiṉṟum uṇṭākāniṉṟa vētaṅkaḷic camīpattilirā niṉṟa ayakkirīvācuraṉāṉavaṉ apakarittāṉ. appōtu srī pakavāṉāyum carva niyantāvāyumirukkiṟa srī hariyāṉavar, tāṉavēntiraṉāṉa ayak kirīvaṉuṭiya cēṣṭiyi aṟintu macciya rūpattit tarittār. antak kālattil cattiya virataṉeṉṟu peyariyuṭiyavaṉāyum makāṉāyum pakavāṉiṭattil paktiyuṭiyavaṉāyumirukkiṟa oru rāja riṣiyāṉavaṉ, jalattiyē pāṉam paṇṇikkoṇṭu tapacu ceytukoṇṭiruntāṉ. yātoru anta cattiya virataṉeṉkiṟa rājāvē intak kalpattil vivacuvāṉuṭiya piḷḷiyāyum cirārtta tēvaṉeṉṟu piracittaṉāyum srī hariyiṉāl maṉuvākak kalpikkappaṭṭirukkiṟāṉ. anta rājariṣiyāṉavaṉ, orukkāl kirutamālā natiyil jalatarppaṇañ ceyyumpōtu, avaṉuṭiya kiyil irāniṉṟa jalattil oru macciyamāṉatu iruntaJ. tiraviṭa tēcātipatiyāṉa antac cattiya virataṉeṉkiṟa rājariṣiyāṉavaṉ, taṉkiyilirāniṉṟa anta macciya jalatti natijalattil cērttuviṭṭāṉ. appōtu anta macciyamāṉatu makā tayāḷuvāṉa antac cattiya virataṉik kuṟittu mikavum tiriyattōṭu oru vārtti colliṟṟu.
“vārāy rājariṣiyē! iṉṟikku ēḻānāḷ intap pūmi mutalāṉa mūṉṟu lōkamum piraḷaya camuttirattil muḻukappōkiṉṟaJ. appōtu nammāl ēvappaṭṭatāyum vicālamāyum oru ōṭamāṉatu uṉṉi aṭiyappōkiṉṟaJ. nīyum camastamāṉa ōṣatikaḷiyum nāṉāvitaṅkaḷāṉa vittukkaḷiyum anta ōṭattil ēṟṟikkoṇṭu, captariṣikaḷōṭum carva palattōṭuṅ kūṭiṉavaṉāy antap peritāṉa ōṭattil ēṟikkoṇṭu, makā antakāramāṉa camuttirattil makāriṣikaḷuṭiya kaṭāṭcattiṉāl tīraṉāyc cañcarikkappōkiṟāy. appōtu makā palavāṉāṉa vāyuviṉāl alikkap paṭṭa anta ōṭattic camīpattil varā niṉṟa eṉṉuṭiya kompilē cērttu, makā carppattiṉāl iḻuttuk kaṭṭakkaṭavāy. appōtu nāṉ, riṣikaḷōṭukūṭa irāniṉṟa uṉṉiyum ōṭattiyumiḻuttukkoṇṭu, piramāviṉuṭiya rāttiri kālamāṉatu evvaḷavō, avvaḷavu kālamum camuttirattil cañcarikkappōkiṟēṉ. appōtu nī paṇṇappaṭṭa piraciṉaṅkaḷiṉāl parappirama corūpamāṉa eṉṉuṭiya makimiyi yatārttamāka aṟiyappōkiṟāyeṉṟu colliṟṟu. piṉpu camuttiramāṉatu varuṣikkappaṭṭa mēkaṅkaḷiṉāl virttiyaṭintu, karipuraṇṭu, pūmiyeṅkum viyāpittaJ. anta rājariṣiyum pakavat pātāravintattit tiyāṉam paṇṇikkoṇṭirukkum pōtu taṉṉaṇṭiyē varāniṉṟa ōṭattip pārttu, anta ōṭattil ōṣatikaḷiyellā mēṟṟik koṇṭu captariṣikaḷōṭuṅ kūṭa tāṉumēṟikkoṇṭāṉ. appōtu anta riṣikaḷellām cantuṣṭāḷāy anta rājariṣiyippārttu, pakavāṉuṭiya pātāravintattit tiyāṉam paṇṇuvīrākil nammi intac caṅkaṭattiṉiṉṟum raṭcippāreṉṟu collāniṉṟa anta riṣikaḷuṭiya vārttiyikkēṭṭu, rājariṣiyum pakavāṉiyē tiyāṉam paṇṇikkoṇṭiruntār.”
௸ pākavata vacaṉam oṉpatāvatu skantam, mutalām attiyāyam.
“tirāviṭatēcātipatiyāṉa cattiyavirataṉeṉkiṟa yātoru rāja riṣi yāṉavaṉ pūrva kalpāntattil makāpuruṣaṉāṉa srī pakavāṉuṭiya cēviyiṉāl uttamamāṉa kiyāṉattiyaṭintāṉō, anta rājariṣiyē ippōtu vivacu tamaṉuvāka irukkiṟāṉeṉṟu eṉṉāl kēṭkappaṭṭaJ. iṭcuvāku mutalāṉa rājākkaḷ anta maṉuviṉuṭiya puttirāḷeṉṟu ummālē collappaṭṭārkaḷ.”
itiṉiṉṟum aṟiyavēṇṭiyatu eṉṉaveṉṟāl, tirāviṭa tēcātipatiyāṉa cattiya virataṉ eṉkiṟa rājariṣiyāṉavar mikunta tapacupaṇṇikkoṇṭiruntāreṉpatum, akkālattil pūmi jalap piraḷayattiṉāl aḻikkappaṭṭateṉpatum, cattiyavirataṉ eṉkiṟa tirāviṭa tēcātipatiyum oru vicālamāṉa kappalil camastamāṉa ōṣatikaḷuṭaṉum nāṉāvitamāṉa vittukkaḷuṭaṉum captariṣikaḷuṭaṉum ēṟikkoṇṭu, maliyiṉ aṭivārattil taṅkiṉāreṉpatum, makāviṣṇu ippaktaṉik kāppāṟṟuvataṟkākat tamatu mutal avatāramākiya maccāvatāratti eṭuttāreṉpatumē, ivviruttāntam, cattiya vētākamattil jalap piraḷayattiyum nōvāviyum nōvā kāppāṟṟappaṭṭa pēḻiyiyum paṟṟic collappaṭṭirukkum varalāṟṟukku muṟṟilum ottirukkiṟaJ. iṭam māttiram āciyātturukkiyiṉ (Asiatic Turkey)  orupākamākavum intiyāviṉ teṉpākamākavum pētappaṭukiṟaJ.
3. tuvārakikkaracaṉākiya kiruṣṇapakavāṉum jalappiraḷayamum.
pākavatam, patiṉērāvatu skantam, 30-m attiyāyam.
srī pakavāṉ vākkiyam.
“vārāycārati! nī tuvārakāpaṭṭaṇattukkuppōy, pantukkaḷukku yātavarkaḷ oruttarukkoruttar yuttampaṇṇi aṭipaṭṭuppōṉatiyum, yōkamārkkattiṉāl palarāmar paramapatattiyaṭintatiyum, nāṉ iccācarīratti viṭṭuviṭṭatiyuñ collakkaṭavāy. pantukkaḷōṭē kūṭikkoṇṭirukkiṟa nīṅkaḷ tuvārakiyilirukka vēṇṭām. eṉṉāl viṭappaṭṭirukkiṟa intat tuvārakāpaṭṭaṇattic camuttiramāṉatu muḻukippōṉatākap paṇṇappōkiṟaJ. ākiyiṉāl nīṅkaḷ camastamāṉa pērkaḷum avāḷavāḷ pantujaṉaṅkaḷiyum nammuṭiya mātā pitākkaḷiyum aḻittukkoṇṭu arccuṉaṉālē rakṣikkappaṭṭavarkaḷāy intirappirastattip pōyaṭiyuṅkaḷeṉṟu pantukkaḷ poruṭṭuc collakkaṭavāy.”
௸pākavata vacaṉam, ௸ skantam, 31-m attiyāyam.
srī cukar vākkiyam.
“vārāymakāṉupāvaṉē! antakṣaṇattil camuttiramāṉatu srī kiruṣṇaṉuṭiya kirakatti viṭṭuviṭṭu avarāl viṭappaṭṭirukkiṟa tuvārakāpaṭṭaṇam camastattiyu muḻukap paṇṇiyaJ.
*   *   *   *   *    *    *   *   *   *
“piṟpāṭu arccuṉaṉāṉavaṉ acēṣarkaḷāyirukkiṟa pālaviruttarkaḷi yaḻittukkoṇṭu intirappirastatti vantaṭintu, avviṭattil vacciraṉukkup paṭṭāpiṣēkam paṇṇivittāṉ.”
௸ pākavata vacaṉam paṉṉiraṇṭāvatu skantam, 2-m attiyāyam.
“eppōtu pakavāṉāṉa srī viṣṇuviṉuṭiya amcamāyum cattuva corūpamāyumuḷḷa tēkamāṉatu srī vikuṇṭattiyaṭintatō, appōtē kaliyāṉatuṇṭāṉaJ. srī laṭcumipatiyāṉa srī kiruṣṇarāṉavar tammuṭiya pātapatmaṅkaḷāl sparicittukkoṇṭu pūmiyiliruntavariyil antak kaliyāṉatu pūmiyi ākkiramikkikkuc camarttuḷḷa tallāmaṟpōyiṟṟu.”
mēlē pākavatattiṉiṉṟum eṭuttuk kāṭṭiya cilapākaṅkaḷiṉāl, tuvāparayukattiṉ kaṭiciyil kiruṣṇapakavāṉiruntatākavum, tuvārakiyum aticcērnta iṭaṅkaḷum jalappiraḷayattil aḻintu pōkappōvati yaṟintu atil tam jaṉaṅkaḷ aḻintu pōkātapaṭi arccuṉaṉukku muṉṉeccarikkiceytu tamatu piṉṉaṭiyārkaḷi intirappirastam allatu taṟkālattil ṭilli eṉṟaḻikkappaṭum paṭṭaṇattiṟkuk koṇṭupōyk kuṭiyēṟṟac coṉṉatākavum, appaṭiyē arccuṉaṉ intirappirastat tiṟkuppōy avviṭattil vacciraṉukkup paṭṭāpiṣēkam ceytuvitta tākavum, kiruṣṇapakavāṉuṭiya tēkam maṟintavuṭaṉ kaliyukam uṇṭāṉatākavum terikiṟatu. iṟṟikku 5014 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ kaliyukam ārampittateṉṟu cātāraṇamāy nām yāvarum aṟivōm. tuvārakāpaṭṭaṇamō  (Dwaraka)  intiyāviṉ mēlpākattil sintu (Indus) natiyiṉ mukattuvārattukkuc camīpattiluḷḷa kac (Cutch)  nāṭṭiṉ teṉpuṟamāy kattiyēvāriṉ (Kathiawar) mēlkōṭiyil kaṭal ōramāyirukkiṟatu. kac nāṭum kattiyēvāriṉ perumpākamum jalappiraḷayattāl orukālattil mikavum aḻikkapṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu tiṭṭamāyt terikiṟatu. intak kattiyēvārukkuc camīpattiluḷḷa kac (Gulf of Cutch) kuṭāvum arapikkaṭaliccērnta pāracīkak (Persian Gulf) kuṭāvum ātēṉ (Gulf of Aden) kuṭāvum, ceṅkaṭalum (Red Sea) oru kālattil intiyāviṉ teṉpākattiluṇṭāṉa pūmimāṟutalāl taṅkaḷukku etirppaṭṭa tāḻnta pūpākaṅkaḷi aḻittuviṭṭaṉaveṉṟu nām niṉikka iṭantarukiṟaJ. pūmiyiṉ naṭuppākattil akkiṉiyirukkiṟa paṭiyāl, avvakkiṉi uṣṇam mikunta cilapākattiṉvaḻiyākap pūmikkuccamīpittup pūmiyiṉ mēṟparappi mēṭupaḷḷamākkic camuttirattit tariyākavum tariyic camuttiramākavum māṟṟivikkiṟa iyaṟkiyi aṉucarittu, arapiyā (Arabia) intiyā (India) parmā (Burma)  mutaliya tēcaṅkaḷukku etiriluḷḷa pūmiyiṉ perumpākaṅkaḷ jalappiraḷayattāl viḻuṅkappaṭṭirukkakkaṇṭumeṉṟu ūkikka iṭantarukiṟaJ. tuvārakā piratēcam aḻintakālattilēyē nōvāviṉkālattu jalappiraḷayamum uṇṭāyirukkalāmeṉṟu nām oruvāṟu niṉikkalām. iṟṟikku 4263 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉuṇṭāṉa teṉṟu collappaṭum piraḷayamum 5000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉuṇṭāṉateṉṟu collappaṭum piraḷayamum kālavittiyācamāyc collappaṭukiṟatētavira, maṟṟappaṭi orēkālattil naṭantatāka niṉikka ētuvirukkiṟatu. teṉṉintiyāvukkut teṉpākattilirunta 49 nāṭukaḷum ataṉ mukkiyanakaramākiya teṉmaturiyum kaṭalāl aḻikkappaṭṭaṉaveṉṟu pūrva tamiḻ nūlkaḷiṉāl pulappaṭukiṟaJ.
4. teṉṉintiyāviluḷḷa āviṭiyārkōyilum jalappiraḷayattukkut tappiya 300 cōḻiyap pirāmaṇarkaḷum.
ikkālattil mikavum nērttiyāṉa ciṟpavēlikaḷuḷḷatāka eṇṇappaṭum āviṭiyār kōyilil naṭanta cilakāriyaṅkaḷ intiyāvil cuṟṟuppirayāṇamceyyum pirayāṇikaḷukku ñāpakamirukkalām.

South India Railway Illustrated Guide 1913.
“In the village of Avudyarkoil is an ancient temple which though small, is considered one of the most perfect specimens of its class in Southern India. *   *  *  *  *  *
According to legends the temple occupies the site where, after the deluge, Siva with a colony of three hundred disciples called Solias was established for the purpose of propagating the Brahmin Religion.”
“āviṭiyārkōyililirukkum pūrvamāṉakōyil ciṟitāyirun tālum teṉṉintiyāviluḷḷa kōyilkaḷuḷ mikavum mukkiyamāṉa ciṟpa vēlippāṭukaḷuḷḷavikaḷil mutaṉmiyāṉaJ. ālayam ippōtu irukkumiṭattil, jalappiraḷayattiṟkuppiṉ cōḻiyarkaḷ eṉṟu aḻikkap paṭukiṟa munnūṟu peyarkaḷi aṅkē koṇṭuvantu, pirāmaṇamatatti viruttipaṇṇumpaṭiyāka, civaṉ kuṭiyēṟṟiṉāreṉṟu purāṇaṅkaḷil collappaṭukiṟatu.”
itik kavaṉikkumpoḻutu, intiyāviṉ teṉpākam orukālattil kaṭalāl viḻuṅkappaṭṭirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟum, tuvārakiyiliruntu intirap pirastattukku uyir tappa ōṭippōṉavarkaḷippōla itteṉpākatti liruntavarkaḷum taṅkaḷukkuc camīpattiluḷḷa uyarnta iṭaṅkaḷukku uyirtappa ōṭippōyk kuṭiyēṟiṉārkaḷeṉṟum colla ētuvirukkiṟatu.
5. jalappiraḷayattiṉāl viḻuṅkappaṭṭa iṭaṅkaḷum
piraḷayakālattiṉ kaṇakkum.
ippaṭi jalattiṉāl viḻuṅkappaṭṭa piratēcaṅkaḷiṉ cilapākam oruvāṟu kaṭalāl viṭappaṭṭup piṉ jaṉaṅkaḷiṉ kuṭiyiruppukkut takutiyāyiṟṟeṉṟu niṉikkavēṇṭum. ceṅkaṭalukkup pakkattiluḷḷa āppirikkāviṉ (Africa) cilapākamum arapiyāvum āciyāt turukki pāracīkam (Persia) pelukistāṉ (Baluchistan) ittēcaṅkaḷiṉ cilapākamum teṉṉintiyāviṉ kīḻpākamum kaṭaṟkarimaṇalkaḷāl nirappappaṭṭiruppatum, maṇal niṟinta iṭaṅkaḷil anēka kirāmaṅkaḷum paṭṭaṇaṅkaḷum aḻintukiṭappatum, maṇalukkuk kīḻē pūrvamāyuḷḷa taṉtarikāṇappaṭuvatum, orukālattil jalappiraḷayam uṇṭāki aṉēkatēcatti aḻittateṉṟu colla iṭantarukiṉṟaṉa.
jalappiraḷayam uṇṭāṉatu yūtarkaḷ (tunaṟaḷa) tāṅkaḷ collum kaṇakkiṉpaṭi ki.mu.  2015-m varuṣam atāvatu iṟṟikku 4019 varuṣaṅkaḷukkumuṉ eṉṟum, kiḷemeṉṭ alakcāṉṭriṉās (Clement Alexandrinas) eṉpavar ki.mu.  3475-m௵ atāvatu iṟṟikku 5389 varuṣaṅkaḷukkumuṉ eṉṟum, iyūripiyas (Euribius) eṉpavar ki.mu.  2459-m ´ atāvatu iṟṟikku 4373 varuṣaṅkaḷukkumuṉ eṉṟum, pīṭ (Bede)eṉpavar ki.mu.  3544-m௵ ´ atāvatu iṟṟikku 5458 varuṣaṅkaḷukkumuṉ  eṉṟum, arṣar (Ursher) eṉpavar ki.mu.  2349-m௵ ´ atāvatu iṟṟikku 4263 varuṣaṅkaḷukkumuṉ eṉṟum, hēls (Hales) eṉpavar ki.mu.  3153-m ´ atāvatu iṟṟikku 5067 varuṣaṅkaḷukkumuṉ eṉṟum collukiṟārkaḷ.
ivikaḷ oṉṟukkoṉṟu ovvāmiyāyirukkiṟatu iyalputāṉ; ēṉeṉṟāl, ikkālaṅkaḷi nirṇayam paṇṇukiṟavarkaḷ, piraḷayamuṇṭāṉa akkālattiliruntavarkaḷalla. ataṟkuc cumār 1000, 2000, 3000, 4000, 5000 varuṣaṅkaḷukkup piṉṉiruntavarkaḷ. jalappiraḷayam uṇṭāṉakālattil allatu ataṟkumuṉ vaḻaṅkiyakālattil uṇṭāṉa kālakkaṇakkukaḷ oṉṟum tiṭṭamāy akappaṭavilli. kālakkaṇakkukaḷ ovvoru rājyattiṉ mukkiyamāṉa paṭṭaṇaṅkaḷiluḷḷa pattiraṅkaḷ mutaliya eḻuttu ātāraṅkaḷ mūlamāy aṟiyappaṭavēṇṭum. appaṭi aṟivataṟkētu villāmal piratāṉa nakaraṅkaḷ aḻintupōṉapaṭiyiṉāl, ataṟkuppuṟampē yirukkum maṟṟavarkaḷcollum kālakkaṇakkōṭu nām tiruptiyāyirukka vēṇṭuvataṉṟi vēṟu eṉṉa ceyyakkūṭum? mikavum pirapalamāyirunta tuvārakiyum intirappirastam eṉṟaḻikkappaṭṭa paṭṭaṇamum veku kālattukku muṉṉēyē aḻintu aṭiyāḷam teriyāmal pōṉatupōlavē maṟṟum anēka iṭaṅkaḷum pōyirukkavēṇṭum.
6. jalappiraḷayakālattil aḻintupōṉa teṉṉintiyāviṉ perumpākam.
jalappiraḷayattiṉāl aḻikkappaṭṭa pala ciṟupākaṅkaḷittavira intiyāviṉ teṉpākattiluḷḷa perumpākamum, camuttirattil muḻukip pōyirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟu kāṭṭap pala cāṭcikaḷ akappaṭukiṉṟaṉa.
itivācikkum kaṉavāṉkaḷē, jalappiraḷayattukku muṉṉuḷḷa kālattil, kiṉṉaram nākacuram ceykiṟavarkaḷum avikaḷi upayōkap paṭuttukiṟavarkaḷum iruntārkaḷeṉṟu cattiyavētattil colliyapaṭi, teṉṉintiyāviṉ teṉpākattil kaṭalāl aḻikkappaṭṭa teṉmaturi yilum, āyiramtantikaḷ pūṭṭiya nāratappēriyāḻ mutaliya vīṇikaḷum nāratīyam akattiyam perunāri peruṅkuruku eṉṉum icinūlkaḷum iruntaṉavākac collappaṭukiṟatiṉāl, teṉṉintiyacaṅkītattiṉ pūrvīkatti aṟivataṟkāka annāṭṭippaṟṟi iṉṉum cilakāriyaṅkaḷi vicārippatu nallateṉṟutōṉṟukiṟatu. ivviṣayam caṟṟu virivākumākiyāl, eṉcuyamāka atikam collāmal, teṉṉintiyāviṉ teṉ pākattip paṟṟi maṟṟavarkaḷcollum apippirāyattil cilapākaṅkaḷi eṭuttuttokuttu iṅkē kāṭṭukiṟēṉ.
7. jalappiraḷayamum teṉṉintiyāvum.
mutalmutal intiyāviṉ teṉpākam oru kālattil jalap piraḷayattiṉāl aḻikkappaṭṭateṉṟum, atumikavum vicālamāṉa pūmi pākameṉṟum, atilēyē ātimaṉitarkaḷ kuṭiyēṟiṉārkaḷeṉṟum, piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇappaṭukiṟatu.
Vol. I of the Manual of the administration, Madras Presidency P. 33 Foot-note (2).
Hypothesis of the geneology and general migrations of the races of man:- “There are a number of circumstances (especially chronological facts), which suggest that the primeval home of man was a continent now sunk below the surface of the Indian ocean, which extended along the south of Asia, as it is at present (and probably in direct connection at some points with it); towards the east as far as Further India and the Sunda Islands, towards the west as far as Madagascar and the south-eastern shores of Africa. Many facts in animal and vegetable geography render the former existence of such a South Indian continent very probable. To this continent has been given the name of Lemuria, from the primitive mammals of that name which were characteristic of it. By assuming Lemuria to have been man’s primeval home, the explanation of the geographical distribution of the human species by migrion is much facilitated.”
“maṉitaṉiṉ āti iruppiṭam, intumakā camuttirattil pūrvam iruntu piṟaku muḻakippōṉa ōr kaṇṭam eṉṟum, atu āciyāvil ippōtu teṉpuṟattilirukkiṟa aḷavavvaḷavāyum, kiḻakkil intucīṉatīpa kalpam (Further India.)  caṉṭā (Sunda)) tīvukaḷ varikkum vicālittiruntatu eṉṟum, mēṟkē akkaṇṭam matakāskār  tīvuvarikkum āppirikkāviṉ teṉkiḻakkuk karivarikkum vicālittiruntatu eṉṟum niṉikka anēka ētukkaḷ uṇṭu. mirukaṅkaḷ ceṭi koṭi maraṅkaḷ mutaliyavaṟṟiṉ amippik kavaṉikkiyil, appērppaṭṭa teṉṉintiya kaṇṭam oṉṟu iruntatu eṉṟu ūkikka iṭamuṇṭu. intakkaṇṭattukku lemūriyā (Lemuria) eṉṟapēr koṭukkappaṭṭirukkiṟatu. antakkaṇṭattil vacitta Mammals (atāvatu kuṭṭikaḷukkup pālkoṭuttu vaḷarkkum mirukaṅkaḷ) mūlamāy antappēr uṇṭāṉaJ. inta lemūriyāk kaṇṭamē ātimaṉitar kuṭiyēṟiṉakaṇṭam eṉṟu vittukkoḷvōmāṉāl, maṉitar iṭamviṭṭu iṭam pērntu kuṭiyēṟiṉa carittiratti veku eḷitil tīrttuviṭalām.”
Vol. I of the Manual of the administration of the Madras Presidency P. III, 110.
“Investigations in relation to race show it to be by no means impossible that Southern India was once the passage-ground by which the ancient progenitors of Northern and Mediterranean races proceeded to the parts of the globe which they now inhabit. Human remins and traces have been found on the East coast of an age which is indeterminate but quite beyond the ordinary calculations of History.”
“jātikaḷ ōriṭamiruntu vēṟiṭam pērnta carittirattik kavaṉittup pārkkumpōtu, ippōtu vaṭakaṇṭattilum mattiyatarik kaṭaliyaṭuttum vacikkum jātiyāriṉ muṉṉōrkaḷ teṉṉintiyā vaḻiyāyttāṉ tāṅkaḷ ippōtu irukkum iṭattiṟkuc ceṉṟirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟu niṉikka iṭamuṇṭu. intiyāviṉ kīḻkariyil kaṇṭupiṭikkappaṭṭa maṉita elumpukaḷiṉ taṉmiyik kavaṉittāl, carittiram toṭaṅkāta kālattuḷḷa maṉitar intiyāviṉ kīḻkariyil orukālam vacittiruntārkaḷeṉṟu tīrmāṉikkalām.”

These results are specially to be regretted, as without doubt the population who have for many ages occupied this portion of the peninsula are a great people, influencing the world not much perhaps by moral and intellectual attributes, but to a great extent by superior physical qualities.”
“kalvimāṉkaḷ ituviṣayattil ceyyum muyaṟcikaḷiṉāl, ippōtutāṉ ātikālattu maṉitarip paṟṟiya anēka caṅkatikaḷ veḷiyā kiṉṟaṉa. ivikaḷ muṉṉālēyē veḷikkuvarāmaṟ pōyiṉavē eṉṟu varuttappaṭa vēṇṭiyirukkiṟatu. ēṉeṉṟāl, intiyāviṉ kīḻkariyil ātiyil vacitta jaṉaṅkaḷ oru periya jātiyāreṉpataṟkum oḻukka viṣayattilāvatu kalvi viṣayattilāvatu avarkaḷ atikamāyt tērcci aṭintirāviṭṭālum tēka amippil maṟṟum aṉēka jātiyariviṭa vicēṣittiruntārkaḷ eṉpataṟkum cantēkamilli.”
itil kaṇṭapaṭiyē, intiyāviṉ teṉpākattilē pūrvīkattil irunta jaṉaṅkaḷ mikunta parākkiramamuḷḷavarkaḷāy, tēka amippil jalap piraḷayattukkumuṉ 900, 1000 varuṣam jīvittirunta maṉitarkaḷiṉ tēka amippiṟku ottiruntatākak kāṇkiṟōm. avarkaḷ kālattilirunta vaḻakka oḻukkaṅkaḷ nākarīkam vitti mutaliyavikaḷip paṟṟi atikam colluvataṟkut takutiyāṉa ātāram iti eḻutiyavarukkuk kiṭikka villi yeṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu.
Manual of the administration of the Madras Presidency Vol. I Chapter. I Page (4).
“The Sanskrit authors of the Pooranas, writing in the north described Ceylon as much more extensive than it now is, and as stretching especially towards the west and south; thereby not representing, no doubt, the fact in Pooranic times, but embodying nevertheless traditions current among Indian Nations. * *   *   *   *    *
The Sanskrit astronomers place their chief Meridian in Lunka, but it was a line to the west of the present Ceylon. These remarks bear on the theory, that in the most ancient times there was a connection between Southern India and Madagascar. *   *   *   *    *
It also accords with the local tradition recorded by the Buddhists which state that Ceylon was gradually contracted by submergence. *   *   *   *    *
The date assigned to the Noachian deluge of Scripture in 2348 B.C. That of the severance in detil a great submergence on the west, and a tradition exists that the great and little Basseo rocks on the east are left by an eastern submergence.”
“vaṭatēcattil purāṇaṅkaḷi camaskirutattil eḻutiṉa nūlāciri yarkaḷ ilaṅki tēcam ippoḻutu iruppativiṭa atika periyatā yiruntatāyum, vicēṣamāka mēṟkēyum teṟkēyum atikamāy vicālittu iruntatāyum collukiṟārkaḷ. ippaṭi avarkaḷ colluvatāṉatu carittiraviṣayamāy niccayattik kāṇpikkāviṭṭālum akkālattil intiyatēcattavarukkuḷ pārampariyāy nicam eṉṟu oppuk koḷḷappaṭṭa oru caṅkatiyi niccayappaṭuttukiṟatāyirukkiṟatu.*  *  *  *  *  *
camaskiruta vāṉacāstirikaḷ taṅkaḷ yāmiyōttara rēkiyik kaṇippatu laṅkiyi mattiyāy vittuk koṇṭutāṉ. āṉāl anta rēki ippōtu irukkiṟa ilaṅkikkuc caṟṟu mēṟkiliruntaJ. atipūrva kālaṅkaḷil teṉṉintiyāvukkum matakāskar tīvukkum campantam iruntatu eṉṟa koḷkiyi itu niccayappaṭuttukiṟaJ. *  *  *  *  *  *
ilaṅkiyāṉatu koñcaṅkoñcamāy kaṭalāl cūḻappaṭṭuc ciṟiya tāyp pōṉatu eṉṟu aṅkuḷḷa putta matastarāl eḻutivikkappaṭṭa pāram pariyac caṅkatikkum itu ottirukkiṟaJ.*  *  *  *  *   *
nōvāviṉ kālattil uṇṭāṉa jalappiraḷayam cattiyavētattil ki.mu.  2348-m srī ttil uṇṭāṉatākac collappaṭṭirukkiṟatu. mēṟkē anēka tēcaṅkaḷ kaṭalukkuḷ muḻukippōṉatāyum kiḻakkil uṇṭāṉa jalap piraḷayattiṉāl kṣayaḷaḷuṭi caṭiumaḷa eṉṟa peritum ciṟitumāṉa kaṉmalikaḷ pūmi maṭṭattukku mēl terintatāyum pārampariyamāyc collik koḷḷappaṭukiṟatu.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷik kavaṉikkiyil, nōvāviṉ kālatti luṇṭāṉa jalappiraḷayampōl oru piraḷayamuṇṭāki intumakā camuttirattil mikavum vicālittirunta ilaṅkit tīviṉ perumpākatti aḻittuviṭṭateṉṟu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. kālattiṉ variyaṟiyi mēṟṟiciyārum kīḻtticiyārum taṉittaṉiyē kuṟittu vittirukkiṟārkaḷ. ākiyiṉāl niccayamākavē oru piraḷaya muṇṭāki yirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟu colvatu tappākamāṭṭāJ.
8. puttariṉ jalappiraḷaya kālakkaṇitam.
I x Manual of the Administration, of the Madras Presidency Vol. I, P. 110, III
“The most ancient facts regarding Southern Indian are remarkable. Geology and Natural history alike make it certin that at a time within the bounds of human knowledge, this country did not form part of Asia. A large southern continent, of which this country once formed part has been assured as necessary to account for the different circumstances. The Sanskrit Pooranic writers, the Ceylon Booddhists, and the local traditions of the West Coast, all indicate in different manners a agreat disturbance of the point of the Peninsula and Ceylon within recent times. The date given by English theologians to the Noachian deluge is 2348 B. C; and that given by the Ceylon Booddhists to the latest submergence in the region of Ceylon is 2387 B. C. The two dates cannot have been arrived at with mutual knowledge. Investigations in relation to race show it to be by no means impossible that Southern India was once the passage ground by which the ancient progenitors of Northern and Mediterranian races proceeded to the parts of the globe which they now inhabit.”
“teṉṉintiyāvippaṟṟiya pūrvakālattuc caṅkatikaḷ mikavum kavaṉikkappaṭattakkavi. teṉṉintiyāvāṉatu, jaṉaṅkaḷ nākarīkamaṭintu carittiraṅkaḷi eḻutiya kālattukkuḷ, āciyākaṇṭattiṉ ōr pākamā yirukkavilliyeṉṟu, pūkarppa cāstiramum iyaṟki cāstiramum niccayamāyk kūṟukiṉṟaṉa. intac cāstiraṅkaḷil ēṟpaṭum caṅkatikaḷukku mukāntaram kūṟuvataṟkāka, oru periya teṉkaṇṭam iruntatākavum intat teṉṉintiyā atil ōr pākamāyiruntatākavum vittukkoḷvatu veku avaciyamāyirukkiṟatu. coṟpa varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ intiya tīpakalpattiṉ teṉ muṉippākamum ilaṅkittīvum veku māṟutalkaḷaṭintaṉaveṉṟu, purāṇaṅkaḷi eḻutiya camaskiruta nūlāciriyarālum, ilaṅkiyiluḷḷa putta matattārālum, intiya mēṟkukkariyiluḷḷa pārampariyaṅkaḷiṉālum teriyavarukiṟatu. nōvāviṉ kālattiluṇṭāṉa jalappiraḷayam ki.mu. 2348-m ௵´ttil uṇṭāṉatāka āṅkilēya vētacāstirikaḷ kūṟukiṟārkaḷ. ilaṅkiyil kaṭiciyil uṇṭāṉa piraḷayam ki.mu. 2387-m ௵ ttil uṇṭāṉatāka ilaṅkiyiluḷḷa putta matakkurukkaḷ kūṟukiṟārkaḷ. iraṇṭukālaṅkaḷum, oruvarukkoruvar muṉṉēcollivittukkoṇṭu eḻutiṉaviyalla. ātiyil jātikaḷ uṟpattiyāṉatippaṟṟic collum cāstiraṅkaḷi ārāyntu pārkkiyil eṉṉa terikiṟateṉṟāl, vaṭakkukkaṇṭattilum mattiyatarikkaṭaliyaṭutta nāṭukaḷilum ippōtu vacikkum jātiyāriṉ muṉṉōrkaḷ tāṅkaḷ nāṭicaceṉṟa tēcaṅkaḷukkup pōkum vaḻiyil teṉṉintiyā vaḻiyāyttāṉ pōṉārkaḷ eṉṟu niṉikka anēka kāriyaṅkaḷ ēṟpaṭṭirukkiṟatu”.
mēlē kāṭṭiya vacaṉaṅkaḷik kavaṉikkiyil, putta camayak kurukkaḷāl jalappiraḷayam uṇṭāṉatākac collappaṭum ki. K.  2387-m ´ atāvatu iṟṟikkuc cumār 4,300 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ ilaṅkiyiṉ perumpākam kaṭalāl aḻikkappaṭṭirukkiṟateṉṟu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. mēlum teṉṉintiyāviṉ teṉpakkamāyuḷḷa pūmiyiṉ perumpākam kaṭalāl aḻikkappaṭṭa kālattil aṅkuḷḷōr teṉṉintiyāviṉ vaḻiyākavē maṟṟa iṭaṅkaḷukkuk kuṭiyēṟic ceṉṟārkaḷ eṉṟu kāṇappaṭukiṟatu.
9. cattiya virataṉatu kappalum malayamaliyum.
The Tamilian Antiquary by Pandit D. Savariroyan, M.R.A.S.
“Before this diluvial catastrophe, the western Ghats were known as the Northern mountins in relation to the southern Land which was submerged by the ocean. The Satapata Brahmana relates that the ark of Manu rested in the Northern mountins and the Puranas mention that he, the ‘Lord of the Dravida’, underwent austere penance in the Malaya. The Mahabharata and the Puranas give an account of seven other Rishis who accompanied Manu and settled in the new colony. This indicates the advent of other clans led by other Rishis who followed the footsteps of the “Lord of the Dravida”. Thus it appears that the Tamilian race that settled in the Pandu land belonged to these eight Rishis or Prajapatis, one of whom was the famous Rishi Pulastya of the extreme south, from whom were descended Agastya, the Tamil Muni and Ravana, the king of South.
The Satapata Brahmana, in which the story of Manu first occurs, does not mention the name of the Nothern Mountins. However, there is ample evidence in the Puranic accounts to identify the ‘Northern Mountins’ with the Western Ghats, and the particular spot on which the ark rested with Malaya.”
“jalappiraḷayattiṉāl nēriṭṭa inta vipattukkumuṉ kaṭalāl mūṭappaṭṭu aḻinta teṉ tēcattippaṟṟic collumiṭattil, mēṟkuk kaṇavāy malikaḷi vaṭakkēyuḷḷa malikaḷ eṉṟu colliyirukkiṟatu. ‘cata pata pirāmaṇam’ eṉṟa nūlil maṉuviṉ pēḻiyāṉatu vaṭakku malikaḷil taṅkiṉatāyum, purāṇaṅkaḷil ‘tirāviṭa atipati’ maliya maliyil koṭuntavam purintatāyum collappaṭṭirukkiṟatu. vēṟu ēḻu riṣikaḷ maṉuvuṭaṉ avar putitāyk kuṭiyēṟiṉa iṭaṅkaḷil avaruṭaṉkūṭac ceṉṟiruntatāka makāpāratamum purāṇa nūlkaḷum kūṟukiṉṟaṉa. itiṉāl eṉṉa veḷiyākiṟateṉṟāl ‘tirāviṭa atipati’ ceytatupōl maṟṟa riṣikaḷāl vaḻi naṭattappaṭṭu maṟṟa jātikaḷum putukkuṭiyēṟiṉārkaḷ eṉpatutāṉ. ivvitamāy, pāṇṭitēcattil kuṭiyēṟiṉa tamiḻarkaḷ mēṟcoṉṉa eṭṭu riṣikaḷ allatu pirajāpatikaḷic cērntavarkaḷāyirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟum avarkaḷil oruvar teṉ kōṭiyil vacitta pērpōṉa pulastiya riṣi eṉṟum teḷivākiṟaJ. intap pulastiya riṣi vaḻiyiltāṉ tamiḻ muṉiyākiya akastiya riṣiyum teṉtēcattaracaṉākiya irāvaṇaṉum tōṉṟiṉārkaḷ.
maṉuviṉ carittiram mutalmutal collappaṭṭirukkiṟa nūlākiya catāpata pirāmaṇattil vaṭakku malikaḷippaṟṟi oṉṟum collappaṭa villi. eṉṟālum, vaṭakkumalikaḷ mēṟkukkaṇavāy malikaḷtāṉ eṉpataṟkum pēḻi taṅkiṉa iṭam ‘malayam’ eṉpataṟkum purāṇaṅkaḷil pōtumāṉa ātāraṅkaḷuṇṭu.”
mēṟkaṇṭa cilavarikaḷil tirāviṭa tēcattaracaṉākiya cattiyavirataṉ jalappiraḷayattukkut tappumpaṭiyāka ēṟicceṉṟa kappal ataṟku vaṭatici yiluḷḷa maliyamaliyil riṣikaḷuṭaṉ vantu taṅkiṉatākak kāṇkiṟatu. malayam eṉpatu potiki malikkuppeyar; kirutamālā natiyeṉpatu ikkālattil illāta natiyeṉṟu carittirakkārar collukiṟārkaḷ. maturik karukiluḷḷa vikinatik kariyil tirāviṭatēcattācaṉākiya cattiyavirataṉ tavacu ceytu koṇṭiruntāṉ eṉpatu poruntāJ. mēlum, malayamali eṉpatu uttara maturikkut teṟkiluḷḷa maliyākumē yoḻiya vaṭakkiluḷḷa maliyākamāṭṭāJ. āṉāl mēṟṟicimalikaḷil uṟpattiyāṉa kumariyāṟu teṉpāṇṭi nāṭṭiṟku vaṭavelliyāy niṉṟu aḻintu pōṉatu pōla, kirutamālānatiyum potiyamaliyiluṟpattiyākit teṉ maturikkarukil ōṭikkoṇṭiruntu cattiyavirataṉ tavañceyvataṟ kēṟṟa iṭamāyiruntu piṉpu aḻintu pōyirukkavēṇṭum. appaṭiyāṉāl, teṉṉintiyāviṟku vekutūram teṟkēyirunta teṉmaturi kaṭalāl aḻikkappaṭṭa kālattil, aṅkirunta cattiyavirataṉeṉṉum tirāviṭa tēcattaracaṉ kappalēṟit teṉmaturikku vaṭakkiluḷḷa malayamalikku vantu cērntāṉeṉṟu colvatē poruntum.
10. kaṉṉiyā kumariyiṉ purātaṉa kōyilkaḷ.
Essays by S.V. Thomas, M.A. Page 84.
“The earliest mention of Cape Comorin by European  writers is contemporaneous with the time of Alexander the Great. We have reason to believe that so early as the time of the Periplus the Cape was already famous as a sacred shrine of the Hindus. The temple was dedicated to Siva’s wife, called Kumari, or young woman as emblematic of eternal youth and beauty. It is impossible to decide the precise site of the ancient temple seen by the Greek silors. There are at present three temples at Cape Comorin. One is in complete ruins, and the sea is every day swallowing it up. This is probably the ancient of the three. Another is situated on an elevated rock the foot of which is constantly washed by the waves. The temple is antique in appearence and structure, but is dedicated evidently to Siva’s son, not wife, as it contins a rude stone image of Ganesa. The third is the temple which pilgrims now go to visit. This has a comparatively modernlook about it. *   *   *   *   *   *
It is very probable that the most ancient temple has been buried under the sea, and that when the encroachments of the sea were arrested by the solid rocks that now stand out to check its progress, another temple was built and dedicated to the same goddess. This view is supported by the report that is current among the fishermen living in the neighbourhood. They say that in 1883, when the green sun perplexed the scientific world, the sea receded all of a sudden some furlongs that  there were then scen the ruins of old buildings with brazen gates and other accompaniments, that some of the fishermen ventured out to drage them ashore, but that before they could succeed the sea came back to assert her ancient dominion, and the enterprise was given over. If this be true, we have strong reasons for supposing that the temple which astonised the Greeks is imbedded in sea, and that the present temple afterwards rose to the honour of the tutelary goddess of the Cape.”
“airōppiya carittirakkārar kaṉṉiyākumariyippaṟṟi mutal mutal colluṅkālam makā alekcāntaruṭiya kālattukkuc cariyāy irukkiṟaJ. peripḷas eṉṉum nūl eḻutappaṭṭa kālattilēyē anta muṉiyāṉatu intukkaḷukku oru puṇṇiya stalamāka iruntatu eṉṟu niṉikka ētuvuṇṭu. atiluḷḷa kōyil civaṉuṭiya maṉiviyākiya pārvatipērāl piratiṣṭi paṇṇappaṭṭiruntaJ. (“kumari” eṉṟāl aḻakiya iḷampeṇ, nittiya iḷamiyiyum aḻakiyum uṭiya pārvati.) kirēkka mālumikaḷāl pārkkappaṭṭa kōyil enta stalattil kaṭṭappaṭṭirunta teṉṟu ippōtu collamuṭiyāJ. ippōtu kaṉṉiyākumari muṉiyil mūṉṟu kōyilkaḷ irukkiṉṟaṉa. avikaḷil ellāvaṟṟilum paḻami yāṉatu eṉṟu lēcil oppukkoḷḷakkūṭiyatu aḻintupōyk kaṭalil muḻukikkoṇṭēvarukiṟatu. maṟṟoṉṟu uyaramāṉa kaṉmaliyiṉmēl kaṭṭappaṭṭirukkiṟatu; atiṉ aṭivārattil kaṭal alikaḷ eppōtum aṭittukkoṇṭēyirukkiṉṟaṉa. intak kōyil veḷittōṟṟattilum kaṭṭaṭa amippilum atikap paḻamiyāṉatākat tōṟṟukiṟatu: atu pārvati pērāl piratiṣṭipaṇṇappaṭāmal, civaṉuṭiya kumāraṉpērāl piratiṣṭi yāyirukkiṟatu. ēṉeṉṟāl, kallāl karaṭumuraṭāyc ceyta kaṇēcaruṭiya vikkirakam atil irukkiṟaJ. mūṉṟāvatu kōyiltāṉ ippōtu cuvāmi taricaṉattukku varupavarkaḷ vaḻaṅkum kōyil. atippārttāl taṟkālam kaṭṭappaṭṭatupōl terikiṟatu.
āṉāl nām nitāṉamāyc collakkūṭiyatu eṉṉaveṉṟāl, avikaḷil ellāvaṟṟilum atika pūrvamāṉa kōyil kaṭalukkuḷ muḻukippōyirukkavēṇṭum eṉṟum, nāḷāvaṭṭattil kaṭal antak kaṉmaliyit tākkamuṭiyāteṉṟu kaṇṭapōtu anta maliyiṉmēl pārvatikku vēṟoru kōyil  kaṭṭappaṭṭatu eṉṟum niṉikkalām. antap pakkattil vacikkum cemapaṭavarukkuḷḷum itē tāṟpariyamtāṉ taṟkālamvari iruppatākatterikiṟatu. avarkaḷ colluvatu eṉṉa veṉṟāl, 1883-m varuṣattil cūriyaṉ pacciniṟamāki, cāstirikaḷellām atiṉimittam mayaṅkiṉapōtu, avviṭattil kaṭalāṉatu cila parlāṅkukaḷ tūram uḷḷukkuc ceṉṟuviṭṭatām. appōtu tiṭīreṉṟu pala iṭinta kaṭṭaṭaṅkaḷ kaṭalil tōṉṟiṉatākavum avikaḷellām veṇkalak katavukaḷuḷḷavikaḷāyiruntamiyāl cila mīṉparavar kaṇṭu avikaḷik karikku iḻuttuvara muyaṉṟatākavum, āṉāl avarkaḷ tirumpivarumuṉ kaṭal avikaḷi maṟupaṭiyum mūṭippōṭṭatākavum, piṟaku avarkaḷ avikaḷik karikkuk koṇṭuvara muṭiyāmal viṭṭuviṭṭatākavum collikkoḷḷukiṟārkaḷ. itu niccayamāy irukkumāṉāl, kirēkkarkaḷ kaṇṭu piramippaṭinta kōyil kaṭalil muḻukippōyirukka vēṇṭum eṉṟum ippōtu pārvatikkuk kaṭṭappaṭṭirukkum kōyil piṉṉāl uṇṭāṉateṉṟum niṉikka iṭamuṇṭu.” 
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷil, teṉṉintiyāviṉ teṟkuk kaṭiciyil irukkum kaṉṉiyākumariyil nittiyakumariyāyviḷaṅkum pārvati tēviyāriṉ kōyil kaṭalukkuḷ amiḻntiruppatākavum ataṟkup piṟkālattil kaṭṭappaṭṭa kōyilkaḷum kaṭalāl mōtappaṭṭu niṟkiṟatākavum kaṇēcar ālayaṅkaḷum aṅkēyirukkiṟatākavum kāṇkiṟōm. kaṭalāl mūṭappaṭṭa ikkaṭṭaṭaṅkaḷ 1883-m varuṣattil oru camayam kaṭalāl cila tūram viṭappaṭṭakālattil, akkaṭṭaṅkaḷilirunta veṇkalak katavukaḷi mīṉ piṭippōr kaṇṭu karicērkkappirayattaṉap paṭukiyil, kaṭal maṟupaṭiyum poṅki varuvatippārttu appirayattaṉatti viṭṭuviṭṭārkaḷeṉṟu collappaṭukiṟatu. akkālattiluḷḷōr taṅkaḷ aracamāḷikiyilum kōṭṭi vācalilum kōyil vācalkaḷilum poṉṉālum veṇkalattālum katavunilikaḷ ceytu upayōkittu vantārkaḷeṉṟu aṟikiṟōm. mēlum kaṉṉiyākumariyiluḷḷa pūrva ālayaṅkaḷ kaṭalāl aḻikkappaṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉpatum terikiṟatu.
11. lemūriyā irunta iṭam.
Castes and Tribes of Southern India Vol. I, Intro. P. 20, 21 by E. Thurston.
“In the chapter devoted to ‘Migration and distribution of organisms, ‘Haeckel, in referring to the continual changing of the distribution of land and water on the surface of the earth, says; “The Indian ocean formed a continent, which extended fromthe Sunda Islands along the Southern Coast of Asia to the east coast of Africa. This large continent of former times Sclater has called Lemuria, from the monkey-like animals which inhabited it, and it is at the same time of great importance from being the probable cradle of the human race. The important proof which Wallace has furnished by the help of chronological facts, that the presentMalayan Archipelago consists in reality of two completely different divisions, is particularly interesting. The western division, the Indo-Malayan Archipelago, comprising the large islands of Borneo, Java, and Sumatra, was formerly connected by Malacca with the Asiatic continent, and probably also with the Lemurian continent just mentioned. The eastern division, on the other hand, the Austro-Malayan Archipelago, comprising Celees, the Moluccas, New Guinea, Solomon’s Islands, etc., was formerly directly connected with Australia.”
“intu makācamuttirattil oru kaṇṭam iruntatu; akkaṇṭam cantāttīvukaḷiliruntu āciyākkaṇṭattiṉ teṉkari neṭukacceṉṟu āppirikkākkaṇṭattiṉ kīḻkarivarikkum eṭṭiyiruntaJ. muṟkālaṅkaḷi lirunta intapperiya kaṇṭattukku, atilvacitta kuraṅku pōṉṟa pirāṇikaḷi liruntu skleyṟṟar eṉpavar lemūriyā eṉṟu pērkoṭuttirukkiṟār. maṉitajātiyiṉ āti piṟappiṭamāyiruntirukkalām eṉṟu uttēcikkap paṭuvatiṉāl ikkaṇṭam atika mukkiyamāṉaJ. taṟkālattilirukkum malāyāttīpakaṇam uṇmiyil oṉṟukkoṉṟu muṟṟum vittiyācamāṉa iraṇṭu pirivukaḷaṭaṅkiyatu eṉpatākak kālavariciyāy ēṟpaṭṭa caṅkatikaḷikkoṇṭu uvāles eṉpavar tiruṣṭāntappaṭuttiyatu vicēṣitta iṉimiyuḷḷaJ. mēṟpirivākiya intumalāyāt tīpakaṇam, pōrṇiyō jāvā cumāttirā eṉṉum periya tīvukaḷaṭaṅkiyaJ. atu muṟkālattil āciyākkaṇṭattōṭum anēkamāy iṅkē colla yirukkappaṭṭa lemūriyākkaṇṭattōṭum malākkāviṉāl iṇikkap paṭṭiruntaJ. itaṟkumāṟākak kīḻppirivākiya āstirōmalāyāt tīpakaṇam, celipis molukkās niyukiṉiyā cālomōṉ tīvukaḷ mutalāṉavikaḷ aṭaṅkiyatāyp paḻiyakālattil āstirēliyāvōṭu iṇikkap paṭṭiruntatu eṉṟu hekkēl eṉpavar kūṟukiṟār.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷik kavaṉikkiyil, intumakācamuttirattil teṉṉintiyāvukkut teṉpakkattilirunta intiyakaṇṭamāṉatu āti maṉuṣarkaḷ uṟpattiyāvataṟku iṭamāyiruntateṉṟum, ellājātikaḷum cukamāyt taṅkiyirunta toṭṭilāyiruntateṉṟum, atu aḻintapiṉ atiliruntu taṅkaḷukku etirppaṭṭa karikaḷukkuc ceṉṟu aṅku viruttiyāṉārkaḷeṉṟum terikiṟatu. intu makācamuttirattic cuṟṟiyuḷḷa tīvukaḷilum tēcaṅkaḷilumirukkum jaṉaṅkaḷiyum paḻakka vaḻakkaṅkaḷiyum ceṭikoṭi maraṅkaḷiyum iyaṟkiyiṉ amipputterinta cāstirikaḷ lemūriyā eṉṟu aḻikkappaṭum intiyakaṇṭattiliruntē jaṉaṅkaḷ paraviyirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟu collukiṟārkaḷ. mēlum, jalappiraḷayamāṉatu uṇṭāṉakālattil ellārum aḻintupōka, teyva aṉukkirakattāl eṅkaḷ tēcattil iṉṉapaktaṉ kappal mūlamāy tappippiḻittā reṉṟum, avaruṭiya cantatitāṉ nāṅkaḷeṉṟum, ovvoru tēcattārum collik koḷḷukiṟārkaḷeṉpati itaṉ piṉvarum varikaḷil kāṇalām:
12. jalappiraḷayattik kuṟitta pala apippirāyaṅkaḷ.
The New Popular Encyclopedia Vol. IV, Page 325.
“Many other nations mention, in the mythological part of their history, inundations which in their essential particulars, agree with the scriptural account of Noah’s preservation. Hence many persons have inferred the universality of this innundation. To this, it has been replied that each nation localizes the chief events and actors as connected with itself, necessitating an Ararat, an ark and a Noah in each instance. Fohi in the Chinese mythology, Sottivrata or Satyavrata in the Indian, Xisuthrus in the Chaldean, Ogyges and Deucalion in the Greek, have each been recognized by many as the Noah of the sacred scriptures under a different name. Even the American Indians have a tradition of a similar deluge, and a renewal of the human race from the family of one individual. All these individuals are sid by their respective nations to have been saved, and to have become a second father of mankind.”
“vēṟu anēka jātiyārum taṅkaḷ purāṇak katikaḷil piraḷayaṅkaḷik kuṟittuc collukiṟārkaḷ. avvaralāṟukaḷellām, mukkiyamāṉa viṣayaṅkaḷil, nōvā kāppāṟṟappaṭṭatākac cattiyavētattiṟ kūṟiyuḷḷa varalāṟṟukku ottirukkiṟaJ. ākiyāl, anēkar intappiraḷayam ulakameṅkum uṇṭāyirukkalāmeṉṟu yūkikkiṟārkaḷ. ataṟku ovvoru tēcattārum piraḷayattiṉ mukkiya caṅkatikaḷit taṅkaḷ tēcattilēyē naṭantatākavum atil campantappaṭṭavarkaḷ taṅkaḷ tēcattārkaḷēyeṉpa tākavum kūṟi, ārarātpōṉṟa oru maliyiyum, pēḻiyippōṉṟa oru kappaliyum, nōvāvippōṉṟa oru muṉiyiyum ēṟpaṭuttukiṟārkaḷē yeṉṟu āṣēpaṉi kūṟappaṭukiṟaJ. cīṉaruṭiya purāṇak katikaḷil collappaṭum ‘pōhi’yum intupurāṇaṅkaḷil collappaṭum ‘cattiya virataṉu’m, kaltēyaruṭiya katikaḷiṟ collappaṭum ‘cicutturacu’m, kirēkkaruṭiya kaṭṭukkatikaḷiṟ collappaṭum ‘ojījecu’m ‘ṭiyukeliyaṉu’m, cattiya vētattiṟ kūṟappaṭṭuḷḷa nōvātāṉeṉṟum nōvāvukkē, vevvēṟu tēcattil vevvēṟu peyar collappaṭukiṟa teṉṟum palar apippirāyappaṭukiṟārkaḷ. amerikka intiyarkaḷukkuḷ ḷēyum, ippaṭip paṭṭa oru piraḷayamuṇṭāṉatākavum piṉpu orēyoru maṉitaṉuṭiya kuṭumpattiliruntu tirumpavum maṉitar viruttiyāṉatākavum pārampariyamāka oru kati collappaṭṭu varukiṟatu. ippaṭiyē avaravarkaḷ tēcattilum oru maṉitaṉ tappip pātukākkappaṭṭāṉeṉṟum, avaṉ maṉita jātikaḷukku iraṇṭāvatu tantiyāṉāṉ atāvatu, iraṇṭāntaram maṉitar avaṉiliruntu uṟpattiyākip perukiṉārkaḷeṉṟum collappaṭṭu varukiṟatu.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷil cīṉā intiyā kaltēyā ciṉṉāciyā amerikkā mutaliya tēcaṅkaḷ jalappiraḷayattiṉāl aḻintupōṉatākavum, appaṭi aḻinta kālattil nōvāviṉ kuṭumpattip pōṉṟa oru uttamamāṉa kuṭumpam kāppāṟṟappaṭṭatākavum collikkoḷḷappaṭukiṟatu. ākavē, jalappiraḷayam oru kālattil uṇṭāyirukka vēṇṭumeṉpatu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. oruvēḷi antantattēcaṅkaḷil jalappiraḷaya muṇṭāyirukkumā eṉṟu nām cantēkikkalām. ākilum uṟṟu nōkkuvōmāṉāl, avikaḷ yāvum uṇmiyē eṉṟu kāṇpōm. lemūriyā eṉṉum peruṅkaṇṭam taṇṇīrukkuḷ amiḻntu pōkiyil, ataṉ cuṟṟukkariyiluḷḷōr taṅkaḷukku etirppaṭṭa maṟṟa iṭaṅkaḷukku ēṉ pōyirukkak kūṭātu? āpattāṉa kālaṅkaḷil aṉēkar aḻintu pōyirukka, cilarmāttiram tappakkūṭātā? tappiyavarkaḷ taṅkaḷukkup pakkattiluḷḷa iṭaṅkaḷil tayārittu viruttiyākak kūṭātā? viruttiyāṉa piṉṉaṭiyār eṅkaḷ muṟpitāvākiya iṉṉār kālattil nāṅkaḷ iṅku vantōmeṉṟu meṉmiyāyc collikkoḷvatu iyalputāṉē. lemūriyākkaṇṭam aḻinta kālattil ciṉṉa āciyāvil nōvāvum intiyāvil cattiyavirataṉum cīṉāvil pōkiyum amerikkāvil oru paktaṉum āppirikkāvil oru puṇṇiyavāṉum tappippiḻittārkaḷeṉṟu colvatu acampāvitamallavē. ittēcaṅkaḷiṉ ōraṅkaḷ lemūriyāviṟku camīpittiruntateṉṟu nām aṟiyavēṇṭum. ivarkaḷippōla iṉṉum ettaṉiyō pēr tappiyirukkalām.
13. aḻintupōṉa uvarippaṭṭiṉam.
Essays by S.V. Thomas, M.A. Page 85.
“About six miles from Cape Comorin stands a small fishing village called Uvari, or Ovari. This village, I am inclined to think, occupies the site of the Ophir of the Bible. In the first place, although Ovari is at present only a small fishing village, yet unlike other villages of a similar kind, it posseses a solid stone temple, which is as old as any other temple in India, and is to-day visited by pilgrims from the remotest corners of the country, thus showing that it once had a glory, and a name that have long ago departed. Agin, the word Uvari or Ovari means in Tamil nothing more or less than a scaport, being connected with Uvar, salt or salt sea. We may, therefore, reasonably conclude that Ovari was a great emporium of trade and that it was by pre-eminence called the seaport, which the merchants of Tyre might have mistaken for its proper name, a thing which often happens in South India. To more than 95 percent of the Tamil people Madras is known only by the name of ‘the city.’ Further with regard to the general appearance of Ovari the sand banks that are found there have the character of earth piled by artificial means, that is, they looks as if they have been dug out of the Earth and thrown up as mounds. Add to this circumstance the fact that when heavy rins have washed down the earth from thesde hcaps of sand, people have now and then picked up pieces of gold. The inhabitants mention this fact not becuse they know anything about Ophir and its gold, so as to be ambitious of connecting their village with that name, but as an instance to show that once their village was inhabited by very rich people, who left their gold in their place when they themselves went the way of all mankind.”
“kaṉṉiyākumari muṉikkuccaṟṟēṟakkuṟiya āṟumil tūrattil uvari allatu ovari eṉṟa cempaṭavar vacikkum oru kirāmam irukkiṟaJ. antakkirāmam cattiya vētattiṟ kūṟiyuḷḷa oppīr nakarirunta iṭattilirukkiṟateṉṟu nāṉ eṇṇukiṟēṉ. ataṟkup pala mukāntaraṅkaḷ uḷa. mutalāvatu, atu mukkiyamaṟṟa cempaṭavaruṭiya kirāmamāṉapōtilum, atippōlotta maṟṟa kirāmaṅkaḷukku vittiyācamāka, oru stiramāṉa kallālamintakōyil ataṟku uṇṭu. antakkōyil intiyāviluḷḷa maṟṟakkōyilkaḷippōlavē atikappaḻamiyāṉatāyum tēcattiṉ nāṉā ticikaḷilumiruntu jaṉaṅkaḷ aṅkē cuvāmi taricaṉattukku varumpaṭiyāṉa avvaḷavu makimiyuḷḷatāyum irukkiṟaJ. itaṉālē atu orukālattil atikappēr pirastāpa makimiyuṭiyatāyiruntateṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu. iraṇṭāvatu, uvari eṉṟāl tamiḻil kaṭaṟṟuṟimukam eṉṟu arttam. atāvatu uppu eṉṟu poruḷpaṭum uvar eṉṟamoḻiyuṭaṉ campantappaṭṭaJ. intakkuṟippukaḷiṉiṉṟu uvariyāṉatu varttakattukkup pērpōṉa iṭamā yiruntirukkavēṇṭum eṉṟum, mukkiya viyāpāra stalamāṉatiṉāl ati uvari allatu vicēṣitta tuṟimukam eṉṟaḻittārkaḷ eṉṟum, tīrunakariṉ varttakar uvariyē ataṟkup pēr eṉṟu tappāy niṉittārkaḷ eṉṟum cantēkamaṟac collalām. ippērppaṭṭa tappāṉa eṇṇaṅkaḷ teṉṉintiyāvil vaḻakkamāy uṇṭākiṟatutāṉ. (utāraṇamāka, nūṟṟil 95 pēr matarācip ‘paṭṭaṇam’ eṉṟutāṉē aḻikkiṟārkaḷ.) mūṉṟāvatu, anta uvari eṉṉum kirāmattiṉ potuvāṉa veḷittōṟṟattik kavaṉittāl, aṅkuḷḷa maṇal mēṭukaḷ tariyil iruntu veṭṭappaṭṭuk kuvikkappaṭṭa mēṭukaḷpōl kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. nāṉkāvatu, perumaḻi peytu inta maṇalmēṭukaḷ kariyumpōtu jaṉaṅkaḷ ammēṭukaḷiliruntu poṉ nāṇayaṅkaḷi eṭuttirukkiṟārkaḷ. akkirāmavācikaḷ intaccaṅkatiyic collumpōtu taṅkaḷ kirāmam pūrvakālattil makimipeṟṟirunta “oppīr” eṉṉum nakaramtāṉ eṉṟa perumiyāṉa kāriyatti stāpikka varāmal, taṅkaḷ kirāmam muṟkālattil taṉavāṉkaḷ vacitta iṭameṉṟum avarkaḷ marikkuṅkāl taṅkaḷ poṉṉiyum tiraviyattiyum avviṭattil viṭṭu marittārkaḷ eṉṟum arttappaṭumpaṭiyākavē appaṭiccollukiṟārkaḷ.”
kaṉṉiyākumarikkuc cumār āṟumil tūrattiluḷḷa uvariyeṉṉum kaṭaṟṟuṟimukattil pūrva kālattil kaṭṭappaṭṭa paḻamiyāṉa kōyilkaḷiruntatākavum, avi ippōtu piratāṉamaṟṟuppōṉatākavum, aṅkuḷḷa mēṭāṉa iṭaṅkaḷil poṉnāṇayaṅkaḷ maḻipeykiṟa kālaṅkaḷil kaṇṭeṭukkappaṭukiṟatākavum tōṉṟukiṟatu. avviṭattil vaḻaṅkivanta poṉ nāṇayaṅkaḷ mika māṟṟuyarntāyiruntatiṉ nimittam cālomōṉ eṉṉum yūtēyātēcattu rājaṉ tāṉ mikunta makimi yuṭiyatāyk kaṭṭiya tēvālayattiṉ katavukaḷip pacumpoṉ takaṭṭiṉāl mūṭiṉāṉ eṉpatikkoṇṭum, oppīriṉ taṅkatti īrāmiṉ kappalkaḷ varuṣantōṟum kāṇikkiyākakkoṇṭu vantu cālomōṉukkuk koṭuttaṉaveṉṟum 1. rājākkaḷ 10, 11, 22-l mūṉṟu varuṣattukku orutaram tarṣīsiṉ kappalkaḷ poṉṉiyum veḷḷiyiyum yāṉit tantaṅkaḷiyum kuraṅkukaḷiyum mayilkaḷiyum koṇṭuvarum eṉṟum colliyiruppatik koṇṭum, cumār 3,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉālēyē uvariyeṉṉum kirāmam kaṭaṟṟuṟip paṭṭaṇamāyiruntateṉṟum, aṅkuḷḷa poṉ mikavum nērttiyāṉateṉṟum, ati mēṟṟici viyāpārikaḷ vāṅkikkoṇṭu pōṉārkaḷeṉṟum teḷivākat terikiṟatu. iṟṟikkuc cumār 3,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukkumuṉ pacumpoṉṉiyum rattiṉaṅkaḷiyum vācaṉittiraviyaṅkaḷiyum cantaṉakkaṭṭikaḷiyum yāṉit tantaṅ kaḷiyum taṅkaḷ viyāpārap poruḷāka maṟṟattēcaṅkaḷukku ēṟṟumati ceyta avviṭam ippōtu aḻintu aṭiyāḷam teriyāmal niṟkiṟaJ. pūrvakālattil aṅkuḷḷōr ceyta viyāpārattik kavaṉikkum pōtu avarkaḷ mikunta nākarikamuṭiyavarkaḷāyum celvapperukkuṭiyavarkaḷāyu miruntārkaḷeṉṟu collāmalē viḷaṅkukiṟaJ.
itaṉmuṉ carittira ārāyccikkārar colliya cilavarikaḷik kavaṉitta nām teṉṉintiyāviṟkut teṟkē intumakācamuttirattil vicālittiruntu jalappiraḷayattiṉāl aḻintu pōṉa pūpākam lemūriyā veṉṟum, intumakācamuttirattil atu irunta iṭam teriyāmal aḻintupōka, atiluḷḷōr taṅkaḷukkuc camīpattiluḷḷa teṉṉintiyā  vilum intu cīṉatīpa kaṟpattilum cantā cumāttirā jāvā mutaliya tīvukaḷilum matakāskar tīvilum āppirikka amerikkāviṉ teṉ pākaṅkaḷilum pōyk kuṭiyēṟiṉārkaḷeṉṟum terikiṟatu. ippaṭi aḻintupōṉa lemūriyāk kaṇṭattiluḷḷōr ulakattiṉ carittiram eḻuta ārampikkum muṉṉālēyē mikunta nākarīkamum kalviyumuṭiyavarkaḷā yiruntārka ḷeṉṟu pala kalvimāṉkaḷ colliyirukkiṟārkaḷ. atōṭu aḻintupōṉa akkaṇṭattiliruntavarkaḷ tamiḻp pāṣiyiyē pēci vantārkaḷeṉṟum lemūriyā aḻintapiṉpē maṟṟappāṣikaḷ tamiḻil kalakka ārampittateṉṟum nām aṟivōm. mēlum, ulaka carittiratti eḻuta ārampitta anēkar, taṅkaḷ taṅkaḷ tēcattil nākarīkam kalvi kit toḻil carittiram uṇṭāṉa kālattiyē piratāṉamāka vittukkoṇṭu, ataṟkuc caṟṟu muṉpiṉṉākavē maṟṟat tēcattuc carittiramirukkumeṉṟu uttēcaṅ kaṭṭiṉārkaḷ. itaṉāl oru tēcattiṉuṭiya pūrvīkam māṟi, uṇmi kaṇṭu aṟivataṟku ētuvillāmaṟ pōkiṟaJ. maṟṟuñ cilar tamakku muṉ eḻutiyavarkaḷuṭiya apippirāyattiyē cātittuc collukiṟārkaḷ. ulakattiṉ iyaṟki amippik kavaṉittuc cariyāṉa kālam collukiṟavarkaḷ pittiyakkārarāka niṉikkappaṭu kiṟārkaḷ. vēṟu cilar, tāṅkaḷ collum kāriyaṅkaḷ ellārālum nampappaṭavēṇṭumeṉpati uttēcittut tāṅkaḷ eḻutivittavikaḷi anātiyāyuḷḷateṉṟum, vekupūr vantoṭṭu irukkiṟateṉṟum, itu riṣikaḷāl eḻutappaṭṭa teṉṟum, iti nampātavarkaḷ iṉṉiṉṉa cāpattukkuḷḷāvārkaḷeṉṟum collukiṟārkaḷ. vēṟu cilar, pūrvamāyuḷḷa kirantaṅkaḷil nūtaṉamāyt taṅkaḷ cuyapirayōjaṉatti virumpicceyta cila cūttiraṅkaḷi aṅkaṅkē nuḻittuviṭukiṟārkaḷ. itaṉāl intiyāviṉ pūrva carittiraṅkaḷ kāla variyaṟi colla ētuvillāmal cantēkikkum niliyil vantuviṭṭatu. nūṟṟik keṭuttatu kuṟuṇi eṉpatupōla taṟkālattavar ceyyum coṟpap puraṭṭukaḷ pūrvamāyuḷḷa nallatiyum keṭuttuviṭukiṟaJ. appaṭi yiruntālum teṉṉintiyāvil pūrvamāyp pēcappaṭṭuvanta tamiḻp pāṣiyiṉ ilakkaṇamākiya tolkāppiyam nāḷatu variyum evvitak kalappumaṟṟut taṉ pūrvatti viḷakkik kāṭṭikkoṇṭu niṟpati nām kāṇkiṟōm. annūl mutal ūḻiyiṉ iṟutiyilirunta atāvatu iṟṟikkuc cumār 8,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉirunta mutaṟcaṅkattiṉ kaṭiciyil kaṭalāl viḻuṅkappaṭṭa teṉmaturiyil āṇṭukoṇṭirunta nilantarutiruviṟ pāṇṭiyaṉ kālattil araṅkēṟṟappaṭṭateṉṟum annūlē iṭiccaṅkattārukku ātāra nūlāyiruntateṉṟum itaṉ piṉ kāṇpōm. tolkāppiyar tamatu kuruvākiya akattiyarāl iyal ici nāṭakameṉṉum muttamiḻippaṟṟiyum eḻutiya 80,000 cūttiraṅkaḷikkuṟukki 8,000 cūttiramākac ceytār eṉṟu collappaṭukiṟatu.
Manual of the Administration of the Madras Presidency, Vol. III, Page 907.
“Tol (bjhš-tol. Tam.) old - Tolguppyam (bjhšfh¥ãa« - tolgappiyam). From (above and kavya, san, Poem). Tamil grammar (lacshanam) by Tolguppyan of Madura, pupil of Agastyan whose grammar, consisting of 80,000 rules, he abridged, reducing the number to 8,000. The Tolguppyam complete should consist of three parts; on letters, words and prosody, or rather versification as an art. Of these the last part cannot be found complete. Tolguppyam has had three commentaries written upon it by Natchinarkiny, Yilampooran, and Shenuvareiyan (Nunnool).”
“tolkāppiyam eṉpatu akastiyaruṭiya māṇākkaṉāṉa maturit tolkāppiyarāl eḻutappaṭṭa tamiḻ ilakkaṇam. 80,000 cūttiraṅkaḷaṭaṅkiya pērakattiyam eṉṉum akastiyaruṭiya ilakkaṇattic curukki ivar 8,000 cūttiraṅkaḷāl tamatu ilakkaṇa nūli eḻutiṉār. tolkāppiyam muḻu nūlum eḻuttu col yāppu eṉṉum mūṉṟu pirivukaḷiyuṭiyatā yirukkavēṇṭum. ivikaḷil kaṭicippirivu pūraṇamāyk kiṭikkavilli. nacciṉārkkiṉiyar iḷampūraṇar cēṉāvariyar eṉṟa mūvar tolkāppiyattukku uri yeḻutiyirukkiṟārkaḷ.”
ivar eḻutiya cūttiraṅkaḷ tamiḻ makkaḷāl pacumpoṉṉilum ciṟantavikaḷāka nāḷatuvariyum pōṟṟappaṭṭuvarukiṉṟaṉa. iruntālum, avar karuttukkaḷ yāvum terintukoṇṭu aṉupōkattukkuk koṇṭuvantavarkaḷ mikac cilar eṉṟē collavēṇṭum. mēlum, tolkāppiyam 8,000 cūttiraṅkaḷuṭiyateṉṟu maklīṉ paṇṭitar collum vacaṉaṅkaḷik kavaṉikkiyil, taṟkālattil vaḻaṅkum 1612 cūttiraṅkaḷ mikac coṟpameṉṟē collavēṇṭum; mītiyāṉa 6,388 cūttiraṅkaḷ antiṉ vāyppaṭṭu aḻintupōyiṉaveṉṟu niṉikka iṭamirukkiṟatu. uviṉslō paṇṭitar collukiṟa 12,000 cūttiraṅkaḷ ciṟṟakattiyamāyirukkalāmeṉṟu eṇṇa iṭamirukkiṟatu. ippaṭi aḻintupōṉa tolkāppiyam, pūrva tamiḻariṉ vaḻakka oḻukkaṅkaḷ rājamuṟimikaḷ nilattiṉ pākupāṭukaḷ vīṇiyiṉ vakikaḷ mutaliyavikaḷippaṟṟi mikat teḷivākac collukiṟaJ. taṟkālattil kāṇappaṭum pāṣiyiṉ tērcci aṉittiṟkummēlāṉa ilakkaṇamuṭiyatāy viḷaṅkukiṟaJ. tamiḻiṉ perumi iṉṉateṉṟu aṟintu koḷvataṟku atu pōtumāṉateṉṟē niṉikkavēṇṭum.
14. mikappūrva kālattikkāṭṭum cila mukkiya kuṟippukaḷ.
tolkāppiyam uṇṭāvataṟku muṉ 4,400 varuṣaṅkaḷākavirunta mutal caṅkattiyum atil talimi vakitta pulavarkaḷiyumpaṟṟi nām kēṭkiyil, itu uḷḷatāyirukkumō, ulakamuṇṭāki iṉṉum 6,000 varuṣamākavilliyē 9,000, 10,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukkuk kaṇakkuc collukiṟārēyeṉṟu nām niṉippōm. 3,400 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ mōcē muṉivarāl eḻutappaṭṭa ātiyākamattil ulakaciruṣṭi kirama varalāṟṟil collappaṭum ovvoru tiṉamum ovvoru ūḻikālamā yirukkavēṇṭumeṉpatu carittirakkārarkaḷuṭiya koḷki. ēṉeṉṟāl, mōcēyiṉ pēḻikkuḷ vantaṭinta mirukaṅkaḷip pārkkilum ettaṉiyō maṭaṅku peritāṉa mirukaṅkaḷiṉ elumpukaḷ maṇṇil putintiruppatākak kaṇṭupiṭittirukkiṟārkaḷ. mikap peritāṉa maṉita elumpukaḷum varavarak kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. kaṭalāl aḻiyāta cila mēṭṭup pāṅkāṉa iṭaṅkaḷil 20,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku mēṟpaṭṭavikaḷāṉa maraṅkaḷ irukkiṟatākak kaṇṭupiṭittirukkiṟārkaḷ. marattiṉ vaḷarcciyikkāṭṭum rēkikaḷik koṇṭu kaṇikkum varuṣaṅkaḷ tavaṟutaluṭiyaviyākā veṉṟu tuṇintu collalāmeṉpatip piṉ varum vākkiyaṅkaḷāl kaṇṭukoḷvōm.
Doubts of Infidels,
“The bones of man, of the type of the North American Indian, have been exhumed from the delta of the Mississippi at New Orleans, which were found lying below the fourth forest level, and making large allowance, must have lin here for more than fifty-thousand years. The exhumed relics of ancient civilization in the valley of the Nile antedate the History of the Jewish theocracy and the foot prints of the Creator are found in the granite pages of the primary and fossiliferous rocks, long anterior to the fabulous era of this Genesical history of creation. Humboldt describes a tree now growing in the famous gardens of Montezuma, as more than six-thousand years old, and another in Central America as but little less than twenty-thousand years old.”
“vaṭaamerikka intiyarkaḷiṉ tēka amippiyotta maṉita elumpukaḷ, micicippinatiyiṉ caṅkama pūmiyil niyu ārliyaṉs eṉṉum iṭattil veṭṭiyeṭukkappaṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉa. vaṉamaṭṭattiṉ nālāvatu paṭittarikkukkīḻē avikaḷ putintu kiṭantaṉa. ākiyāl eppaṭippārttālum, kuṟinta paṭcam 50,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku mēlākavē avikaḷ aṅkē iruntirukkavēṇṭum. nīlanatiyiṉ paḷḷattākkil veṭṭiyeṭukkappaṭṭavikaḷāṉa paṇṭikkālattu nākarīkac ciṉṉaṅkaḷ yūtaruṭiya vētacarittirattukku muṟpaṭṭavikaḷāyirukkiṉṟaṉa. aṅkuḷḷa pāṟikaḷiṉ mutal aṭukkilum stāvarajaṅkama cilārūpaṅkaḷaṭaṅkiya aṭukkukaḷilum ciruṣṭikarttāviṉuṭiya aticayacceyalkaḷiṉ aṟikuṟikaḷ kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. ivikaḷellām ātiyākamattil kūṟiyuḷḷa ciruṣṭippuc carittirattukku nīṇṭakālattiṟku muṟpaṭṭavikaḷā yirukkiṉṟaṉa. moṉṟijūmāviṉ pērpeṟṟa tōṭṭattil 6,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku mēṟpaṭṭa oru marattippaṟṟiyum mattiya amerikkāvil koñcaṅkuṟiya 20,000 varuṣaṅkaḷāyirukkiṟa iṉṉoru marattip paṟṟiyum hampōlṭs eṉpavar viparamāyk kūṟukiṟār.”
vaṭaamerikka intiyariṉ tēka amippiyotta maṉita elumpukaḷ niyūārliyaṉs eṉṉum iṭattil veṭṭiyeṭukkappaṭṭaṉaveṉṟum avikaḷ kuṟintatu 50,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku mēluḷḷatāka irukkavēṇṭu meṉṟum collukiṟār. piraḷayattiṉāl kāṭukaḷum cōlikaḷumāyirunta iṭaṅkaḷ maṇṇāl mūṭappaṭṭu maṟupaṭiyum tariyāka ataṉmēl anēkāyiram varuṣaṅkaḷāṉatiṉ piṉ vēṟoru piraḷayamuṇṭāki maraṅkaḷ cōlikaḷi aḻittu maṇṇāl nirappiyatākavum ippaṭiyē nālutaram piraḷayamuṇṭāki vaṉaṅkaḷi aḻittu maṇpaṭiyuṇṭāyirukkiṟatākavum terikiṟatu. ovvoru maṇpaṭiyiṉ kuṟippukaḷiṉālum appaṭikaḷi lirukkum maraṅkaḷiṉ kuṟippukaḷiṉālum ovvorupaṭiyum uttēcam anēka āyiramvaruṣaṅkaḷāki yirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟu cāstirikaḷ kaṇakkiṭukiṟārkaḷ. itil kālāvatiyāyirukkum aṭippaṭiyil vaṭaamerikka intiyariṉ aṅkaṅkaḷ putintiruppatik kaṇṭupiṭittavarkaḷ avikaḷ maṇṇil 50,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉālēyē putintirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟu collukiṟārkaḷ. itupōlavē nīlanatiyiṉ kari yōraṅkaḷilum pūrva nākarīkattik kuṟikkum ciṉṉaṅkaḷiruntatākac collukiṟārkaḷ. mēlum jīvaṉuḷḷavikaḷil vekukālam nīṭit tirukkakkūṭiya papparappuḷi yeṉṟamaram 20,000 varuṣaṅkaḷ āyuḷuṭiya tāyirukkiṟateṉṟu collukiṟārkaḷ. immaram teṉṉintiyāvil aṅkaṅkē nāḷatu variyum kāṇkiṟōm. teṉṉintiyāviṟku itu iyaṟkiyāyuḷḷa maramēyoḻiya nūtaṉamāṉa maramalla. mēlum immarattiṉ taṉmiyi aṟinta cittarkaḷ atil oru ciṟu tuvārañceytu turucu cuṇṇatti amukkivippārkaḷ; ciṟitu nērattukkuḷ atiliruntu taṇṇīr ūṟum; antat taṇṇīric ciṟu pāttiraṅkaḷil piṭittut taṅkaḷukku vēṇṭiya aḷavu cāppiṭuvārkaḷ. cāppiṭṭavuṭaṉ taṅkaḷiyaṟiyāta mayakkam avarkaḷukku uṇṭāka nālunāḷ pariyantam pirakkiṉiyaṟṟup paṭuttiruntu piṉ eḻuntiruppārkaḷ. ippaṭi ataṉ racattic cāppiṭuvatu avarkaḷ ceyyum yōkacātaṉikkum kaṟpacātaṉikkum aṉukūlamāṉateṉṟu eṇṇappaṭukiṟaJ. oru marattiṉ upayōkatti naṉṟāy oru tēcattār aṟintiruppārkaḷāṉāl anta maram attēcattiṟkēyuriya teṉṟu collāmalē viḷaṅkum. teṉṉintiyāviṉ cila iṭaṅkaḷil itippūta virukṣameṉṟu colluvārkaḷ. itu paricuttamāṉa virukṣameṉṟu potuvāka eṇṇappaṭukiṟaJ. ākiyiṉāl ataṉ kiḷikaḷiyāvatu ilikaḷiyāvatu oruvarum cētappaṭuttukiṟatilli. ataṉ aṭippākam peruttu nuṉippākam ciṟuttuk kōpurattiṉ cāyvāka vaḷarntirukkum. immarattit tañcāvūr jillāvilum ērāḷamāyk kāṇalām. tirunelveli maturi mutaliya iṭaṅkaḷilum aṅkaṅkēyirukkiṟatu. tañcāvūril, civakaṅkit tōṭṭattil oru maramum veṇṇāṟṟaṅ kariyil oru maramumirukkiṟaJ. ippaṭiyē kāvērippāyccalilum aṉēka maraṅkaḷirukkalām. immaraṅkaḷik kuṟukkāka aṟuttu avikaḷil cuṟṟic cuṟṟiyirukkum rēkikaḷikkoṇṭu ittaṉi varuṣamāka immaraṅkaḷ niṟkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu cāstirikaḷ kaṇṭupiṭikkiṟārkaḷ. ivikaḷikkoṇṭu nām collukiṟa mutal ūḻiyiṉ kālam atikamākāteṉṟē tōṉṟukiṟatu. immaraṅkaḷ vēṟu cila iṭaṅkaḷiliruppatāka aṭiyilvarum vacaṉattil kāṇalām.
Dravidian comparative Grammar by Bishop Caldwell,
“Huge old speciments of the Baobab, or Adansonia Digitata, an African tree, of which the Hindus do not know even the name, may still be seen in or near - various sites of foreign commerce in the extreme south of the Indian peninsula: e.g. in Kottar, near Cape Comorin, and near Tuticorin in Tinnevelly - possibly  on the site of the ancient Kolkhi.”
“intiyāviṉ teṉkōṭiyil, anniyatēcaṅkaḷōṭu varttakam naṭattivanta anēka tuṟimukappaṭṭaṇaṅkaḷilum avikaḷukkuccamīpattilum, pēyōpāp allatu aṭaṉsōṉiyā ṭijiṟṟāṟṟā eṉṟa āppirikkāk kaṇṭattu maram, nīṇṭu pārittup piramāṇṭamāy vaḷarntu niṟpatik kāṇalām. avikaḷ vekukālattu maraṅkaḷ. utāraṇamāka, kaṉṉiyākumari muṉikkuc camīpamāṉa kōṭṭāṟṟilēyum tirunelvēliyiccārnta tūttukkuṭikkuc camīpamākak koṟki yeṉṟa paḻiyanakarirunta iṭattilēyum immaraṅkaḷ niṟkiṉṟaṉa. ivikaḷiṉ pērmutalāy intukkaḷukkut teriyavilli.”
mēlēkūṟiya pēyōpāp eṉṟamaram āppirikkākkaṇṭattu marameṉṟum aṉṉiyatēca viyāpāracampantamāṉa tuṟimukappaṭṭaṇaṅkaḷil atu kāṇappaṭukiṟateṉṟum ataṉ peyar intukkaḷukkut teriyavilli yeṉṟum collukiṟār. immarattiṉ upayōkatti aṟintirunta teṉṉintiya jaṉaṅkaḷukku atu nūtaṉamāṉa maramalla. āṉāl āppirikkāvukkum teṉṉintiyāvukkum naṭuviluḷḷa pūpākattil immaram ērāḷamā yiruntirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟum, appūpākam aḻintapiṉ ataṟkuc campantappaṭṭatāṉa pūmikaḷil aṅkaṅkē kāṇappaṭukiṟateṉṟum nām niṉikka ētuvirukkiṟatu. ammarattippaṟṟi,

uviṉslō akarāti
“papparappuḷi - (also perukkamaram) A species of large tree, Adonsonia digitata, L” eṉṟum, vācutēvanāyuṭu eḻutiya
āyurvēta pārāvāram
“yāṉippuḷiyamaram, papparappuḷi, pūrimam, aṭaṉsōṉiyā ṭijiṭāṭā haṉaṭiḷēṭinyi ṉupiviyavaya pēyōpāp kṣayaṭiyeb maṅki preṭ ṭrī  āṭimēnala-cenayaṉa vacanana. āpirikkā tēcattilum ippōtu namatu tēcattilum inta maram vistāramāyviṭṭatu. paḻattiṉ catikku saṅkōcani (narampukaḷiyum carīratātukkaḷiyum curukki utiram cīḻ mutaliyavaṟṟi niṟuttum) antaras niktakāri (uṭkoṇṭāl tātukkaḷiṉ ericcalic cānti ceytu avaṟṟittuvaḷvikkum) ceykikaḷuṇṭu. itic cītapētikkum cītacuraṅkaḷukkum koṭukkalām.”
eṉṟum colliyiruppatiṉāl mēlēkūṟiya maram papparappuḷi eṉṟu aṟiyalām. mēṟkaṇṭa varikaḷik kavaṉikkiyil narampukaḷiyum carīratātukkaḷiyum curukki utiram cīḻ mutaliyavaṟṟi niṟuttakkūṭiyatāṉa cakti papparappuḷi (pūta virukṣam) eṉṟa marattukku iruntatiṉāl cittarkaḷ ati upayōkappaṭuttukiṟatākac collappaṭukiṟatu mikapporuttamākat terikiṟatu. nīṇṭa āyuḷuḷḷa immaram nīṇṭa āyuḷi virumpit tavañceyta periyōrkaḷuḷḷa lemūriyāvil atu pūrvamāyiruntirukka vēṇṭum. lemūriyā aḻintupōṉa piṉ atōṭu nilac campantam peṟṟa āppirikkā teṉṉintiyā mutaliya pūpākaṅkaḷil kāṇappaṭuvatu iyaṟkiyē. potikimaliyiṉ pakkattilum kāvēriyāṟṟiṉ pakkaṅkaḷilum iruppatē avikaḷ maṟṟat tēcaṅkaḷiliruntu varavilli yeṉpatik kāṭṭukiṟatu.
Castes and Tribes of Southern India by E. Thurston Vol. I, Intro. P. XXIV.
“On the evidence of the very close affinities between the plants and animals in Africa and India at a very remote period, Mr. R.D. Oldham concludes that there was once a continuous stretch of dry land connecting South Africa and India.”
“mikappaḻamiyāṉakālaṅkaḷil āppirikkāvilirunta stāvarajaṅkamaṅ kaḷukkum intiyā vilirunta stāvarajaṅkamaṅkaḷukkum irukkum neruṅkiṉa oṟṟumiyikkoṇṭu, ār.ṭi. ōlṭahām eṉpavar, orukālattil teṉ āppirikkāviyum intiyāviyum orētoṭarcci yāṉa peruntari iṇittuk koṇṭiruntatu eṉṟu tīrmāṉikkiṟār.”
mēṟkaṇṭavarikaḷi nām kavaṉikkiyil lemūriyākkaṇṭamum teṉ āppirikkāvum vekukālattukkumuṉ toṭarcciyāṉa pūcampanta muṭiyatāyiruntateṉṟu terikiṟatu. lemūriyā aḻintapiṉ āppirikkāviṉ teṉpākattilum teṉṉintiyāvilum kāṇappaṭum tāvara varkkaṅkaḷiṉ oṟṟumiyē itaṟkuk kāraṇameṉṟuñ collukiṟār. itikkoṇṭu papparappuḷi eṉṟa maram āppirikkāviliruntu, koṇṭuvarap paṭavilli, teṉṉintiyāviṟkēyuriya marameṉṟu teḷivākatterikiṟatu.
mēlum patiṉāyira varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉālēyē ekiptiyar kaḷiruntārkaḷeṉṟum, avarkaḷ taṇṭacakkaram mutaliya yantiraṅkaḷiṉ utaviyiṉṟik kiyiṉālēyē maṇpāṇṭaṅkaḷ ceytārkaḷeṉṟum kāṇkiṟōm.
Hutchinson’s History of the Nations,
Early Egyptians making pottery 10,000 years ago.
“The most abundant handwork of the early Egyptians was the finely made pottery entirely formed by hand. It was built up from the base and in form so true that no error is pereeptible. The facing was finished with a coat of red hacmatite, which turned to a brilliant black in the furnace. It is interesting to note that the same materials are used in the same kindof patterns by the hill tribes at the back of Algeria at the present time.”
āti ekiptiyar 10,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ maṇpāṇṭaṅkaḷ ceytal.
“āti ekiptiyar aparimitamāyc ceytuvanta kittoḻil eṉṉaveṉṟāl, veku nērttiyāy yantira utaviyillāmal kiyiṉāl ceyta maṇpāttiravēliyē, aṭimutal uruvattil yātoru picakum illāmal kiyālēyē atu ceyyappaṭṭaJ. civappuc ceṅkal poṭiyiṉāl ataṟku mēl pūccuppūcappaṭṭaJ. iti neruppil cuṭṭavuṭaṉ antaccivappu kaṟuppu niṟamāy māṟiṉaJ. itil vicēṣamāyk kavaṉikka vēṇṭiyatu, atē taḷavāṭaṅkaḷiṉāl atēvita maṭpāttiraṅkaḷ ippōtu aljīriyāvukkuppiṉ pakkattiluḷḷa malivācikaḷāl ceyyappaṭukiṟatu eṉpatē.”
itaṉāl, iṟṟikkup pattāyira varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉālēyē maṉitarkaḷiruntirukkiṟār kaḷeṉṟu terikiṟatu. mēlum ataṟku nīṇṭakālam muṉṉākavē pūrvamāka maṉitarkaḷiruntirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟum itik koṇṭu collalām. ēṉeṉṟāl, maṉuṣar uṇṭāṉapiṉ avarkaḷi aṭintirukakum tērcciyikkoṇṭu nāṭkaḷik kavaṉippōmāṉāl, mutalmutal kāykaḷiyum paḻaṅkaḷiyum vittukkaḷiyum tēṉiyum cāppiṭṭukkoṇṭirunta kālameṉṟum, iraṇṭāvatu karakarappuḷḷa kiḻaṅkukaḷiyum kāykaḷiyum koṭṭikaḷiyum cuṭṭuttiṉṉa neruppiṉ upayōkatti aṟintakālameṉṟum, mūṉṟāvatu camippataṟku aṉukūlamāka maṭpāttiraṅkaḷi yuṇṭākki atil pākappaṭuttiya tāṉiyavakikaḷiyum kāy kaṟi kīrikaḷiyum cāppiṭṭu vantakālameṉṟum nām niṉikka vēṇṭum. iṉṉum karuvikaḷ campantamākavum vastira campantamākavum vācastala campantamākavum carittira ārāyccikkārar veku pūrvīkattic collukiṟārkaḷ. avvitamāy nām kavaṉikkiyil oru ekiptiyaṉ maṇpāttiram ceyta kālam maṉuṣaṉ uṟpattiyāṉa kālattiṟku veku kālattiṟkup piṉṉuḷḷatāyirukkavēṇṭum. aimpatiṉāyiram varuṣaṅ kaḷukkumuṉ putintirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟu coṉṉa maṉita elumpik koṇṭu maṉitarkaḷ vekukālattiṟku muṉṉālēyuṇṭāyirukka vēṇṭu meṉṟu terikiṟatu. amerikkāvilum ekiptilum kāṇappaṭum poruḷkaḷ teṉṉintiya kaṇṭattilum atāvatu lemūriyāvilum maṉitarkaḷirun tirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟu rucuppaṭuttukiṉṟaṉa. ākiyiṉāl cumār 8,000 varuṅkaḷukku muṉ tolkāppiyariruntāreṉṟum 12,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉpē mutal caṅkam ārampittateṉṟum nām colvatu atikamākāJ. 8,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉum 12,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukkup piṉṉumāṉa mutaṟcaṅkattil akastiyariruntāreṉṟum atil iyal, ici, nāṭaka meṉṉum muttamiḻippaṟṟiyum ilakkaṇañ coṉṉār eṉṟum colvatu aṟpa āyuḷuḷḷa namakku āccariyamākattōṉṟum. iṟṟikku 1,800 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉiruntu iṟiyaṉārakapporuḷukku uriyeḻutiya nakkīraṉār collukiṟati nām nampāmal irukkalāmō? avar colliya vacaṉaṅkaḷ poyyāyirukkumō? ‘neṟṟikkaṇkāṭṭiṉālum kuṟṟam kuṟṟamē’yeṉṟu paramacivaṉōṭu vātāṭiya oru vitvacirōmaṇi poy colluvārā? avar appaṭic coṉṉālum avar kālattilirunta tamiḻc caṅkappulavarkaḷ maṟukkāmal viṭṭu viṭuvārkaḷā? oruvar etiyuñ collukiṟatu, tāṭcaṇiyattiṟkāka maṟṟavar kēṭṭukkoṇṭirukkiṟatu eṉṟa ikkālamallavē akkālam. ikkālatti aṭiyārkku nallārum collukiṟār; ākiyiṉāl ikkāriyam pūrvantoṭṭu uṇmiyāṉa tākavē tōṉṟukiṟatu. tolkāppiyattil kāṇappaṭum mutal ūḻiyi lirunta tēcattiṉ ellikaḷum tamiḻppāṣiyiṉ ciṟanta ilakkaṇamum iṟṟikku 8,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉālēyē collappaṭṭirukkiṟatē pōtumāṉatākat tōṉṟukiṟatu.
15. teṉṉintiyāviṉ mārkkam
pūrvattiluḷḷōrippaṟṟi nām vicārikka ārampippōmāṉāl, nītitavaṟāmal aracāṭciceyta rājākkaḷiyum iṭiviṭāmal teyvatti vaṇaṅkivanta paktarkaḷiyum paktiyilum kalikaḷilum ciṟanta cittarkaḷiyum (riṣikaḷ) paṟṟi vistāramākak kēḷvippaṭuvōm. teṉṉintiyāviṟkut teṉpakkattiluḷḷa lemūriyāvilirunta jaṉaṅkaḷākiya avarkaḷ intu matattiṟkum teyva vaṇakkattiṟkum nākarīkattiṟkum kāraṇamāyiruntārkaḷeṉṟum intu mārkkam avarkaḷālēyē vaṭanāṭṭiṟkuk koṇṭupōkappaṭṭateṉṟum piṉ varum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
District Manual of Madras, Pt. III,
“Mr. Pope in his edition of the Abbe Dubois’ work says that in South India numberless legends relating to devout worship pers of the Linga are current: that some of them are curious, and they are exclusively of southern origin. And Wilson states in his introduction to the catalogue that tradition uniformly Points to an extension of Hinduism and civilization from the extreme south of the Peninsula.”
“teṉṉintiyāvil liṅkattip pūcittavarkaḷippaṟṟiya kaṭṭuk katikaḷ anēkamuṇṭu. avikaḷil anēkam vinōtamāyum, teṟkē yiruntu uṟpattiyāṉatāyum tōṟṟukiṉṟaṉa eṉṟu pōp turi Abbe Dubois nūliṉ patippuriyil kūṟukiṟār. intumatamum nākarīkamum intiyāviṉ teṉkōṭiyil uṟpattiyāki vaṭakkē koṇṭupōkappaṭṭatu eṉṟu pārampariyaṅkaḷ oṉṟu pōlak kūṟukiṉṟaṉa eṉṟu ṭākṭar uvilcaṉ (Dr. Wilson) tamatu nūliṉ mukavuriyil collukiṟār.”
mēlēkaṇṭa vākkiyaṅkaḷiyum, kiruṣṇapakavāṉ tam jaṉaṅkaḷit tuvārakiyiliruntu vaṭakiḻakkiluḷḷa intirapirastattil jalappiraḷayat tiṟkut tappippōyk kuṭiyēṟumpaṭi arccuṉaṉukkuc coṉṉatiyum, jalappiraḷayattiṉ aḻiviṉiṉṟu tappiya 300 cōḻiya pirāmaṇarkaḷip pirāmaṇamatam viruttiyākap paramacivaṉ āviṭiyārkōyilil vittuk kāppāṟṟiyatiyum kavaṉikkiyil, teṟkiliruntu jaṉaṅkaḷ vaṭakkē pōṉārkaḷeṉṟu colluvatu oppukkoḷḷak kūṭiyatāyirukkiṟatu. atōṭukūṭa, teṉṉintiyāviṉ mukkiyamāṉa iṭaṅkaḷ ovvoṉṟilum piramāṇṭamāṉa kōyilkaḷ aṟputamāṉa kalvēlippāṭu kaḷuḷḷavikaḷāyum peṉṉamperiya tērkaḷuḷḷavikaḷāyum irukkavum civaṣēttiraṅkaḷum liṅka stāpaṉaṅkaḷum ērāḷamāy irukkavum kāṇkiṟōm. vaṭintiyāvilō, appērppaṭṭa kōyilkaḷik kāṇpatu mika ariJ. mēlum teṉmaturiyiluḷḷōr vicēṣamāka civapaktiyiṟ ciṟantavarkaḷāyiruntārka ḷeṉpatu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. civaperumāṉum avar kumāraṉākiya murukakkaṭavuḷum vikṉēsvararum teṉmaturi yiluḷḷōr koṇṭāṭum tēvarkaḷāyiruntārkaḷ. aḻintupōṉa lemūriyā eṉṉum perum kaṇṭattiluḷḷa tēcaṅkaḷ yāvaṟṟilum civapaktiyē vicēṣittiruntatāka nām kāṇalām. civaperumāṉum murukak kaṭavuḷum teṉmaturi rājyattiṟku mūlapuruṣarkaḷāyiruntu anēka cīrtiruttaṅkaḷ ceytu muṉpiṉ 4,400 varuṣaṅkaḷākavirunta mutaṟ caṅkattil talimi vakittut tamiḻi ātarittuvantavarkaḷāyu miruntārkaḷ. ataṉālum avarkaḷiṭattil viḷaṅkiya teyvīkattiṉālum avarkaḷiṭattil pakti koṇṭu peṉṉam periyakōyil kaṭṭi mūla stāṉattil liṅkastāpaṉam ceytu tērkaḷ amittu uṟcavaṅkaḷ koṇṭāṭiṉārkaḷ. kōyilkaḷil uṟcavaṅkaḷum tiṉapūjiyum kiramamāy naṭantu varuvataṟkeṉṟu māṉiyaṅkaḷum kāṇikkikaḷum koṭuttu vantirukkiṟārkḷa. ulakam muṭintālum oḻintupōkātirukkumpaṭi umpaḷam campaḷaṅkaḷiṉāl ūḻiyam ēṟpaṭutti oḻuṅkupaṭuttiyirukkiṟārkḷa. pakal iravāka 60 nāḻikiyilum iṉṉiṉṉa pūji iṉṉiṉaṉa aṉṉapāṉāti kaḷuṭaṉ iṉṉiṉṉarāl naṭattappaṭa vēṇṭumeṉṟu niyamam ceytirukkiṟārkaḷ. kōyilkaḷil teyva cannitiyil āṭukiṟavarkaḷum pāṭukiṟavarkaḷum taṅkaḷukkuk kuṟikkappaṭṭa kālaṅkaḷil taṅkaḷ paṇiviṭikaḷic ceyvārkaḷ. teyva paktiyuḷḷa ovvoruvarum taṅkaḷ kikaḷil kāṇikkikaḷi eṭuttukkoṇṭu taṅkaḷukku avakācamāṉa enta nērattilum pōy cuvāmi taricaṉañ ceyvārkaḷ. kōyilukku vēṇṭiya puṣpaṅkaḷ tarum nantavaṉaṅkaḷi vittu vaḷarppatu, atiliruntu atikāliyil puṣpaṅkaḷ eṭuttukkoṭuppatu, kōyil pirākāraṅkaḷic cuttañ ceyvatu, viḷakkup pōṭuvatu mutaliya kiṅkariyaṅkaḷi mika uṟcākattōṭu ceyvārkaḷ. teyva cannitiyil tēvāram tiruvācakam pōṉṟa paṇkaḷiṉāl maṉamurukappāṭi teyvatti ārātippārkaḷ. aimpulaṉkaḷiyumaṭakki, pakavāṉuṭiya cannitiyil niṣṭiyiruntu tiyāṉippārkḷa. irākāti tīkkuṇaṅkaḷukku iruppiṭamākiya uṭali varuttum virataṅkaḷ aṉuṣṭippārkaḷ. iṉṉum niṉittaṟkum collutaṟku muṭiyāta anēkavitamāṉa ārātaṉikaḷālum kāṇikki kaḷālum āṭal pāṭalkaḷālum ālayam eppoḻutum niṟintirukkum. iti ālavāy eṉṉum uttaramaturiyiṉ ālayattilē ammaṉ canniti yilē ari nāḻiki kāttiruntu pārttavarkaḷ naṉṟāy aṟivārkaḷ. cariyi kiriyi yōkam ñāṉam eṉṉum nālu paṭikaḷiyumuṭiyavarkaḷ taṅkaḷ taṅkaḷ aṟivukkēṟṟa vitamāy ārātittu varakkūṭiyatāka ālayaṅkaḷ amikkappaṭṭiruntaṉa.
jalappiraḷayattiṟkut tappiṉa cilar, teṉṉintiyāviṟku vantu kuṭiyēṟit taṅkaḷ kālattiluḷḷa pūrvamāṉa ālayaṅkaḷippōla ālayaṅkaḷ kaṭṭi, avviṭattil tāṅkaḷ vaṇaṅkiya teyvattiyēiṅkē vaṇaṅkiṉārkaḷeṉṟum teriyavarukiṟatu.
eppaṭi intumatam teṉṉintiyāviṉ teṟkiluḷḷa vistāramāṉa nāṭukaḷil mikap pūrvīkamāyiruntatō, appaṭiyē apperunāṭṭiṉ pāṣi yākiya tamiḻum evvitamāṉa kalappumaṟṟut taṉippāṣiyāy jalap piraḷayattukku muṉṉālēyē yiruntateṉṟu piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil pārppōm.
IV. tamiḻppāṣiyiṉatu toṉmi.
1. teṉṉāṭṭiluḷḷōr vaḻaṅkivanta tamiḻp pāṣiyiṉ pūrvīkamum ciṟappum.
iti vācikkum kaṉavāṉkaḷē, teṉṉintiya caṅkītattiṉ nuṭpattiyum ataṉ pūrvīkattiyum nām aṟiyavēṇṭumāṉāl caṅkītat tamiḻiyum nāṭakat tamiḻiyum taṉ aṅkamākakkoṇṭu muttamiḻ eṉṟu peyarvaḻaṅkum tamiḻppāṣiyippaṟṟi nām caṟṟuvicārikka vēṇṭiyatu avaciyam. tamiḻppāṣiyuṇṭāṉa kālamē karnāṭaka caṅkītamum uṇṭāṉa kālamām; tamiḻppāṣikkuriya iṉimiyē teṉṉintiya caṅkītattiṉ iṉimiyām. tamiḻppāṣi eppaṭi aṉṉiya pāṣikaḷōṭu kalavāta taṉitta pāṣiyō appaṭiyē teṉṉintiya caṅkītamum maṟṟac caṅkītaṅkaḷōṭu kalavāmal taṉitta vitikaḷuṭiyatām. aṟiñarkaḷāl uyarntateṉṟu eṇṇappaṭum teṉṉintiya caṅkītattiyum ataṉ maṟṟum varalāṟukaḷiyum nām vicārikku muṉ tamiḻppāṣiyiṉ toṉmi yiyum ataṉ ciṟappiyum paṟṟippārkka vēṇṭum. iṅkē maṟṟap pāṣikaḷōṭu oppiṭṭum maṟṟattēcaṅkaḷōṭu oppiṭṭum maṟṟavarāl tamiḻppāṣikku uṇṭāṉa naṉmi tīmikaḷik kāṭṭiyum aṟiñarkaḷ collum cila vacaṉaṅkaḷi eḻutiṉēṉē yoḻiya maṟṟa enta vitattilum oru pāṣiyiyāvatu oru jātiyāriyāvatu kuṟicolla vantatāka niṉiyātirukkumpaṭi mikavum kēṭṭuk koḷḷukiṟēṉ.
ulakac carittiraṅkaḷ eḻutappaṭuvataṟku muṉṉum maṟṟat tēcattār nākarīkamuṭiyavarkaḷākuvataṟku muṉṉum, aḻintupōṉa lemūriyāk kaṇṭattiluḷḷōr nākarīkamuṭiyavar kaḷāyiruntirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟu carittirakkārar colvataṟkiṇaṅka, akkaṇṭattiluḷḷa teṉmaturiyum, atil aracāṇṭu vanta pāṇṭiya rājarkaḷum caṅkappulavarkaḷum pēci vanta pāṣiyākiya tamiḻum, mikuntatoṉmiyum taṉicciṟappum vāyntavikaḷā yiruntaṉa. tamiḻ eṉṉum patattiṉ mutal iraṇṭu eḻuttukkaḷ cērnta tami eṉṉum mutaṉiliyāṉatu oppiṉmi, taṉimi eṉṟum, tamiḻ eṉṉum patamāṉatu iṉimi, maturam eṉṟum arttappaṭumeṉṟu nām aṟivōm. avvarttattiṟkiṇaṅkat tamiḻppāṣiyāṉatu mēṉmi poruntiyatāy maṟṟap pāṣikaḷ kalavātāyiruntateṉṟu cila aṟiñar collum apippirāyaṅkaḷi iṅkē pārppōm.
Manual of the Administration of the Madras Presidency, Vol. I, P. 42.
“There is little doubt that the Dravidian languages are incomparably older in point of time than the Sanskrit. It is not an unreasonable supposition that they once occupied the whole of Hindustan and have been driven to their present position to the south and along the coast by the encroachment of other languages coming from the North-west.”
“tirāviṭa pāṣikaḷ camaskiruta pāṣikku veku kālattukku muṉṉuḷḷavi yeṉpataṟkuc cantēkamēyilli. ātiyil avikaḷ intustāṉam muḻuvatum vaḻaṅkivantateṉṟum, piṟaku vaṭamēṟkiliruntu uṟpattiyāṉa maṟṟap pāṣikaḷ varavara, avi taṟkālam irukkiṟa kīḻkari, teṉkōṭi mutaliya iṭaṅkaḷukkut turattappaṭṭaṉa eṉṟum niṉikka iṭamuṇṭu.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷik kavaṉikkiyil, āriyar intiyāviṟku varuvataṟkumuṉ intutēcam muḻuvatilum pēcappaṭṭa pāṣi tamiḻ eṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu. mēlum tamiḻāṉatu kālacampavaṅkaḷiṉāl māṟutali yaṭintālum ataṉ ilakkaṇam māṟāmal orēmātiriyāyirukkakkūṭiya avvaḷavu tērcciyi anēkamāyiram varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉālēyē aṭintiruntateṉṟu piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Manual of the Administration of the Madras Presidency, Vol. I, P. 112.
“As far as present evidence goes, however, they are indigenous to India, and perhaps specially indigenous to Southern India........ As to their language, no other is known to which it can be affiliated. It stands alone, without any immediate predecessor. In origin, it must be long anterior to the Sanskrit, which has subsequently played so important a political part with regard to it. Its original strength is shown by the great persistence of its grammatical formations through all the vicissitudes of history.”
“taṟkālattil ēṟpaṭum kuṟippukaḷik kavaṉittāl, avarkaḷ intiyāvilē, mukkiyamāyt teṉṉintiyāvilē uṟpattiyāṉavarkaḷeṉṟu terikiṟatu. avarkaḷ pāṣiyōveṉṟāl, maṟṟa entap pāṣikkum contamāṉatākac collakkūṭiyatāka illi. ataṟkumuṉṉuḷḷa maṟṟentap pāṣiyōṭum atu cērāmal taṉiyē niṟkiṟaJ. uṟpattiyip pārttāl, atu camaskirutattukku veku kālattukku muṉṉuḷḷaJ. rājāṅka viṣayamāy camaskirutam piṟkālattil veku mukkiyattukku vantapōtilum uṟpattiyil atu pintiṉatu tāṉ, tamiḻppāṣiyiṉuṭiya ātipalatti yaṟiyavēṇṭumāṉāl, carittira campantamāṉa māṟutalkaḷ ettaṉiyō uṇṭākiyum ataṉ ilakkaṇa uṟuppukaḷ māṟāmal ekkālum atē vitamāy iruppatik kavaṉittāl viḷaṅkum.”
