mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷi nām kavaṉikkiyil, epirēya pāṣiyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷiliruntu cila kirēkka eḻuttukkaḷum kirēkka pāṣiyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷiliruntu pāli pāṣiyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷum, pāli pāṣiyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷiliruntu camaskiruta pāṣiyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷum avikaḷi liruntu anēka pirākirata pāṣiyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷum vantateṉṟum, appāṣiyiṉ vārttikaḷ oṉṟukkoṉṟu campantamuṭiyavarkaḷā yirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟum colluvatu pōla, tamiḻppāṣiyiṉ iṉṉa pāṣiyiliruntu uṇṭāṉateṉṟu colla ētuvillāmal taṉitta pāṣiyāyirukkiṟateṉṟu collukiṟār. mēlum ettaṉiyō carittira campantamāṉa māṟutalkaḷ uṇṭāṉapōtilum avikaḷiṉāl māṟṟappaṭāta ilakkaṇa vitikaḷip pūrvamāy aṭintirukkiṟateṉṟum collukiṟār. mikappūrvamāyuḷḷa ittamiḻmoḻi ataṉ piṉvanta vēṟu entap pāṣi yālum yātoru māṟutaliyumuṇṭākka muṭiyāmal taṉippāṣiyā yiruntu varukiṟateṉpatip piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Manual of the Administration of the Madras Presidency, Vol. I, P. 49.
“North Indian Civilization, when it came as far south as the Tamul country, found the people already in possession of the art of writing and of cultivated language. in consequence of this, Sanskrit did not regulate the Tamil phonetic system, and merely held the place of a foreign learned language.”
“vaṭintiyāviṉ nākarīkamāṉatu, teṟkē tamiḻ nāṭumaṭṭum vantapōtu, eḻuttu, pāṣi mutaliya nākarīkattukkuriya ciṉṉaṅkaḷ teṉṉāṭṭu jaṉaṅkaḷukkuḷ muṉṉamēyirukkak kaṇṭaJ. itaṉāl tāṉ camaskiruta pāṣiyāṉatu tamiḻp pāṣiyil evvita māṟutaliyum uṇṭākkamuṭiyāmal, oru aṉṉiya pāṣiyākavē iruntuvarukiṟatu.”
mēṟkaṇṭa varikaḷik kavaṉikkiyil vaṭatēcattiliruntu vanta āriyarippōlavē appōtirunta tamiḻmakkaḷum nākarīkattilum kalvi yilum ciṟantavarkaḷāyiruntārkaḷ; tāṅkaḷ pōkumiṭaṅkaḷil taṅkaḷ pāṣiyiṉ nalaṅkāṭṭi aṅkuḷḷa pāṣiyil pala māṟutalkaḷi yuṇṭākkiṉatu pōla tamiḻmoḻiyi māṟṟa avarkaḷāl iyalāmal pōyiṟṟeṉṟu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. mikat toṉmiyāṉa tamiḻ moḻikkut tirāviṭameṉṟum aticcērnta pāṣikaḷ pēcum tēcattukkut tirāviṭatēcameṉṟum jaṉaṅkaḷukkut tirāviṭareṉṟum āriyarkaḷ peyar vittārkaḷ. tamiḻeṉṟa patattic ciṟantatāka eṇṇum taṅkaḷ pāṣiyil eḻutikkoḷḷat tiṟamiyillāmal ataṟku muṟṟilum campanta millāta vēṟoru peyarikkoṇṭu aḻikkap pukuntārkaḷ eṉpatip piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇka.
ciṅkāravēlu mutaliyār eḻutiya apitāṉa cintāmaṇi. pakkam. 489.
“tamiḻ nāṭṭaracar mūvarum cūryacantira vamcattiliruntu pirintavarkaḷ; ivarkaḷ tuvāparayukattiṟku muṉpum aracāṇṭatākat terikiṟatu. oru pāṇṭiyaṉ pārata yuttattil pāṇṭavarkaḷiṉ cēṉikku aṉṉamiṭṭatākat terikiṟatu. ivaṉ tamiḻp pulavari ātarittavaṉ. *   *   *   *   *   *   * piṉṉumivvariya pāṣi maṟṟip pāṣikaḷpōl vēṟu pāṣikaḷiṉ tuṇivali peṟātu tāṉāy viḷaṅkum ēṟṟamuṟṟatu; itaṉi aṟivuṟḷōr palar paḻiya nūlkaḷiṟ kaṇṭaṟiyalām. āyiṉ tamiḻ eṉpatu tirāviṭam eṉpatiṉṟiripaṉṟōveṉiṉ tiripākāJ. tamiḻ, teluṅku, kaṉṉaṭam, makārāṣṭaram, kūrccaram ivikaḷiyun tirāviṭameṉpavākaliṉ itaṟkē yuriyatākāJ. āyiṉum appeyar kūṟavanta vaṭanūlār akkālattiṟku muṉṟāṉē tōṉṟiya pāṣikkut tāṅkaḷ peyaraṟiyātu annāṭṭil tāṅkaḷ vanta kālattu iṭṭa peyarākavumiruttaliṉ itu vaṭamoḻikkup pintiyatākāJ. vaṭamoḻi vaṭanāṭṭiliruntatupōl teṉmoḻi teṉṉāṭṭi liruntaJ. itaṉāl tamiḻp pāṣi taṉitta pūrva pāṣiyeṉpatu koḷḷakkiṭantaJ.”
tamiḻmoḻiyeṉṟu collavuṅkūṭa veṟutta āriyar, tirāviṭa pāṣiyeṉṟu peyar vittār. tamiḻ eṉṟa patam camaskiruta pāṣiyiṉiṉṟē yuṇṭāṉateṉṟu maṟṟavar eṇṇumpaṭi tirāviṭam eṉṟa vārtti, ṭa, vukku ḷa, varalām eṉṟa vitippaṭi tirāmiḷam eṉṟu vantatākavum tirāmiḷam eṉṟa vārtti iraṇṭām eḻuttuk kuṟukit tiramiḷam eṉṟu vantatākavum, ataṉpiṉ iraṇṭām eḻuttuk keṭṭut tamiḷam eṉṟu āṉatākavum, tamiḷamtāṉē amkeṭṭu tamiḻ eṉṟu vanta tākavum collukiṟārkaḷ. eppaṭiyāṉālum ākaṭṭum vi atāvatu vakara ikarattiṉ keti eṉṉavāyiṟṟu? ataṟkum oru niyāyam coṉṉāl naṉṟāyirukkum. ataṟkum makara irakattiṟkum eṉṉa campantam? ippaṭiyē taliyic cuṟṟi mūkkit toṭukiṟavaṉ katipōla collakkūṭiyatā yiruppatiṉāl allavō coṟpa varikaḷukkum arttanteriyāmal eḷiyanaṭiyiluḷḷa cāraṅkatēvar eḻutiya caṅkītaratṉākarattiṉ oru culōkattiṟku 20 vitamāy arttañceytum, uṇmiyaṟiyāmal pōṉārkaḷ. eḻuttu eḻuttāy arttañceyvati nām kavaṉittāl ellām piramam eṉṟu terintu koṇṭavaṉ muṉam cātittatu pōlākumē yoḻiya vāytiṟakkamuṭiyātu eṉpatu teḷivāyt terikiṟatu. camaskirutam uyarnta pāṣiyeṉṟum ariya viṣayaṅkaḷ atil eḻutappaṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟum nām oppuk koṇṭālum pēccuppaḻakkat tiṟku varāta pāṣiyeṉṟē collavēṇṭiyatāyirukkiṟatu.
tamiḻil vaḻaṅkum aṉēka vārttikaḷit taṅkaḷ pāṣiyil mutal mutal iruntatāka maṟṟavar niṉikkumpaṭi kālākālaṅkaḷil māṟṟi, māṟṟiyataṟ kiṇaṅka nūlkaḷum purāṇaṅkaḷum kaṭṭukkatikaḷuñ ceytu parappivantārkaḷ. nīr eṉṟa patam nīram eṉṟa camaskiruta patattiliruntu vantateṉṟum tamiḻar pēcum vārttikaḷil nūṟṟukkut toṇṇūṟu camaskirutameṉṟum vāy kūcāmal colluvārkaḷāṉāl vēṟu eṉṉa collamāṭṭārkaḷ! mutal ūḻikku muṉ 4,400 varuṣaṅkaḷākac caṅkam vittu naṭattiya vitvacirōmaṇikaḷum akkālattum ataṟku muṉṉumirunta ērāḷamāṉa tamiḻ makkaḷum taṇṇīr kuṭikkavilliyō? allatu ataṟku ēṟṟa peyar vikkat teriyātiruntārkaḷō? āriyar vantapiṉtāṉō avarkaḷ kuḷirnta nīrukku taṇṇīraṉṟum cūṭuḷḷa nīrukku vennīreṉṟum ippaṭiyē āṟṟunīr ūṟṟunīr cēṟṟunīr cennīr ceyanīr kaṭalnīr kuṭinīr iḷanīr kaṇṇīr umiḻnīr eṉṟum colla ārampittārkaḷ? ippaṭiyē ciṟanta ērāḷamāṉa tamiḻ moḻikaḷit taṅkaḷ pāṣiyiliruntē vantateṉṟu colvataṟku ēṟṟatākac cila eḻuttukkaḷik kūṭṭiyum cila eḻuttukkaḷik kuṟittum neṭil kuṟilākavum kuṟil neṭilākavum māṟṟiyum campantamillāta eḻuttukkaḷākattirittum vaḻaṅkiṉārkaḷ. tamiḻ nāṭṭiluḷḷa cila mukkiyamāṉa malikaḷukkum āṟukaḷukkum paṭṭaṇaṅ kaḷukkum maṉuṣarkaḷukkum camaskirutap peyarkaḷ vittup purāṇaṅkaḷum katikaḷum kaṭṭiṉārkaḷ. tāṅkaḷ ceyta purāṇaṅkaḷiyum kavikaḷiyum piṟar aṟiyap piracaṅkittu rājākkaḷiyum jaṉaṅkaḷiyum uṇmiyeṉṟu nampumpaṭi nīr eṉṟa vārttiyi, nīra nīram nāram eṉṟu māṟṟi avikaḷiliruntu nāratam nārāyaṇaṉ eṉṟu toṭuttukkoṇṭu piṉ nāratattilum, nīrattiliruntum nīr vantateṉṟu coṉṉati nampukiṟavarkaḷ, avarkaḷ kaṭṭiya katikaḷiyum nampiṉārkaḷ. avarkaḷ coṉṉati yellām nampukiṟavarkaḷ ēṟpaṭṭapiṉ camaskiruta pāṣiyiṉ aṉēka vārttikaḷ tamiḻil vantu vaḻaṅkattalippaṭṭaṉa. iti vivēkikaḷ aṟivārkaḷ. atikam colla iṅku avaciyamilli. iyal ici nāṭakameṉṉum muttamiḻiluḷḷa icit tamiḻākiya caṅkītattit teṉṉāṭṭiliruntē maṟṟavarkaḷ koṇṭupōṉārkaḷeṉpatu paṟṟiyum vaṭapāṣikkārarum tamiḻ nāṭṭiṉiṉṟē icit tamiḻik koṇṭu pōyirukka vēṇṭumeṉpatu paṟṟiyum iṅku eḻuta nēriṭṭatu. ataṉ viparam itaṉpiṉ pārppōm. tamiḻmoḻiyōṭu vaṭamoḻi kalantavitam piṉ varum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
2. tamiḻil camaskirutam kalanta varalāṟu.
Manual of the Administration of the Madras Presidency, Vol. I, P. (4.)
“It is to be observed that though the long list of names mentioned in the Pooranas are all Sanskrit, these are only book names. The names of the country reported or ascertined by Aryan travellers and settlers were invariably translated into Sanskrit by the literary caste of the Aryans. It is a very common error to suppose that becuse none but Sanskrit names are found in the ancient literature of the country, it was therfore a country occupied by an Aryan people, and that all the places mentioned were founded by the Aryans. But in fact as the Aryan visitors to India had the monopoly of literature, the indigenous names could only appear in a Sanskrit form; and no argument is to be thence deduced in one direction or another as to the extent of Aryan coloizations. In later times Aryan influence has undoubtedly given current names to geographical places even in Southern India. *    *    * It will be seen from the next note that Greek literature is analogous to Sanskrit in presenting indigenous Indian names in such a Greek dress that they are not easily recognisable; but the Greeks did not at all to the same extent actually translate Indian names.”
“purāṇaṅkaḷil collappaṭṭirukkiṟa nīṇṭa aṭṭavaṇip peyarka ḷellām camaskirutap peyarkaḷāyiruntapōtilum avikaḷellām pustakap peyarkaḷēyoḻiya uṇmiyāṉa peyarkaḷalla. āriyarāṉa pirayāṇikaḷum putukkuṭiyēṟuvōrkaḷum tāṅkaḷ kaṇṭatāka allatu kēḷvippaṭṭatākac collum tēcaṅkaḷiṉ peyarkaḷellām āriya vitvāṉkaḷāl tappāmal camaskirutattil moḻi peyarkkappaṭṭaṉa. intu tēcattiṉ paḻamiyāṉa nūlkaḷilellām camaskirutappeyarkaḷē kāṇappaṭuvatāl antat tēcam āriyarāl kuṭiyēṟappeṟṟa tēcameṉṟum atil colliyirukkum iṭaṅkaḷellām āriyarāl stāpikkappaṭ ṭaviyeṉṟu niṉippatu mikavum cātāraṇamāṉa tappāyirukkiṟatu. uṇmi eṉṉaveṉṟāl, intiyāvukku vanta āriyartāṉ intiya nūlkaḷ camastattiyum eḻutiṉavarkaḷāṉatāl aṅkaṅkuḷḷa iṭappeyarkaḷ camaskirutattil māttiram irukkumpaṭiyāyiṟṟu. intap peyarkaḷi vittukkoṇṭu āriyar kuṭiyēṟiṉa nāṭukaḷippaṟṟi ippaṭiyāvatu appaṭiyāvatu yātoṉṟi stāpikka muyaluvatu tappu. āṉāl pintiya kālaṅkaḷil āriyarukkuṇṭāṉa celvākkiṉāltāṉ teṉṉintiyāvil kūṭat taṟkālam vaḻaṅkivarum iṭappeyarkaḷ ellām camaskirutattil irukkiṉṟaṉa. piṉ collappōkum viṣayattiṉāl, camaskirutam pōlavē kirēkka nūlkaḷum intiyappērkaḷi kirēkkap pērkaḷpōla māṟṟic colvatiṉāl avikaḷ intiyap pērkaḷ eṉṟa uruvē teriyāmal pōkiṉṟaṉa eṉṟa caṅkati veḷiyākum. āṉālum āriyar ceytatu pōla kirēkkar avvaḷavu tūram intiyap pērkaḷik taṅkaḷuṭiyavipōl māṟṟikkoḷḷavilli.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷik kavaṉikkiyil tamiḻ moḻikaḷ āriyarāl camaskirutattil māṟṟappaṭṭup purāṇaṅkaḷil eḻutappaṭṭatākavum tāṅkaḷ kaṭṭāta paṭṭaṇattiṟkuṅ kuṭiyirāta vīṭṭukkum camaskirutap peyar vittuk kēḷvippaṭṭati māttiraṅkoṇṭu purāṇakkatikaḷ kaṭṭuvatil cilar tērntavarkaḷ eṉpatākavum terikiṟatu. tāṅkaḷ kēḷvippaṭṭati māttiramalla, uttēcamāy niṉippatiyum uḷḷatupōl eḻutivikkum cāmarttiyam āriyarukkuc cupāvamākavē amintirukkiṟaJ. avarkaḷiṟ cilar nūtaṉamāy ulakattil oṉṟu uṇṭāṉāl, atuvum eṅkaḷ paḻiya purāṇaṅkaḷi lirukkiṟateṉṟu collakkūṭiya vitamāyk kaṟpaṉi ceytuvippārkaḷ. intak kaṟpaṉikaḷukkuḷ intiyarkaḷ. kaṭṭuppaṭa vaḻakkappaṭṭuviṭṭārkaḷ. nūtaṉamāy intiyāvil piravēcitta āriyar, itaṉmuṉ intiyāvil vacitta jaṉaṅkaḷiyum avarkaḷ pāṣiyiyum appāṣiyiluḷḷa nūlkaḷiyum aṅkuḷḷa rājākkaḷ periyōrkaḷiṉ carittiraṅkaḷiyum muṟṟilum maṟittu, taṅkaḷ pāṣiyiyum taṅkaḷ nūliyum taṅkaḷ periyōriyumē colla ārampittārkaḷ. intiyāviṟku vanta kirēkkarkaḷ intiyāviluḷḷa cilapeyarkaḷit taṅkaḷ pāṣiyil māṟṟiyapōtu avikaḷ intiyappeyarkaḷeṉṟu uruvē teriyāmal pōyiṟṟeṉṟum atippārkkilum atikamāy āriyarkaḷ intiyapeyarkaḷic camaskirutattil māṟṟiṉārkaḷeṉṟum collukiṟār. intiyā āriyarālēyē kuṭiyēṟṟappaṭṭa tēcameṉṟum atiluḷḷa iṭaṅkaḷ ellām āriyarāl stāpikkappaṭṭaṉaveṉṟum niṉippatu mikavum cātāraṇamāṉa tappāyirukkiṟateṉṟu maklīṉ paṇṭitar collukiṟatu pōlavē, camaskirutattiliruntu tamiḻ uṇṭāṉateṉṟum, camaskiruta vārttikaḷ kalavāmal tamiḻp pāṣi vaḻaṅkamuṭiyāteṉṟum colvatum tappeṉṟu collavēṇṭiyatāyirukkiṟatu. mutal ūḻiyil mutal tamiḻccaṅkamum accaṅkattiṟkuriya nūlkaḷum attēcamum aḻintu pōṉapiṉ atil tolkāppiyam oṉṟē miñciṉatākak kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. ataṉpiṉ iṭiccaṅkattār kālattu uṇṭākiya pala nūlkaḷum iraṇṭāvatu ūḻiyil aḻintupōyiṉa. avaṟṟil miñciyirunta cila nūlkaḷum camaskiruta moḻikkalappālum camaskirutattiliruntu vantateṉṟu collum vacaṉattālum kaṟippaṭuttappaṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉa veṉṟu piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil terikiṟatu. 
tamiḻp paṇṭitar cūriyanārāyaṇa cāstiriyār B.A.,eḻutiya tamiḻ moḻiyiṉ varalāṟu, pakkam 14, 15.
“vaṭamoḻiyāḷar tamiḻarkaḷatu oḻukkavaḻakkaṅkaḷi yuṇarntu avaṟṟiṟkēṟpa vaṭamoḻiyil nūlkaḷ vakuppāṉ pukuntaṉar. avarkaḷellām āṉma nūṟpayiṟci mikkuṭiyārāyum, kaliyuṇarcci cāṉṟavarāyu miruntamipaṟṟit tamiḻaratu tivviya stalaṅkaḷukkup purāṇaṅkaḷ vakuttaṉar; tamiḻarkaḷiṭattillātirunta ‘antaṇar, aracar, vaṇikar, vēḷāḷar’ eṉṟa nālvakiccāti muṟiyi mella mella nāṭṭiviṭṭaṉar.
		‘muṟpaṭippataṉil vēṟākiya muṟimipōl
		nālvakic cātiyin nāṭṭiṉīr nāṭṭiṉīr!
eṉṟu āriyari nōkki muḻaṅkuṅ kapilarakavaliyuṅ kāṇka. iṉṉum avar tam puntinalaṅ kāṭṭit tamiḻaracarkaḷiṭam amiccarka ḷeṉavum mēlatikārap pirapukkaḷeṉavum amintukoṇṭaṉar; tamiḻariṭat tirunta pala ariya viṣayaṅkaḷiyum moḻipeyarttut tamiḻar aṟiyumuṉṉarē avaṟṟit tāmaṟintaṉa pōlavum vaṭamoḻiyiṉiṉṟumē tamiḻiṟku avi vantaṉapōlavum kāṭṭiṉar. *  *  *  *  *  *
tāṅkaḷ cellumiṭaṅkaḷukkut takkapaṭi putiya putiya ilipikaḷ ēṟpaṭuttikkoḷḷu miyalpuṭiya āriyar tamiḻnāṭṭiṟkēṟṟapaṭi, tamiḻilipiyi yoṭṭik ‘kirantam’ eṉṉum peyariṟ putuvatōr ilipi vakuttaṉar; tamiḻari vacīkarikkumāṟu avvilipiyiṟ pala nūlkaḷ varintaṉar. tamiḻppulavarāvār etaṟkum aciyātu taṅkaḷ tamiḻ moḻiyiṉ pōkkiyē taḻuvic celvārāyiṉār.”
mēṟkāṭṭiya vacaṉaṅkaḷik kavaṉikkiyil camaskiruta pāṣi yilēyē mikunta paṟṟuḷḷa oru āriyar colvati nām oppukkoḷḷa vēṇṭiyatāyirukkiṟatu. tamiḻariṭattirunta pala ariya viṣayaṅkaḷiyum moḻi peyarttut tamiḻar aṟiyumuṉṉamē avaṟṟit tām aṟintaṉa pōlavum vaṭamoḻiyiṉiṉṟumē tamiḻiṟku avi vantaṉapōlavum kāṭṭiṉāreṉṟu colvati nām kavaṉikkiyil, tamiḻiluḷḷa pala nūlkaḷ vaṭamoḻiyil māṟṟappaṭṭatākavum taṟkālamvaḻaṅkum tamiḻ nūlkaḷil camaskirutattiṟkup piṉṉē ivikaḷuṇṭāṉaviyeṉṟu tōṉṟumpaṭi cila tittuppāṭukaḷ aṅkaṅkē ceytatākavum niṉikka iṭantarukiṟaJ. vaṭamoḻic campantamuḷḷa cila pērakattiyac cūttiraṅkaḷum, tolkāppiyac cūttiraṅkaḷum, cila vittiya, vāta, yōka, ñāṉa, cōtiṭa nūlkaḷum cila tittuppāṭukaḷiyuṭiyaṉavāyirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu collattakkatāka aṅkaṅkē cila vārttikaḷum cūttiraṅkaḷum kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. akattiyaruṭiya māṇākkaril oruvarākiya kaḻārampar iyaṟṟiyatāka veḷi vantirukkum pēricic cūttiram eṉṉum nūli nām kavaṉippōmāṉāl, ivvuṇmi naṉku pulappaṭum. appustakattil mutalāvatu oli vaṭivāyirunta kālameṉṟum iraṇṭāvatu aṭcarakālameṉṟum mūṉṟāvatu ilakkaṇa kālameṉṟum nālāvatu caṅkakālameṉṟum nām aintāvatu maṭātipatikaḷiṉ ātīṉakālameṉṟum āṟāvatu camaṇakālameṉṟum ēḻāvatu purāṇakālameṉṟum eṭṭāvatu atamakālameṉṟum oṉpatāvatu tiruviḷiyāṭaṟ kālameṉṟum pattāvatu taṟkālameṉṟum collukiṟār. ivvoḻuṅkic cumār 34 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ vīracōḻiyattiṟkup patippuri eḻutiya ci.vi. tāmōtarampiḷḷi avarkaḷ vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇkiṟōm. ākiyāl vicu varuṣattiṟkup piṉṉē ippēricic cūttiram putitāka eḻutap paṭṭirukkavēṇṭum. atilum aintāvatu anātārakālameṉṟu tāmōtarampiḷḷi avarkaḷ coṉṉati māṟṟi
		“cittelā niṟintu cittā yamarnta
		tēcikar marapil ciṟantu viḷaṅkum
		maṭāti patikaḷā māṇpami ñāṉiyar
		aḷaviṟ paṭuvatav vatīṉa kālam.”
eṉṉum cūttiram aintāvatāka eḻutappaṭṭirukkiṟaJ. ivikaḷi nām kavaṉikkiyil, akattiyaruṭiya māṇākkar paṉṉiruvaruḷ kaḻārampar eḻutiyirukka māṭṭāreṉṟu tiṭṭamāyt terikiṟatu. tamiḻ naṭiyum ati rucuppaṭuttukiṟaJ. mēlum akattiyar kālattilirunta kaḻārampar taṟkālam varikkum tamiḻppāṣiyiṉ kālavarttamāṉatti eppaṭic coṉṉār? appaṭic coṉṉatu uṇmiyāṉāl taṟkālattukku mēlvarum piṟkālattippaṟṟi ēṉ yātoṉṟum collavilli? itu camas kirutattil pērapimāṉamuḷḷa oruvar kaṟpaṉiyē eṉpōm. itu pōlavē pērakattiya cūttiraṅkaḷilum tolkāppiyac cūttiraṅkaḷilum tamiḻ vaṭamoḻikkup pintiyateṉṟu kāṭṭumpaṭi ceytirukkum cila eḻuttu māṟutalkaḷiyum vārtti māṟutalkaḷiyum cūttiramāṟutalkaḷiyum itu mutaṟkoṇṭāvatu tamiḻ makkaḷ ūṉṟippārppārkaḷāka. ippaṭiyē tamiḻil vaḻaṅkiya caṅkītanūlum atāvatu icittamiḻum ātiyil camaskirutatti liruntē tamiḻarukku vantateṉṟu yāvarum eṇṇum paṭiyākiviṭṭatu. āṉāl camaskiruta nūlkaḷil vaḻaṅkivarum caṅkīta muṟikkum teṉṉāṭṭil vaḻaṅkum karnāṭaka caṅkīta muṟikkum mikunta vittiyāca mirukkiṟateṉṟu aṟivāḷikaḷ kāṇpārkaḷ. vaṭapāṣiyil eḻutappaṭṭa curuti muṟikaḷuḷḷa kāṉam teṉṉāṭṭil vaḻaṅkāmalpōṉālum vaṭapāṣiyiluḷḷa peyarkaḷē caṅkītattil vaḻaṅkivarukiṉṟaṉa. ivviparam yāvum itaṉpiṉ pārppōm. mēlum kaṭiccaṅka kālattil tōṉṟiya jiṉarālum āriyarālum camaskiruta vārttikaḷ tamiḻ moḻiyōṭu kalakka ārampittateṉṟum tamiḻ moḻikaḷ maṟṟappāṣikaḷōṭu kalantateṉṟum piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷāl terikiṟatu.
Manual of the Administration of the Madras Presidency, Vol. I, P. (41-42.)
“The greater number of the Sanskrit and Pracrita words in the Dravidian languages were introduced by the Jaina writers. Some tatsamas, however, were introduced by the three comparatively modern philosophic schools: the sheiva Siddhunta, the School of Sankaracharya and the School of Ramanoojacharya. Sanskrit words are sid to have been introduced even before the time of the Jains, but it is doubtful whether these are not ancient words common to both Aryan and Dravidian languages.”
“tirāviṭapāṣikaḷil varum camaskiruta pirākiruta vārttikaḷil mikutiyāṉavi jiṉa vittuvāṉkaḷāl mutal mutal tirāviṭa pāṣikaḷil upayōkikkappaṭṭaṉa. āṉālum cila taṟcamaṅkaḷ ciṟitu kālaṅkaḷukku muṉṉēṟpaṭuttappaṭṭa civacittāntam caṅkarācāriyam rāmāṉujācāriyam eṉṟa mūṉṟu camayavātikaḷālum mutal mutal ippāṣikaḷil upayōkik kappaṭṭaṉa. jiṉaruṭiya kālattukku muṉṉēyē camaskiruta vārttikaḷ upayōkikkappaṭṭu vantatākac collappaṭukiṉṟaJ. āṉāl avikaḷ āriya tirāviṭapāṣikaḷukkup potuvāṉa vārttikaḷāyirukkappaṭātō eṉṟu cantēkikka iṭamirukkiṟatu.”
itil, akkālattil vaḻaṅkivanta cila moḻikaḷ āriya tirāviṭa pāṣikaḷukkup potuvāṉa vārttikaḷāyirukkappaṭātōveṉṟu cantēkikka iṭamirukkiṟateṉkiṟār. camaskiruta pāṣiyil mikunta virākkiyamuḷḷa āriyar tāṅkaḷ cellumiṭaṅkaḷiluḷḷa pāṣikaḷil vaḻaṅkum vārttikaḷiyum karuttukkaḷiyum putitu putitāka amittu nūl uṇṭākkiṉārkaḷ. ākiyiṉāl tamiḻmoḻikaḷum camaskirutattil pala cērkkappaṭṭateṉṟu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. camaskiruta pāṣiyōṭu cērntu pūrva culōkaṅkaḷil amintapiṉ avvārttikaḷ iraṇṭukkum potuvāyirukkalāmōveṉṟu cantēkikka iṭantaruvatu kāla iyalputāṉē. iraval vāṅkiṉatit tirumpak koṭukkakkūṭāteṉṟa eṇṇam vantapiṉ ati iṉanteriyāmal paṇṇuvatu ulaka iyaṟkitāṉē. ‘iraval uṭimi, eṉak kicivāyirukkiṟatu, eṉ appā āṇi nāṉ koṭukka māṭṭēṉ’ eṉṟatupōla ituvumāyiṟṟu. ippaṭit tamiḻmoḻikaḷ palavum camaskirutattil kalanta piṉpum, anēka tamiḻ nūlkaḷ camaskirutattil tiruppappaṭṭapiṉpumtāṉ, camaskirutattiliruntē tamiḻmoḻikaḷ palavum, nūlkaḷ palavum vantaṉaveṉṟu collat tuṇintārkaḷeṉpatip piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Dravidian Comparative Grammar by Bishop Caldwell
“Professor Wilson observes that the spoken languages of the South were cultivated in imitation and privalry of the Sanskrit, and but partially aspired to an independent literature; that the pricipal compositions in Tamil, Telugu, Canarese, and Malayalam are trinslations of paraphrases from Sanskrit works; and that they largely borrow the phraseology of their originals. This representation is not perfectly correct, in so far as the Tamil is concerned; for the compositions that are universally admitted to be the ablest and finest in the language, viz., the Cural and the Chintamani, are perfectly independent of the Sanskrit, and original in design as well as in exccution.”
“teṟkē pēcappaṭukiṟa pāṣikaḷ camaskirutattip pārttu ataṟku māṟāka uṇṭākkappaṭṭavikaḷeṉṟum, avikaḷ cuya kirantaṅkaḷi uṇṭākkap pirayattaṉam paṇṇiyum palikkavilliyeṉṟum, tamiḻ teluṅku kaṉṉaṭam maliyāḷa pāṣikaḷiluḷḷa mukkiyamāṉa nūlkaḷellām camaskiruta nūlkaḷiṉ moḻi peyarppukaḷēyeṉṟum, avikaḷ camaskiruta moḻikaḷiyum toṭarmoḻikaḷiyum vācakappōkkiyum naṭiyiyu muṭiyavikaḷā yirukkiṉṟaṉa veṉṟum uvilcaṉ paṇṭitar kūṟukiṟār. ippaṭi avar kūṟuvatu, tamiḻppāṣiyiṉ viṣayattil muṟṟilum cariyalla. ēṉeṉil, antappāṣiyil mikavum nērttiyāṉavikaḷum ciṟantavikaḷumeṉṟu ulakattiluḷḷa aṟiñarkaḷ yāvarālum oppukkoḷḷappaṭukiṟa tirukkuṟaḷum cintāmaṇiyum camas kirutak kalappēyiṉṟi muḻutum tamiḻmayamākavē viḷaṅkukiṉṟaṉa.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷik kavaṉikkiyil, camaskiruta pāṣiyē muntiyuṇṭāṉateṉṟum ataṟku etiriṭiyākat tamiḻum tamiḻiccērnta pāṣikaḷu muṇṭākkappaṭṭaṉaveṉṟum tirāviṭa nūlkaḷ camaskiruta pāṣiyiṉ moḻi peyarppukaḷāyirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟum avaṟṟiṉ naṭi camaskirutattiṉ naṭiyiyē ottirukkiṟateṉṟum uvilcaṉ paṇṭitar cantēkikkiṟār. itupōlavē taṟkālattiluḷḷa camaskirutapaṇṭitar ovvoruvarum collikkoḷḷukiṟārkḷa. āriya pāṣiyiṉ vārttikaḷ kalantapiṉpum tamiḻ moḻikaḷi āriyapāṣi vārttikaḷākac cērttukkoṇṭa piṉpum camaskirutattiliruntē tamiḻ vantateṉṟu collāmal vēṟē eṉṉa colvārkaḷ? ceṉṟa 800 varuṣaṅkaḷukkuḷ pala purāṇaṅkaḷum cila vētānta nūlkaḷum camaskirutattiliruntu tamiḻil moḻipeyarkkappaṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉa eṉpatu uṇmiyē. āyiṉum atukoṇṭu ataṟku muṉṉuḷḷa tamiḻ nūlkaḷellām camaskirutattiliruntē vantaṉa eṉṟu cātittal eṅṅaṉam poruntum? camaskirutapāṣikaḷellām muḻutum tamiḻ vārttikaḷāl amintiruppataṟkut tirukkuṟaḷum cintāmaṇiyum iṅkē utāraṇamākak kāṭṭappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. ivaṟṟil vaḻaṅkivarum cila tamiḻ vārttikaḷic camaskiruta vārttikaḷeṉṟu collum pētikaḷumuṇṭu. tirukkuṟaḷ tamiḻ nāṭum tamiḻ vēntarum palavitattilum cīrkulintu-tēyntakālam ceyyappaṭṭateṉṟu nām aṟivōm. itilum mēlāṉavikaḷāka iyal ici nāṭakameṉṉum muttamiḻukkum eḻutiya nūlkaḷum nīti nūlkaḷum mutaṟ piraḷayattālum iraṇṭām piraḷayattālum aḻintupōyviṭṭaṉa. āriyarum camaṇarum tamiḻnāṭṭil kalantapiṉpum mūṉṟāvatu tamiḻc caṅkattiṉ iṟutiyilum tirukkuṟaḷ mutaliya tamiḻ nūlkaḷ veḷivantaṉa. ataṉ muṉṉuḷḷa tamiḻ moḻikaḷ mikacciṟantavakiyeṉṟum kalappaṟṟavi yeṉṟum ciṟanta ilakkaṇamuṭiyavi yeṉṟum tolkāppiyattāl aṟivōm. tamiḻ moḻiyiṉ toṉmiyik kavaṉittaṟinta evarum camaskirutattiṉiṉṟu tamiḻ vantateṉṟu collamāṭṭār.
3. tamiḻē tāyppāṣiyāyiruntatu eṉpatu.
tamiḻppāṣiyāṉatu vekukālaṅkaḷukku muṉṉālēyē mikac cīrtirutta maṭintirunta teṉpatum ilakkaṇaṅkaḷiyuṭiyatā yirunta teṉpatum piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇpōm.
Dravidian Comparative Grammar by Bishop Caldwell, P.29.
“No person who has any acquaintance with the principles of comparative philogy and who has carefully studied the Grammars and vocabularies of the Dravidian Languages, and compared them with those of the Sanskrit, can suppose that the grammatical structure and inflexional forms of those languages and the greater number of their more important roots are capable of being derived from the Sanskrit by any process of corruption whatsoever.”
“pāṣātattuva cāstiravitikaḷik koñcamāvatu aṟintu, tirāviṭa pāṣikaḷiṉ ilakkaṇaṅ kaḷiyum nikaṇṭu tivākaram piṅkalanti akarāti mutalāṉavikaḷiyum kavaṉamāyppaṭittu, avikaḷic camaskiruta pāṣiyiṉ amaram mutaliya nikaṇṭukaḷōṭum akarātikaḷōṭum oppiṭṭup pārtta evarum, antappāṣikaḷiṉ ilakkaṇavaṭivaṅkaḷum patarūpa pētaṅkaḷum vēṟṟumikaḷum avikaḷiṉ atika mukkiyamāṉa pakutikaḷil mikutiyāṉavikaḷum, camaskirutat tiṉiṉṟu maruviyēṉum allatu vēṟu evvitamāyākilum citintēṉum vantirukkumeṉṟu niṉikkamāṭṭārkaḷ.”
Dravidian Comparative Grammar by Bishop Caldwell, P.4
“This language (Tamil) being the earliest cultivated of all the Dravidian idioms, the most copious, and that which contins the largest portion and the richest variety of indubitably ancient forms, it is deservedly placed at the head of the list.”
“tirāviṭa pāṣikaḷellāvaṟṟilum tamiḻē atipūrvakālattilēyē cīrppaṭuttappaṭṭu virttiyaṭinta pāṣiyāyum vistāramāṉa pāṣi yāyum yātoru cantēkamumiṉṟip paṇṭiyuruccoṟkaḷi ērāḷamāyum niṟivāyumuṭiya pāṣiyāyumiruppatiṉālē, ati niyāyamāy mutal mutal vittirukkiṟaJ.”
“From the various particulars mentioned above, it appears certin that the Tamil language was of all the Dravidian idioms the earliest cultivated; it also appears highly probable, that in the endeavour to ascertin the characteristics of the primitive Dravidian speech, from which the various existing dialects have been derived, most assistance will be furnished by the Tamil.”
“mēlē kūṟiya paṟpala viṣayaṅkaḷiṉālum tirāviṭapāṣika ḷellā vaṟṟilum tamiḻppāṣiyē atikap pūrvīkamāṉateṉṟu niccayamāyt tōṉṟukiṟatu. taṟkālattilirukkum pala kiḷippāṣikaḷukku mūla pāṣiyāyirunta tirāviṭapāṣiyiṉ ātirūpatti aṟiya muyaṟci ceytāl, tamiḻē muṉṉiṉṟu atika utavi ceyyumeṉṟum tōṉṟukiṟatu.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷik kavaṉikkiyil, tirāviṭapāṣiyeṉṟu āriyar aḻikkum pāṣikaḷukkut tamiḻē tāyp pāṣiyeṉṟum, ciṟanta ilakkaṇamuṭiyateṉṟum, pūrvakālattatu eṉṟum, camaskirutattiṉiṉṟu muṟṟilum vēṟāṉa pāṣiyeṉṟum teḷivākat terikiṟatu. mēlum, tolkāppiyam mikap paḻamiyāṉateṉṟum, nuṭpamāṉa ilakkaṇa vitikaḷiyuṭiya teṉṟum, avar mēṟkōḷkaḷikkoṇṭu avarukku muntiya kālattilēyē tamiḻ vitvāṉkaḷiruntirukka vēṇṭumeṉpati aṟiyalāmeṉṟum piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Manual of the Administration of the Madras Presidency. Vol. I, P (56)
“Tamil literature is the oldest among the Dravidian languages. To the sage Agastiya (of unknown date) are attributed not only the formation of the alphabet and first treatise upon grammar, but also a number of treatises on various sciences. But nothing authentic survives from such an ancient time. The oldest extent Tamil grammar is called the “Tolguppiam’ that is to say ‘The ancient book’: Such a work must have been preceded by centuries of literary culture as it lays down rules for different kinds of poetical compositions, duduced from examples furnished by the best authors whose works were then in existence. Its date cannot, however, be fixed.”
“tirāviṭa pāṣikaḷil eḻutappaṭṭa nūlkaḷil atikap paḻami yāṉavi tamiḻppāṣiyil uḷḷavi. aṟiyamuṭiyāta atipūrvakālat tirunta akattiyamuṉi, tamiḻukku neṭuṅkaṇakku ēṟpaṭutti, mutal ilakkaṇa nūliyum vittiyam, cōtiṭam, ñāṉam mutaliya pala cāstira nūlkaḷiyum eḻutiṉatākac collappaṭukiṟatu. āṉāl avvaḷavu paḻami yāṉa kālattiliruntu niccayamāṉa yātoṉṟum namakku kiṭikkavilli. ippōtirukkiṟa paḻamiyāṉa tamiḻ ilakkaṇa nūlukkut “tolkāppiyam” (allatu āti pustakam) eṉṟu peyar. āṉāl antap pustakattil palavakippākkaḷ iyaṟṟuvataṟku vēṇṭiya palavitikaḷ nuṭpamāyc collappaṭṭiruppatāl atu eḻutappaṭṭa kālattukku ati muṉṉēyē jaṉaṅkaḷ nūl eḻutuvatil atika nākarīkamaṭintirukka vēṇṭumeṉpatu veḷiyākiṟatu. yāppiyalukku vēṇṭiya vitikaḷukku utāraṇaṅkaḷ akkālattukku muṉṉuḷḷa vitvāṉkaḷiṉ nūlkaḷiliruntu eṭukkap paṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉa. ākilum tolkāppiyam eḻutappaṭṭa kālatti niccayikka muṭiyāJ.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vākkiyaṅkaḷik kavaṉikkiyil, tamiḻppāṣikku eḻuttukkaḷ amittu ilakkaṇam vakuttu ilakkiyam ceytavar akastiyareṉṟu collukiṟārkaḷ. avarirunta kālam teriyavilli. āṉāl tolkāppiyar kālattiṟku muṉṉālēyē anēka vitvāṉkaḷirun tiruppatākat terikiṟateṉkiṟār. akattiyar kālattiṟku muṉpē tamiḻp pāṣiyiruntateṉṟum avarkaḷ tamiḻppāṣiyil ciṟanta nūlkaḷ ceytiruntārkaḷeṉṟum akattiyar tāmē colliyirukkiṟār. atu piṉvarum cūttiraṅkaḷāl terikiṟatu.
pērakattiyam
		“ilakkaṇa meṉpatilakkiya muṟiyuṟa
		vittateṉṟu vaḻaṅkappaṭumē.”
		“ilakkiya miṉṟi yilakkaṇamiṉṟē
		eḷḷiṉṟāki leṇṇeyumiṉṟē
		eḷḷiṉiṉṟeṇ ṇeyeṭuppatu pōla
		ilakkiyattiṉiṉ ṟeṭupaṭu milakkaṇam.”
itaṉāl akattiyarukku muṉṉālēyē tamiḻ mikat tērcci peṟṟiruntateṉṟu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. tiruviḷiyāṭaṟpurāṇamum itaṉi valiyuṟuttukiṟaJ. akkālattirunta tamiḻiṉ tērccikkut takuntapaṭi iyal ici nāṭakam eṉṉum muttamiḻukkum ilakkaṇam vakuttāreṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu.
		“vaṭavēṅkaṭan teṉkumariyāyiṭit
		tamiḻkūṟu nallulakattu
		vaḻakkuñ ceyyuḷumāyirumutaliṉ
		eḻuttuñ collum poruḷunāṭic
		centamiḻiyaṟki civaṇiyanilattoṭu
		muntunūlkaṇṭu muṟippaṭaveṇṇip
		pulantokuttōṉē. - - - -  ”
itil tolkāppiyar tamakku muṉṉuḷḷa tamiḻnūlkaḷi ārāycci ceytu, tamiḻ kūṟum nallulakattu vaḻakkum ceyyuḷumākiya iruvaḻiyilum vaḻaṅkum eḻuttu, col, poruḷkaḷukku ilakkaṇam coṉṉatākac collukiṟār. itikkoṇṭu tamiḻiṉ pūrvamum ataṉ pūraṇa niliyum teḷivāka aṟiyalām.
4. tamiḻpāṣiyiṉ vārttikaḷi varttaka campantamuḷḷa maṟṟa tēcattavar vaḻaṅkivantārkaḷeṉpaJ.
mēlum teṉṉintiyāvōṭu varttakañ ceyta kirēkkarum piṉīciyarum viyāpārap poruḷākak koṇṭupōṉa vastukkaḷiṉ peyarkaḷit taṅkaḷ pāṣiyil appaṭiyē vaḻaṅkivantārkaḷeṉṟu piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷāl terikiṟatu.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary
“It is sid that the Language of the Mountineers of Rajah Mahal abounds in terms identified with Tamil and Telugu. What is more singular, the names by which the ivory, apes, and peacocks, conveyed by Solomon’s ships of Tarshish were known, are the same with those still used in Tamil; seeming to imply that the traders visited Ceylon, or India, and obtined with these novelties their Tamil names, Danta, Kapi, and Togi, as found in the Hebrew Bible.”
“rājāmahāl malittēcavācikaḷ pāṣiyil, tamiḻ teluṅku moḻikaḷ ērāḷamāyiruk kiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu collappaṭukiṟatu. cālōmō ṉuṭiya tarṣīsiṉ kappalkaḷ koṇṭupōṉa tantam, kuraṅku, mayil mutalāṉavikaḷiṉ peyarkaḷik kavaṉikkiyil, avikaḷ taṟkālattilum tamiḻppāṣiyil vaḻaṅki varukiṟa peyarkaḷeṉṟum, aṉṉiya tēcatti liruntu viyāpārikaḷ ilaṅki intiyā mutalāṉaviṭaṅkaḷukku vantiruntārkaḷeṉṟum, apporuḷkaḷōṭu epirēya pipiḷil kāṇappaṭum tantam, kapi, tōki eṉṉum avikaḷiṉ tamiḻp peyarkaḷiyum kēṭṭaṟintu koṇṭu pōṉārkaḷeṉṟum teriyavarukiṟatu.”
mēṟkāṭṭiyapaṭi intiyāvil viyāpārañceyta kālam kiṟistuvukkuc caṟṟēṟakkuṟiya 1,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṟpaṭṭaJ. cumār 3,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ teṉṉintiyāvōṭu viyāpāra mūlamāyc campantappaṭṭavarkaḷ intiyāviṟku māttira muriyatāṉa viyāpārac carakkukaḷiṉ peyarkaḷit tamiḻilēyē colli vantirukkavēṇṭum. āṉāl varttakarkaḷ entac carakkukaḷi intiyāvil iṟakkumati paṇṇiṉārkaḷeṉṟu teriyavilli. intiyāviṟku nūtaṉamākat tōṉṟum kōlikaḷ pīṅkāṉkaḷ viḷiyāṭṭup poruḷkaḷ mutaliyavikaḷit tavira vēṟu eti iṟakkumati ceytiruppārkaḷ? ākiyiṉāl  kirēkku, epirēyu mutaliya aṉṉiyapāṣikaḷiṉ moḻikaḷ, tamiḻōṭu atikamāyk kalantirukkamāṭṭāveṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu.
Manual of the Administration of the Madras Presidency, Vol. I, P (48,)
“The Phoenicians were the first to adopt a purely alphabetic system. The general voice of antiquity gives them this credit and the facts agree with the rumour. The Indo-Arabian alphabet is held to represent the Himyarite of South Arabia and the alphabets of India as shown in the Asoka’s inscriptions. It will be seen later that this scheme does not provide for the original alphabet of the Dravidian nations, which remins thus unaffiliated in the same way as are the Dravidian languages themselves.”
“eḻuttukkaḷi mutalmutal upayōkittavarkaḷ piṉīciyar. pūrva nūlkaḷellām itippaṟṟic cāṭci koṭukkiṉṟaṉa. viṣayaṅkaḷum avikaḷukku ottirukkiṉṟaṉa. acōkaṉuṭiya cili eḻuttukkaḷip pārttāl, intiya arapiyaruṭiya eḻuttukkaḷellām teṉ arapiyāviluḷḷa himayāriṭi eḻuttukkaḷiṉiṉṟum intiyāviṉ eḻuttukkaḷiṉiṉṟum uṇṭāṉaviyeṉṟu terikiṟatu. inta muṟiyil tirāviṭaruṭiya eḻuttu muṟi uṟpattiyippaṟṟi yātoṉṟum collavilli. tirāviṭa pāṣikaḷ eppaṭi oṉṟōṭum cērāmal taṉimi yāy niṟkiṉṟaṉavō appaṭiyē tirāviṭa eḻuttukkaḷum niṟkiṉṟaṉa.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷil, āciyātturukkiyil mattiya tarikkaṭali yaṭutta piṉīciya nāṭṭilēyē mutalmutal eḻuttukkaḷ uṇṭāyiṉaveṉṟu collappaṭukiṟatu. acōkaṉuṭiya cili eḻuttu, teṉ arēpiyāviṉ cittira eḻuttukkaḷālum intiya eḻuttukkaḷālumuṇṭāṉa teṉṟu collukiṟār. āṉāl tirāviṭa pāṣiyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷ avikaḷōṭu cērāmal taṉittu niṟkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu colvati nām kavaṉikkavēṇṭum.
5. tirāviṭapāṣiyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷ piṉīciya pāṣiyiliruntum camaskiruta pāṣiyiliruntum uṇṭākavilliyeṉpaJ.
intiya pāṣiyiṉ aṭcaraṅkaḷ piṉīciya pāṣiyiliruntu uṇṭā yirukkamāṭṭāveṉṟu aṭiyil varum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Sanskrit-English Dictionary by Monier Williams, M.A., Preface, P. XVI.
“According to Mr. Edward Thomas (Prinsep’s Indian Antiquities, Vol. II. Page 42) the theory by which Professor Weber has sought to establish a Phoenician origin for the Indian alphabets is untenable. There are, however, two sets of Buddhist inscriptions, and that of Kapurdigiri is decidedly traceable to a Phoenician source. Those on the rock of Girnar (Giri-nagara) in Kathywar, Gujarat, which are sid to be most important in their relation to the present Indian alphabets, are not so clearly traceable. Mr. Thomas appears to have good ground for thinking that many of the Nagari letters were derived from the Dravidians of the south.”
“eṭvarṭ tāmas eṉpavar (intiya purātaṉaṅkaḷ 2-m pākam, pakkam 42.) collukiṟatāvatu intiya pāṣiyiṉ aṭcaram piṉīciya pāṣiyeḻutti liruntu vantateṉṟu uvepar paṇṭitar collukiṟatu niṟkakkūṭiya ātāramuṭiyatalla. eṉṟālum, iruvitamāṉa puttacilācācaṉaṅkaḷ irukkiṉṟaṉa. avaṟṟuḷ kappurṭikiriyiluḷḷa ciliyeḻuttukkaḷ piṉīciya akṣarattiliruntu uṇṭāyiruppatākat teriya varukiṟatu. kujarāttiluḷḷa kattiyavāril kirinakarā eṉṉum pāṟiyil kāṇappaṭum mika mukkiyamāṉaviyum taṟkāla intiya pāṣikaḷiṉ akṣaraṅkaḷukkuc campantamuṭiyaviyumāṉa eḻuttukkaḷ eṉṟu collappaṭupavi avvaḷavu teḷivāṉa oṟṟumiyillātavikaḷāyirukkiṉṟaṉa. nākari eḻuttukkaḷil anēkam teṉṉintiyāviluḷḷa tirāviṭa pāṣikaḷiliruntu vantateṉṟu tāmas turi ūkikkiṟataṟku ēṟṟa mukāntaraṅkaḷirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉak kūṟukiṟār.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷil cilācācaṉaṅkaḷikkoṇṭu intiyapāṣi piṉīciyapāṣikku avvaḷavu  ottirukkavilliyeṉṟu collukiṟatākak kāṇkiṟōm. ākiyiṉāl intiya pāṣi piṉīciya pāṣiyi liruntuṇṭāṉatalla veṉpatu terikiṟatu. tamiḻppāṣi camaskiruta pāṣiyiliruntuṇṭāṉa pāṣiyalla. ataṉ ilakkaṇam camaskiruta pāṣiyiṉ ilakkaṇattiṟku vittiyācamuṭiyatā yirukkiṟateṉṟu aṭiyilvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil pārppōm.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary.
“Unlike several of the vernaculars of India, it is not, as some have supposed, a dughter of the Sanskrit. Its Alphabet differs not only in character, but in sound; and is more limited. Its grammar, though conformed to the Sanskrit, as far as the genius of the Language would allow, is still very different. It has not article, no relative pronoun, no dual number, no optative mood. It differs in its numerals, in many nouns, and adverbs, and in technical terms in grammar. In the declension of its nouns, the conjugation of its verbs, and the arrangement of its sentences, it more resembles the Latin.”
“cilar niṉikkiṟapaṭi, intiyāvil vaḻaṅkivarum anēka cutēca pāṣikaḷippōl tamiḻppāṣi camaskirutattiliruntu uṇṭāṉatalla. atiṉ akṣaram vaṭivattil māttiramalla, ōciyilum vittiyācappaṭukiṟaJ. curukkamāyumirukkiṟaJ. atiṉ ilakkaṇam, pāṣip pōkku iṭaṅ koṭukkiṟa maṭṭukkum camaskiruta pāṣiyiṉ ilakkaṇatti aṉucarittuc ceyyappaṭṭi runtālum, perum vitkkiṟaJ. ya, yan, vāna eṉṉum (yacaviuṭanaḷa) pōṉṟa kuṟippiṭic coṟkaḷāvatu piratip peyarc coṟkaḷāvatu tuvivacaṉam eṉṉum irumikāṭṭum coṟkaḷāvatu atil kiṭiyātu. eṇṇuccoṟkaḷilum, anēka peyarccoṟkaḷilum, viṉic coṟkaḷilum, viṉiyuriccoṟkaḷilum, ilakkaṇa paripāṣiccoṟkaḷilum vittiyāca muṭiyatāyirukkiṟatu. peyarccoṟkaḷ viṉiccoṟkaḷiṉ rūpa pētaṅkaḷilum, vākkiya amippilum atu lattīṉ pāṣiyi ottirukkiṟaJ.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷil, camaskiruta pāṣiyiyum tamiḻp pāṣiyiyum ilakkaṇattil oppiṭṭukkāṭṭi tamiḻ lattīṉ pāṣikku ottirukkiṟateṉṟum ataṉ coṟkaḷum eḻuttukkaḷum curukkamāṉavi yeṉṟum ōciyilum vaṭivattilum camaskirutattiṟku vittiyācamāṉavi yeṉṟum collukiṟār. ciṟu kuḻantiyiṉ vāyiṉāl colvataṟkēṟṟa mataḷic (maḻali) coṟkaḷum, tērnta vitvāṉkaḷ collat takunta uyarnta vārttikaḷum cātāraṇa jaṉaṅkaḷ taṅkaḷ karuttip piṟarukkut terivikkap pōtumāṉa eḷitāṉa vārttikaḷumuṭiyatu tamiḻppāṣiyē. yāvarum mikacculapamāy uccarikkavum uccarittatiṉpaṭi eḻutavum eḻutiya eḻuttukkaḷ yāvum tirumpa uccarikkakkūṭiyatākavum viḷaṅki niṟpatu tamiḻē. camaskirutapāṣiyiṉ vārttikaḷ kalavāmal tamiḻ taṉiyāy pēcap paṭakkūṭiya pāṣiyeṉṟu aṭiyilvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷilkāṇalām.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary.
“It is evident that there was an early literature in Tamil independent of Sanskrit; it is certin that Tamil could do without Sanskrit much better than English without Latin. *  *  *  *  *
The reason why Tamil is more independent of Sanskrit than the Northern Languages, and even than the other Dravidian tongues, is, that it has not been left, like those, principally to the cultivation of the Brahmans.”
“pūrvatamiḻnūlkaḷ camaskirutak kalappillāmalirukkiṉṟaṉa. lattīṉ kalavāmal iṅkilīṣi eḻutakkūṭiyatippārkkilum atika naṉṟāyc camaskirutam kalavāmal tamiḻp pāṣiyi yeḻutalām. *  *  *  *  *
tamiḻil camaskiruta pāṣi atikamāyk kalavātiruppataṟkuk kāraṇameṉṉaveṉṟāl vaṭapāṣikaḷippōlum, maṟṟattirāviṭa pāṣikaḷip pōlum, pirāmaṇa nūlāciriyarkaḷ anēkar tamiḻppāṣik killāmaṟpōṉatē.”
mutal iraṇṭu caṅkaṅkaḷilumirunta ici nūlkaḷum nāṭakanūlkaḷum aḻintu kuṟivu paṭṭa kālattil teṉṉintiyāviṉ caṅkītatti pūraṇamāykkaṟṟum ataṟkuriya cila nūlkaḷ eḻutiyum taṟkālam variyum nīṭittirukkum nilikkup perumpālum āriya vitvacirōmaṇikaḷ kāraṇamāyiruntārkaḷeṉṟu collavum naṉṟipārāṭṭavum tamiḻmakkaḷ maṟantu pōkakkūṭāJ.    
6. tamiḻ pala ciṟanta kalikaḷiyuṭiya pāṣi eṉpatu.
tamiḻppāṣi mika nērttiyāṉa pataṅkaḷuṭiya pāṣiyeṉṟum, pūrvīka nūlkaḷiyum pulavarkaḷiyumuṭiyateṉṟum, piṉvarum vacaṉattil nām kāṇpōm.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary.
“A native author of repute, well versed in English, as well as his own vernacular, has sid, adopting the words of Mr. Taylor before mentioned ‘it is one of the most copious, refined, and polished languages spoken by man’. This author has added, what may admit of doubt, ‘few nations on earth can perhaps boast of so many poets as the Tamils’. As, however, all their carlier literature was in poetry, even Dictionaries and Grammars, and works on Medicine, Law, Architecture and Theology, the Number of poets, so called, must have been great.”
“āṅkilēya pāṣiyit tīrakkaṟṟavarum, tammuṭiya cuyapāṣiyiṉ tiṟami peṟṟavarumāṉa ōr pērpōṉa cutēca nūlāciriyar, misṭar ṭēylar colvataṟku oṟṟumiyāka, maṉitar pēcum pāṣikaḷuḷ tamiḻppāṣi anēka apippirāyaṅkaḷit terivikkakkūṭiyatum, mika nērttiyum ērāḷamumāṉa coṟkaḷiyuṭiyatumāṉa pāṣiyeṉṟu terivikkiṟār. piṉṉum avar tamiḻp pāṣiyil tērnta pulavarkaḷ ērāḷamāyiruppatu pōl ulakattil maṟṟa entappāṣiyilumilli eṉṟu kūṟukiṟār. avarkaḷiṉ pūrvīka kāviyaṅkaḷ, akarātikaḷ, ilakkaṇa nūlkaḷ, vittiya cāstiraṅkaḷ, caṭṭacāstiraṅkaḷ ciṟpacāstiraṅkaḷ, vētacāstiraṅkaḷ mutalāṉavikaḷellām ceyyuḷilēyēyiruppatāl, avikaḷiṉ nūlāciriyarkaḷākiya pulavarkaḷum ērāḷam.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷik kavaṉikkiyil, oru pāṣikkum oru tēcattukkumuriya kalikaḷ yāvum tamiḻiliruntatākak kāṇkiṟōm. aṟupattunālu kalikaḷiyum ovvoṉṟum oru tiruviḷiyāṭṭākap paramacivaṉ naṭattikkāṭṭiyatu tamiḻ nāṭṭilallavō. ākiyiṉāl vēṇṭum kalikaḷ yāvum pūrva tamiḻ nāṭṭiliruntaṉaveṉṟum piṉ aḻintupōyiṉaveṉṟum nām niṉikkavēṇṭum.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary.
“It is not perhaps extravagant to say, that, in its poetic form, the Tamil is more polished and exact than the Greek, and in both dialects, with its borrowed treasures, more copious than the Latin. In its fulness and power it more resembles English and German than any other living language. Its prose style is yet in a forming state, and will well repay the labor of accurate scholars in moulding it properly. Many natives who write poetry readily, cannot write a page of correct prose.”
“tamiḻp pāṣiyāvatu ceyyuḷ naṭiyil kirēkka pāṣiyip pārkkilum atika paḷapaḷappum tiruttamum poruntiyatāy, karuttukkaḷi atikattiṭṭamāyk kāṭṭakkūṭiyatāyirukkiṟateṉṟu colvatu ati maṭṭukku (aḷavukku) miñcippukaḻvatākāJ. atu maṟṟap pāṣikaḷiliruntu cērttukkoṇṭa moḻikaḷuḷpaṭa ceyyuḷ naṭiyilum vācaka naṭiyilum ataṟkirukkum moḻikaḷ lattīṉ moḻikaḷiviṭa atika ērāḷamām. ataṉ niṟivilum pelattilum taṟkālattilirukkum maṟṟentap pāṣiyiyum viṭa iṅkilīṣ jarmaṉ pāṣikaḷi ottirukkiṟaJ. ataṟku vacaṉa naṭi paṭippaṭiyāy ēṟpaṭṭukkoṇṭu varukiṟatu: atic cariyāṉa naṭiyil oḻuṅkupaṭutta muyalukiṟavarkaḷ taṅkaḷ pirayācattiṉ palaṉiyaṭiyāmaṟ pōkamāṭṭārkaḷ. coṉṉavuṭaṉē kavi yeḻutakkūṭiya anēka tamiḻarkaḷ piḻiyillāmal vacaṉam oru pakkaṅkūṭa eḻuta muṭiyātavarkaḷā yirukkiṟārkaḷ.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷil tamiḻppāṣiyāṉatu āṅkilēya jermāṉiya pāṣikaḷippōlap pūraṇamum ciṟappum peṟṟu pēccu vaḻakkuṭiya uyirppāṣiyāy viḷaṅkukiṟa teṉkiṟār.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary
“The Tamil is not a vulgar dialect. Before the principal basis of the English had a written character, it was a highly polished language. Its name signifies sweetness, and though not so musical as the Telugu, in its poetic form especially, it is not without its clim to cuphonic charms, and ‘linked sweetness.”
“tamiḻppāṣi kocciyāṉa pāṣiyalla. āṅkilēya pāṣikku eḻuttu vaṭivam ēṟpaṭuvataṟku muṉṉamē tamiḻ paḷapaḷappum maḻamaḻappum mikuntu tērnta ciṟanta pāṣiyāyiruntaJ. tamiḻ eṉpataṟku maturam eṉṟu arttamām. teluṅkippōl avvaḷavu iṉiya ōci yuḷḷatalla veṉṟālum kēṭpavarkaḷukku ānantatti viḷivippatilum toṭarcci yāṉa iṉimi payappatilum tamiḻppāṣikkuc caktiyillāmaṟ pōka villi.”
mēṟkaṇṭa varikaḷi nām kavaṉikkiyil, iṅkilīṣpāṣikku eḻuttukkaḷuṇṭāvataṟku muṉṉālēyē tamiḻppāṣi ciṟantu viḷaṅkiṉateṉṟu terikiṟatu.
7. camaskirutapāṣi yuṇṭāṉatippaṟṟiya cila kuṟippukaḷ.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary.
“In the opinion of the Rev. William Taylor, the able Editor of Dr. Rottler’s Dictionary, ‘there was originally one simple homogeneous dialect, spoken by rude, simple aborigines from the Himalaya to Cape Comorin ‘ Mr. Taylor thinks that, ‘the earliest probable refinement of it was the Pali of the North, and the Tamil of the extreme South,’ and that, ‘the Sanscrit assumed its own form by engrafting numerous Chaldic terms of science and others of common use in the old Pali.’ It is evident from their names, that the Pali must have been anterior to the Sanscrit, the former signifying root or original, and the latter finished or polished. It is stated by Colonel Sykes that very ancient inscriptions on rocks and coins, are found in Pali and Pracrit four hundred years earlier than in Sanscrit.”
“rāṭlar akarātiyip piracurañceytavarāṉa villiyam ṭēylar eṉpavar, ātiyil imaya parvata mutal kaṉṉiyākumarimuṉivari, nākarīkamillātavarkaḷum pētamiyuḷḷavarkaḷumāṉa pūrvīkak kuṭikaḷukkuḷ yātoru kalappillāta orēpāṣi pēcappaṭṭu vantateṉṟum, atu mutaṟ cīrtiruttatti yaṭintu vaṭa tēcattil pāliyākavum teṉkōṭiyil tamiḻākavum vaḻaṅkiyateṉṟum, paḻiya pālipāṣiyil ērāḷamāṉa kaltēya cāstira campantamāṉa paripāṣikaḷiyum cātāraṇa vaḻakkatti luḷḷa anēka moḻikaḷiyum cērttukkoṇṭu camaskirutapāṣiyi yuṇṭākkiṉār kaḷeṉṟum apippirāyappaṭukiṟār. intap pāṣikaḷiṉ pērkaḷik kavaṉikkiyil pālipāṣi camaskirutattiṟku muntiṉateṉṟu teḷivākiṟaJ. ‘pāli’ eṉṟāl mūlam allatu muntiṉatu eṉṟum, ‘camaskirutam’ eṉṟāl tiruttam peṟṟatu allatu cemmiyākkappaṭṭatu eṉṟum arttam. kaṟpāṟikaḷilum nāṇayaṅkaḷilumuḷḷa atipūrvīka cāsaṉaṅkaḷil pāliyilum pirākirutattilum eḻutappaṭṭavikaḷ camas kirutattil eḻutappaṭṭavikaḷukku 400 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muntiṉavikaḷā yirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu karṉel ciks kūṟukiṟār.”
mēṟkaṇṭa varikaḷi nām kavaṉikkiyil, intiyāviṉ pūrvattōr imayamali mutal kaṉṉiyākumari variyum kuṭiyiruntārkaḷeṉṟum, avarkaḷuḷ yātoru kalappumaṟṟa orē pāṣi pēcappaṭṭu vantateṉṟum, appāṣi vaṭapākattil pāliyeṉṟum teṉpākattil tamiḻ eṉṟum collappaṭṭateṉṟum collukiṟār. pāli pāṣiyiliruntum aṉēka kaltēya pataṅkaḷiṉiṉṟum kalantu eṭukkappaṭṭu camaskirutameṉṟa oru pāṣiyuṇṭāṉateṉṟu collukiṟār. pūrvīkattil kallil eḻutappaṭṭa eḻuttukkaḷikkoṇṭum nāṇayaṅkaḷiluḷḷa eḻuttukkaḷikoṇṭum pāli pāṣiyē pūrvamāṉateṉṟu terikiṟatu. pālipāṣiyippōla pirākiruta pāṣikaḷum camaskirutattiviṭa 400-varuṣaṅkaḷukku muntiṉatu eṉṟum pālipāṣiyi ātimūlapāṣi yeṉṟum camaskirutam atiliruntu eṭukkappaṭṭu muṭivu peṟṟirukkiṟa pāṣiyeṉṟum collukiṟār. itikkoṇṭu pāli pāṣiyiliruntum pirākiruta pāṣiyiliruntum naṉṟāyuṇṭākkappaṭṭa pāṣi camaskiruta meṉṟu terikiṟatu. camaskirutam eṉṟa peyarukkē ‘naṉṟāyc ceyyappaṭṭatu’ eṉṟu arttamām. pāli eṉpatu mūlameṉṟum pirākirutiyeṉpatu mutal eṉṟum arttamām. ivvārttikaḷiṉ arttaṅkaḷik kavaṉikkiyil, āti yākavum mutalākavumuḷḷa pala pāṣikaḷiliruntu moḻikaḷ cērkkappaṭṭu ataṟkiṇaṅka ilakkaṇamuñ ceyyappaṭṭu naṉṟāyt tiruttappaṭṭa pāṣi camaskirutameṉṟu viḷaṅkukiṟaJ. āriyarkaḷ tāṅkaḷ pirayāṇam paṇṇivanta tēcaṅkaḷiluḷḷa pāṣiyiṉ palamoḻikaḷiyum oṉṟu cērttu, camaskirutameṉṟa putuppāṣi oṉṟu ceytārkaḷeṉṟu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. appaṭiyē kirēkku, lattīṉ, epirēyu, cīttiyam, pāli, pirākirutam, tamiḻ mutaliya pāṣikaḷ itil kalantirukkiṟatit taṟkāla aṉupōkattāl kāṇkiṟōm. mēlum iṭukuṟippeyarkaḷē āti vārttikaḷā yirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟum ataṉ piṉpē kāraṇappeyarkaḷ vaḻaṅkakkūṭiyateṉṟum aṟivāḷikaḷ yāvarum oppukkoḷvārkaḷ. caṅkīta cāstirattilum pūrva tamiḻ nūlkaḷ vaḻaṅkivanta iyaṟkiyāṉa moḻikaḷ niṟka, ovvoṉṟiṟkum kāraṇappeyarkaḷ amittu vaḻaṅki varuvatik kavaṉittāl, camaskirutattiluḷḷa caṅkīta cāstiraṅkaḷ yāvum piṟkālattiluṇṭāṉaviyeṉṟē niṉikka ētuvirukkiṟatu.
8. epirēya cīttiya airōppiya camaskirutapāṣikaḷil tamiḻ moḻikaḷ kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa eṉpataṟkuc cila tiruṣṭāntam.
pāṣikaḷil ciṟantatākac collappaṭum camaskiruta pāṣiyum cila vārttikaḷit tamiḻppāṣiyiliruntu eṭuttiruppatākap piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary.
“While nearly all the vernaculars of India have been greatly enriched from the Sanscrit, that wonderful language has condescended to borrow even from the Dravidian group, of which the Tamil is the oldest, and the principal. Dr. Caldwell in his learned Dravidian Comparative Grammar, instances 31 words in Sanscrit taken from Dravidian tongues, and 25 borrowed by both from some common source. He is of opinion that the Sanscrit derived its cerebral consonants from the Dravidian.”
“intiyāviluḷḷa maṟṟellāppāṣikaḷum kaṭaṉvāṅkikkoḷḷum paṭiyāka avvaḷavu ciṟanta tāy camaskirutamiruntālum, atuvum tirāviṭa pāṣiyiliruntu kaṭaṉ vāṅkiyirukkiṟatu. tirāviṭa pāṣikaḷil atikap pūrvīkamāṉatum mukkiyamāṉatum tamiḻtāṉ. ṭākṭar kālṭuvel tammuṭiya ciṟanta tirāviṭapāṣi ilakkaṇattil, tirāviṭapāṣiyi liruntu camaskirutattil cērkkap paṭṭirukkiṟataṟku utāraṇamāka 31 vārttikaḷum, camaskirutamum tirāviṭamum vēṟoru potuppāṣiyi liruntu kaṭaṉ vāṅkiyiruppataṟku utāraṇamāka 25 vārttikaḷum kāṭṭiyirukkiṟār. camaskirutapāṣiyāṉatu neñciliruntu varakkūṭiya cila meyyeḻuttukkaḷi (ṭa ṇa ḷa ra) tirāviṭa pāṣiyiliruntu cērttukkoṇṭatāka apippirāyappaṭukiṟār.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷil pala tamiḻ moḻikaḷ camaskiruta pāṣiyil cērntiruppatākavum camaskirutattiṟkum tamiḻukkum potuvāṉa vēṟu pala vārttikaḷ camaskirutattil varuvatākavum tamiḻukkuriya ṭa ṇa ḷa ra eṉṟa eḻuttukkaḷ tamiḻiliruntu camaskirutat tiṟku eṭuttuk koḷḷap paṭṭaṉaveṉṟum collukiṟār. mēlum, oru kālattil oru iṭattiliruntē jātikaḷ yāvarum taṉittaṉi pirintupōyirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟum, avarkaḷ pirintu pōkumuṉ tamiḻp pāṣiyiyē pēcivantārkaḷeṉṟum, pala kāraṇaṅkaḷikkoṇṭu ūkikka iṭamirukkiṟatu. ulakaccarittiram uṇṭāvataṟku muṉṉālēyē ippērppaṭṭa oru perum pirivu uṇṭāyirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟum tōṉṟukiṟatu. appaṭippirintupōṉavarkaḷ tāṅkaḷ pūrvamāyp pēcivanta pāṣiyiyē arikuṟiyāyp pēciyirukkavēṇṭum. ēṉeṉṟāl, jalappiraḷayattiṉāl aḻikkappaṭum jaṉaṅkaḷil, aṉēka aṟivāḷikaḷum celvavāṉkaḷum tappittukkoḷḷat tiṟamiyaṟṟirukka, cātāraṇa kūli vēliceykiṟavaṉum nīntat terintavaṉumē tappip piḻikka muṭiyum. cātāraṇa maṉitaṉukku 200, 300 vārttikaḷ terintirukkavēṇṭumāṉāl maṟṟoruvaṉ kūṭṭuṟavum vēṇṭum. illiyāṉāl maṟantupōm. maṟantapiṉ taṅkaḷukkuppiriyamāṉatum lēcāṉatumāṉa nūtaṉa vārttikaḷik koṇṭē ulakakkāriyaṅkaḷ pēccuppaḻakkattiṟku varavēṇṭum. ippaṭi vekukālam ceṉṟu viṭṭāl, taṅkaḷ pūrva vārttikaḷ vekutūram māṟi, mutal eḻuttu nīṇṭum kuṟukiyum iṉa eḻuttu vantum vaḻaṅkum. appaṭiyē kaṭi eḻuttukkeṭṭum nīṇṭum kuṟukiyum vēṟoru eḻuttu vantu viraviyum vaḻaṅkum. ippaṭip pala pāṣikaḷilum vaḻaṅkum vārttikaḷi oruṅku cērttup pārppōmāṉāl, 8,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉirunta tolkāppiyar kālattil 4,400 varuṣaṅkaḷāka irunta mutaṟcaṅkattār ātarittu vanta tamiḻppāṣiyē pūrvamāṉa pāṣiyeṉṟum, avarkaḷirunta lemūriyā aḻintapiṉ taṅkaḷukku etirppaṭṭa karikaḷukkut tamiḻar kuṭiyēṟiṉārkaḷeṉṟum, tiṭṭamākac collalām. avarkaḷ citaṟippōṉa piṉ tēca cītōṣṇa nilikkut takuntapaṭiyum ākārātikaḷukkut takuntapaṭiyum kittoḻilukkum kalvikkum takuntapaṭiyum vevvēṟu pāṣikkārarākavum tēcattārākavum aḻikkappaṭṭārkaḷ eṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu. mēṟpaṭi jātiyār pēcum pāṣikaḷil tamiḻ vārttikaḷ kāṇappaṭuvatē ataṟkup pōtumāṉa attāṭci. camaskiruta pāṣiyil anēka vārttikaḷum cila eḻuttukkaḷum tamiḻiliruntu eṭukkappaṭṭu vaḻakkattilirukkiṉṟaṉa.
Preface to Winslow’s Dictionary.
“But from affinities traced out by him, in addition to those hereafter given, it would seen that we may go farther back for many roots and forms in these tongues, to some common fountin both for them and for the languages of the Indo-European family, including Sanscrit; nearer to the time when ‘the whole earth was of one language.’ He specifies 85 words in the Dravidian, as having Seythian affinities, 31 as Semitic, and 106 connectedwith the west Indo European family, distinct from those in Sanscrit..”
“avarāl kāṭṭappaṭṭirukkiṟa oṟṟumikaḷiliruntum iṉik kūṟappaṭupavikaḷiliruntum intap pāṣikaḷiluḷḷa anēka mutaṉili kaḷukkākavum patarūpaṅkaḷukkākavum avikaḷukkum camaskiruta muḷpaṭṭa intu airōppiya pāṣiyiṉaṅkaḷukkum potuvāṉa ūṟṟuvarikkum vekutūram pōkalāmeṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu. atāvatu, pūmiyeṅkum orē pāṣiyiruntakālam varikkum eṭṭippārkkalām eṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu. cīttiya campantamuḷḷa 85 coṟkaḷiyum cēm vamicattāriṉ pāṣikkuc campantamuḷḷa 31 coṟkaḷiyum camaskirutattukku vēṟākiya mēli intu airōppiya iṉatticcērnta 106 coṟkaḷiyum eṭuttuk kāṭṭukiṟār.”
Comparative Grammar By Bishop Caldwell P. 453.
“Some of the words which are contined in the following list, have Sanskrit as well as Classical or West Aryan analogies; but they have been placed in this, rather than in the preceding, list, becuse the West Aryan affinities are clearer, more direct, and more certin than the Sanskrit ones. The greater number, however, of the words that follow, though indubitably connected with the western tongues, and especially with the Greek and Latin, exhibit no analogy whatever to any words contined in the Sanskrit. If the existence of this class of analogies can be clearly established, it must be concluded either that the Dravidians were at an early period near neighbours of the West Aryan tribes, subsequently to the separation of their tribes from the Sanskrit speaking people, or that both races were descended from a common source. The majority of the Dravidian words which exhibit West Aryan analogies, do not belong to that primary, rudimental class to which the words that the Dravidian languages have in common with the Scythian are to be referred. Nevertheless, they are so numerous, many of them are so remarkable, and when all are viewed together, the analogy which they bring to light is so distinct, that an ultimate relation of some kind between the Dravidian and the Indo-European familes, may be regarded as conclusively established.”
“piṉ collappaṭum aṭṭavaṇiyil uḷḷa cila vārttikaḷ camaskirutattōṭum mēṟṟici āriya allatu ciṟantavilakkiya pāṣika ḷōṭum campantappaṭṭavikaḷāyirukkiṉṟaṉa. āṉāl avikaḷic camaskiruta aṭṭavaṇiyil cērkkāmal mēṟku āriya pāṣikaḷōṭu vittaṟkuk kāraṇam eṉṉaveṉṟāl, antappāṣikaḷōṭu cērnta campantaṅkaḷ camaskiruta pāṣic campantaṅkaḷiviṭa atikat teḷi vāṉavi yāyum atika neruṅkiṉaviyāyum atika niccayamāṉavi yāyum irukkiṉṟaṉa. piṉvarum vārttikaḷilaṉēkam mukkiyamāṉa āriya pāṣikaḷākiya kirēkku lattīṉ pāṣikaḷōṭu cantēkamiṉṟic campantap paṭṭavikaḷāyiruntum camaskiruta pāṣiyōṭu entavitattilum campantappaṭṭiruppatākat teriyavilli. ivvita vakuppāṉa campantaṅkaḷ iruppatiṉ niccayatti nām stāpikkakkūṭumāṉāl, tirāviṭar ātiyil mēṟku āriya jātiyārōṭu neruṅki vācam ceytārkaḷeṉṟāvatu atāvatu camaskirutam pēcum jātiyāriviṭṭuppirinta piṟaku appaṭi vacittārkaḷeṉṟāvatu allatu irujātiyārum orē potuvāṉa jātiyiliruntu uṟpattiyāṉārkaḷeṉṟāvatu niccayamāyc colla ētuvuṇṭu. mēli yāriya campantattik kāṇpikkum tirāviṭa vārttikaḷ perumpālum tirāviṭa pāṣikaḷukkum cīttiya pāṣikaḷukkum potuvāyuḷḷa āti pūrva pāṣikkuc contamāṉavi yalla veṉṟālum, avikaḷ ērāḷamāyiruppatālum avikaḷil anēkam vicēṣittavikaḷāyiruppatālum, avikaḷ ellāvaṟṟiyum oṉṟu cērttuk kavaṉikkiyil avikaḷukkuḷḷa campantam veku teḷivāyt terivatālum, tirāviṭapāṣikaḷukkum allatu intu airōppiya pāṣikaḷukkum ētō oruvitamāṉa campantam iruntirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟu niccayamāy stāpikkalām.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉattiṉāl pāṣi campantamāṉa oṟṟumiyik kavaṉittāl maṉitar ātiyil orē potuvāṉa jātiyiliruntu uṟpattiyāṉārkaḷeṉṟum tamiḻp pāṣiyōṭu maṟṟellāp pāṣikaḷukkum campantamiruppatiṉāl avarkaḷ ātiyil orupāṣi pēciyirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟum niccayikka etuvirukkiṟateṉpatip piṉvarum vacaṉattil kāṇalām.

mēlē kāṭṭiya aintu aṭṭavaṇikaḷil kaṇṭa vārttikaḷik kavaṉikkiyil, avikaḷ ovvoṉṟum tamiḻ nāṭṭil cātāraṇamāy kalviyaṟivillāta ēḻi jaṉaṅkaḷum nāḷatuvari pēcikkoṇṭirukkiṟa vārttikaḷā yirukkiṟatēyoḻiya uyarnta vārttikaḷāyilli. ivikaḷē piṟanāṭṭukkuk koṇṭupōkap paṭṭirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟum avviṭattil pala tiripukaḷiyum vikāraṅkaḷiyumaṭintu vittiyācap paṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉa veṉṟum nām niṉikka vēṇṭum. iṅkē kaṇṭa vārttikaḷi mutalāka vittukkoṇṭu vikuti, iṭinili, cāriyi, canti, vikāram, vēṟṟumi urupukaḷ, aṭimoḻi mutaliyavi cērntu ettaṉiyō vārttikaḷuṇṭāki nāḷatu variyum vaḻakkattiliruntu varukiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu tamiḻ makkaḷ naṉṟāy aṟivārkaḷ. itaṉāl mēṟkāṭṭiya aṭṭavaṇiyil kaṇṭa vārttikaḷ tamiḻ vārttikaḷē yeṉṟu niccayamākac collalām. mēlum tamiḻppāṣikkum camaskiruta pāṣikkum potuvāṉa vēṟoru pāṣiyiliruntu vaḻaṅkivarum vārttikaḷ 25 eṉṟu colvati nām kavaṉikkiyil avvārttikaḷ pūrvantoṭuttu tamiḻpāṣiyilē vaḻaṅki varukiṟa vārttika ḷeṉpatiyum pūrvanūlākiya tolkāppiyattil collappaṭum vārtti kaḷākavē kāṇappaṭukiṟateṉpatiyum aṟivāḷikaḷ aṟivārkaḷ. mēṟkāṭṭiya 25 vārttikaḷum tamiḻ vārttikaḷeṉpataṟkuc cantēka milli.
mēṟkaṇṭa cila tamiḻmoḻikaḷum iṉṉum pala moḻikaḷum pala māṟutalkaḷuṭaṉ anēka pāṣikaḷil kalantirukkiṟateṉpati iṉṉum pūraṇamāy vicārippōmāṉāl, cātāraṇamāyt tamiḻil vaḻaṅkum vārttikaḷē maṟṟavarkaḷ pāṣikku ātiyāyiruntirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟu nām eṇṇa iṭantarum.
(a) tamiḻ moḻikaḷ palavāṟākat tirintu maṟṟa pāṣikaḷil vaḻaṅki varuvatik kāṭṭum cila utāraṇaṅkaḷ.
iyaṟkiyiṉ amippil jīvappirāṇikaḷiṉiṭam viḷaṅki niṟkum cila ōcikaḷ tamiḻppāṣiyiṉ cila eḻuttukkaḷiṉ ōciyiyum cila vārttikaḷiṉ ōciyiyum otta tāyirukkiṉṟaṉa. ammā eṉṟa pacuṅkaṉṟiṉ ōciyi yaṟiyāta intukkaḷ oruvarumilli. appaṭiyē akkā, akkō eṉṟa kuyiliṉ allatu akkāpaṭciyiṉ ōciyumirukkiṟaJ. kā, ki, ku, kū, cu, ī, ū, mā, mē, ñā mutaliya eḻuttukkaḷi mutaṟkoṇṭu toṉikkum jīvappirāṇikaḷiṉ cattattiyum nām kēṭṭiruppōm. ivvōciyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷukku iṇaṅka vaḻaṅkivarum vārttikaḷi nām kavaṉittāl maṉuṭa tōṟṟattiṉ ātikālam ivvārttikaḷiyum ōcikaḷiyu muṭiyatākavē yiruntateṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu. entap pāṣiyilum, kēṭkavum kaṟṟukkoḷḷavuṅkūṭāta ciṟu pirāyattil pacuṅkaṉṟiṉ ōciyi otta mā, mmā, ammā eṉṟa vārttikaḷic colluvati nām kāṇalām. ivvārtti nāviṉālum utaṭṭiṉālum uccarippataṟku veku culapamāṉa muntiya vārttiyāyirukkiṟatu. itaṉ piṉ utaṭukaḷiṉālum nāviṉālum veku culapamāy uccarikkakkūṭiya appā, akkā, atti, māmā, tāttā, pāppā mutaliya vārttikaḷ paṭippaṭiyāy paḻakkattiṟku varukiṉṟaṉa. ivvārttikaḷi nām kūrntu kavaṉippō māṉāl akāram eṉṟa mutal eḻuttum ammā eṉṟa pacuṅkaṉṟiṉ ōciyum akkā eṉṟa paṭciyiṉ ōciyum mutal mutal vaḻaṅkivantirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟu niṉikka iṭamirukkiṟatu.
1.	ammā eṉṟa tamiḻmoḻi, camaskirutattil ampā, ammā eṉṟum, hi jermaṉ pāṣiyil ammā eṉṟum, āskaṉ pāṣiyil ammā eṉṟum, aislāṇṭu pāṣiyil ammā - pāṭṭi eṉṟum, jermaṉ pāṣiyil ammē = Nurse eṉṟum, cāmāyṭi pāṣiyil ammā eṉṟum, jeṉici pāṣiyil am, ammā eṉṟum, esriyaṉ pāṣiyil emmā eṉṟum, piṉīciya pāṣiyil emā eṉṟum, cintu pāṣiyil amā eṉṟum, maliya pāṣiyil ama eṉṟum, tūlu pāṣiyil ammē (mother),, appē (father) eṉṟum, maṅkōliya pāṣiyil amā, fatherca eṉṟum, tipēttu pāṣiyil mā, mō stirī eṉṟum, epirēya pāṣiyil ēm, im eṉṟum, ciriyāk pāṣiyil āmō eṉṟum vaḻaṅki varukiṟatu.
2.	appā eṉṟu collukiṟa tamiḻ vārttiyi kavaṉippō māṉāl teluṅku kaṉṉaṭaṅkaḷil appā eṉṟum, pūttāṉ pāṣiyil appā eṉṟum, pōttiyā pāṣiyil apā eṉṟum, epirēyu pāṣiyil āp eṉṟum, kaltēya pāṣiyil appā eṉṟum, cīriyā pāṣiyil āpō eṉṟum, āramēk pāṣiyil appā eṉṟum, ciṅkaḷa pāṣiyil appā eṉṟum collappaṭukiṟatu.
3.	akkāḷ eṉṟa tamiḻ vārtti camaskirutattil akkā = tāy eṉṟum, kaṉṉaṭam teluṅkil akkā=mūtta cakōtari eṉṟum, makārāṣṭarattil akā eṉṟum, taṉ kūciyaṉ pāṣiyil akkiṉ eṉṟum, maṅkōliya pāṣiyil akkāṉ eṉṟum, tīpēttu pāṣiyil akkē eṉṟum, turukki pāṣiyil ekē eṉṟum, mārṭuviṉ pāṣiyil aki eṉṟum, ukkiriyaṉ pāṣiyil ikkiyaṉ eṉṟum, lēppis pāṣiyil akkē=camcāram allatu pāṭṭi eṉṟum, māṅkal pāṣiyil akā = mūtta cakōtaraṉ eṉṟum, aikar pāṣiyil akā=mūtta cakōtaraṉ eṉṟum, taṉ kūciyaṉ pāṣiyil  aki-mūtta cakōtaraṉeṉṟum, ōsṭiyāk pāṣiyil ikki=mūttavaṉ eṉṟum, piṉiciya pāṣiyil ukkō=mūttavaṉ eṉṟum, aṅkēriya pāṣiyil ak eṉṟum, vaḻaṅkivarukiṟatu.
mēlkaṇṭa vārttikaḷik kavaṉikkiyil kalappillāta 10 cēr pālil mutalvaṉ oru cēr pāl eṭuttukkoṇṭu oru cēr taṇṇīr viṭṭuvittuppōṉāṉ. iraṇṭāvatāka vanta oruvaṉ orucēr pāl eṭuttukkoṇṭu oru cēr taṇṇīr viṭṭu vittuppōṉāṉ. ippaṭi palar eṭuttapiṉ pōkappōka pāl niṟamummaṇamum kuṟintu paccit taṇṇīr āvatu pōla pāṣiyiṉ vārttikaḷum aṭiyāḷam teriyāmal ākiṉṟaṉa. ammā eṉṟa tamiḻ vārtti ammē, ammi, amā, ama, am, emā, emmā, ēm, im, ampā, appē eṉṟum āmō eṉṟum mā, mō eṉṟum māṟi varukiṟatu. akkāḷ eṉṟa tamiḻ vārtti akkā, akkām, akkiṉ, akkē, aki, akki, akā, ikki, ekē, ikkiyaṉ, ukkō, āk eṉṟum māṟi varukiṟatu. appā eṉṟa tamiḻ vārtti appā, apā, āpō, āp eṉṟum māṟi varukiṟatu.
4.	mēlum kō, kōṉ eṉṟa tamiḻmoḻi tirāviṭapāṣikaḷil vaḻaṅkukiṟaJ. atupōlavē turukki pāṣiyilum maṅkōliya pāṣi yilum kāṉ, kākāṉ eṉṟum, ōsṭiyāk pāṣiyil kōṉ eṉṟum cīttiyapāṣiyiṉ kō eṉṟum vaḻaṅkukiṟaJ.
5.	kol eṉṟa tamiḻppatam raṣiyaṉ pāṣiyil kolyu eṉṟum, iṅkilīṣil kil, kuvēl eṉṟum, piṉīciyapāṣiyil kiyōl eṉṟum, ḻaramis pāṣiyilkōlam eṉṟum, cirsāṉiyaṉ pāṣiyil kulā eṉṟum, aṅkēri pāṣiyil al eṉṟum, nārvē pāṣiyil killā eṉṟum, ṭac pāṣiyil kollaṉ eṉṟum, aislēṇṭ pāṣiyil kollā eṉṟum māṟi varukiṟatu. kol eṉṟa tamiḻppatam kolyu, kollaṉ, kollā, kil, killā, kulā, kuvēl, kōlam, kiyōl, al eṉṟum māṟivaruvatu mikavum vintiyāyirukkiṟatu.
6.	ippaṭiyē kuṭi eṉṟa tamiḻppatamāṉatu vīṭu eṉṟu arttap paṭukiyil camas kirutattil kuṭi, paṭakuṭiṟam= kīttukkuṭici eṉṟum, kuṭumpā eṉṟum, teluṅku kaṉṉaṭaṅkaḷil kuṭi = kōvil eṉṟum, kuṭici = ciṟu vīṭu eṉṟum piṉīciya pāṣiyil kōṭṭā eṉṟum, caramis pāṣiyil kuṭā eṉṟum, mārṭuviṉ pāṣiyil kuṭō eṉṟum ōsṭiyāk pāṣiyilum cākcaṉ pāṣiyilum kāṭ eṉṟum vaḻaṅkivarukiṟatu.
7.	nīr eṉṟa tamiḻmoḻi camaskirutattil nīra, nīram eṉṟum, teluṅkil nīramu, nīru, nīḷḷu eṉṟum, kāṇṭu pāṣiyil nīr eṉṟum, pirāku pāṣiyil īr eṉṟum, kirēkka pāṣiyil nīrō eṉṟum vaḻaṅki varukiṟatu.
8.	miṉ eṉṉum vārttiyi ātāramākak koṇṭu piṟanta mīṉ eṉṉum tamiḻ moḻi camaskirutattil mīṉam, mīṉ eṉṟu vaḻaṅkukiṟaJ.
9.	paṭṭaṇam, paṭṭi eṉṟa tamiḻppatam kaṉṉaṭattil aṭṭi eṉṟum, teluṅkil paṭṭi eṉṟum, camaskirutattil pēṭṭā, paṭṭam, paṭṭaṇam, pattaṉam eṉṟum vaḻaṅkukiṟaJ.
10.	kaṭu, kaṭimi, kaṭi eṉṟa tamiḻppatamāṉatu camaskirutattil kaṭu, kaṭukā, kaṭuku eṉṟu vaḻaṅkukiṟaJ.
ippaṭiyē tamiḻp pataṅkaḷ pala māṟutalkaḷiyaṭintu ulakattiluḷḷa mukkiyamāṉa pāṣikaḷōṭu kalantiruppati nām yāvarum tiṭṭamākak kāṇkiṟōm. vārttikaḷiṉ iṉimiyiyum eḻuttukkaḷiṉ curukkattiyum uccarippiṉ culapattiyum kavaṉikkiyil, tamiḻmoḻikaḷē ātiyil uṇṭākiyirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟu teḷivākakkāṭṭukiṟatu. iṉṉum nām kavaṉippōmāṉāl aṭṭavaṇiyil koṭutta vārttikaḷip pārkkilum ērāḷamāṉa vārttikaḷ ovvoru pāṣiyilum vaḻaṅki varuvatākak kāṇpōm. tamiḻmoḻikaḷ vev vēṟuvita māṟutalkaḷi yaṭintu maṟṟa pāṣiyil vaḻaṅkuvatum ataṟku ilakkaṇaṅkaḷ collap paṭṭatum pōla tamiḻ moḻikaḷōṭu maṟṟa pāṣikaḷ kalavāmal taṉittaṉiyāy niṟpatē tamiḻ kalappuṟāta taṉippāṣiyeṉṟu kāṭṭukiṟatu.
camaskirutattiṟkum ataṉ kiḷippāṣikaḷukkum inta airōppiya pāṣikaḷukkum potuvāṉa ūṟṟu varikkum nām pōy cīttiya pāṣiyil cērntirukkum tamiḻ moḻikaḷiyum epirēya pāṣiyil kalantirukkum tamiḻ moḻikaḷiyum camaskiruta pāṣiyil kalantirukkum tamiḻ moḻikaḷiyum intu airōppiya pāṣikaḷil kalantirukkum tamiḻ moḻikaḷiyum kavaṉikkiyil, tamiḻppāṣi tāyppāṣiyāyirukkalā meṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu. mutaṟ caṅkamirunta kālattiyum atil caṅkīta mirunta uyarnta niliyiyum pūrva tamiḻ nūlkaḷiṉ mūlamāy nām aṟikiyil itu cariyāyirukkalāmeṉṟu niccayamāyt tōṉṟukiṟatu. ivvaḷavu pūrvamum mēṉmiyum poruntiya tamiḻppāṣiyi ātarittu virutti ceyyavēṇṭumeṉṟu kālṭuvel attiyakṣaravarkaḷ collum vacaṉaṅkaḷāvaṉa:-
Dravidian Comparative Grammar by Bishop Caldwell, P. 31.
“The Tamil, however, the most highly cultivated ab-intra of all Dravidian idioms, can dispense with its Sanskrit altogether, if need be, and not only stand alone but flourish without its aid.”
“eṉṟālum, tirāviṭapāṣikaḷ aṉittilum tamiḻ taṉṉilēyē viruttiyaṭintu cīrppaṭuttappaṭṭatāyiruppatiṉāl, avaciyamāṉāl taṉṉōṭu kalantirukkiṟa 
ōm na ma ci va ya eṉṟa aintu aṭcaraṅkaḷ carīrattil niṟkum vitattikkāṭṭum paṭam.






tirumūlar tiruvampalac cakkaram 67.
añcukavañ ceḻuttuṇmi yaṟintapiṉ
ṉeñcakattuḷḷē niṟiyum parāparam
vañcakamilli maṉikkuma ḻivilli
tañcamituveṉṟu cāṟṟukiṉ ṟēṉē.
civavākkiyam pakkam 3.
navviraṇṭu kālatāy naviṉṟamavvu vayiṟatāyc	 yavviraṇṭu kaṇṇatāya marntirunta kāraṇañ
civviraṇṭu tōḷatāyc ciṟanta vavvuvāyatā 		cevviyottuniṉṟatē civāyaminteḻuttumē
camaskirutatti muṟṟilum kaḷintupōṭṭu, taṉiyē niṟka māttira malla, camaskirutattiṉ utavi yevvaḷavumiṉṟit taḻittuc ceḻittup pirakācittirukkavum kūṭum.”
tamiḻmoḻi mutaṟ caṅkattārālum, iraṇṭāñ caṅkattārālum yātoru kalappumiṉṟi pēṇappaṭṭu vantatupōla mūṉṟām caṅkattār kālattil pala kalappuṭaṉ pēṇappaṭṭu vantatu. tamiḻiṉ arumi terinta pala putta vitvacirōmaṇikaḷum tamiḻ āriya vitvacirōmaṇikaḷum ilakkaṇa ilakkiyaṅkaḷ eḻutavum vēṟu cilar pūrva nūlkaḷukku uri yeḻutavum ārampittārkaḷ. itiṉāl pala camaskiruta vārttikaḷ tamiḻil kalakkavum camaskirutattiliruntu tamiḻ uṇṭāṉatāka eṇṇavum cila karuttukaḷ kalakkavum ētuvāyiṟṟu. iruntālum muntiya iraṇṭu caṅkaṅkaḷiṉ iṟutiyil aḻintupōṉa tamiḻ nūlkaḷukkup patil cila nūlkaḷ eḻutiyum uriyeḻutiyum vittārkaḷēyeṉṟu cantōṣappaṭa vēṇṭiyatā yirukkiṟatu. teṉṉāṭṭiṉ kalikaḷil mikac ciṟanta icit tamiḻākiya caṅkītattip putiporuḷākat taṟkālam eṇṇuvatupōlavē akkālattiluḷḷavarum eṇṇit taṅkaḷ cuya pāṣiyilēyē palapērkaḷ koṭuttu eḻuti vittārkaḷeṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu. tamiḻp pāṣiyi naṉṟāy ārāyntu atil mikunta tērcciyaṭinta kālṭuvel attiyakṣar avarkaḷ tamiḻppāṣiyil vittirukkum apimāṉattip pōl tamiḻmakkaḷ apimāṉam vittu ūkkaṅ kāṭṭiṉāl, tamiḻppāṣi pūrva uyar niliyi aṭiyumeṉṟu nampukiṟēṉ.
9. tamiḻppāṣiyē ellāppāṣikaḷukkum tāyppāṣiyā yiruntirukka vēṇṭumeṉpataṟku yāvarum maṟukkakkūṭāta mukkiya ātāram piraṇavamantiram.
ōm eṉṟa piraṇava aṭcaram paramacivattiyē kuṟikkumeṉṟum, atit tiyāṉam paṇṇukiṟavaṉ civattiyē aṭikiṟāṉeṉṟum, civamākavē viḷaṅkukiṟāṉeṉṟum, avveḻuttē mūlamantiramā meṉṟum, am mantirattikkoṇṭē tāṅkaḷ ārampikkum etiyum toṭaṅka iṭi yūṟiṉṟi muṭiyumeṉṟum aṟinta tamiḻmakkaḷ atiyē taṅkaḷukku jepamākak kālimālikaḷil ceytu vantārkaḷeṉpatu nām yāvarum aṟinta viṣayamē. pirāṇāyāmam ceytu cuvācikkum cuvācattiṉ kāla aḷavi virutti ceytu ataṉāl āyuḷ nīṭittirukkum vallamipeṟṟu
		“vāci vāveṉṟu vāciyil ūṭāṭi
		vāciyi uḷḷē vittunī pūjittāl
		vāciyum īcaṉum maruvi oṉṟākum
		vāciyip pōlcitti maṟṟoṉṟum illiyē.”
eṉṟa vākkiyattiṉpaṭi teyvap piracaṉṉamum peṟṟu viḷaṅkiṉārkaḷ. tāṅkaḷ yōka cātaṉi ceykiyil ōm eṉṟa piraṇava aṭcarattiyē uccarittu māttirikaḷiṉ kaṇakki niccayappaṭuttik koṇṭu vantārkaḷ.
ivveḻuttiṉāl kuṟikkappaṭum civattiṉiṉṟu īcāṉam, taṟpuruṣam, akōram, vāmatēvam, cattiyōcātam eṉṟa pañcacattikaḷ uṇṭākiṟa tākavum accaktikaḷiṉ ceyalkaḷip peṟuvataṟku na ma ci va ya eṉṟa aintu aṭcaraṅkaḷi atāvatu pañcāṭcaratti naṭuvaṇi piṭittumāṟi aintu mantiraṅkaḷākki na ma ci va ya,  ci va ya na ma,  ya na ma ci va,  ma ci va ya na,  va ya na ma ci eṉṟa aintu mantiraṅkaḷiliruntum 125 eṉṉum anēka mantiraṅkaḷuṇṭākki, avikaḷ ovvoṉṟiṟkum ōm eṉṟa piraṇava mantirattic cērttu jepittu, avvovvoṉṟiṟkumuriya ceyal kaḷiyum peṟuvārkaḷeṉṟu virivākac colliyum, maṟṟavarkaḷukku upatēcittum, tāṅkaḷ cātittum, tampaṉam, mōkaṉam, vaciyam, māraṇam, uccāṭaṉam, ākaruṭaṇam, vittuvēṭaṉam, pētaṉam eṉṉum aṣṭa karmacittikaḷiyum peṟṟuc ciṟantu viḷaṅkiṉārkaḷ. iṉṉum ivvintu eḻuttukkaḷ niṟinta citamparaccakkarattic jepikkavum kōyilkaḷil stāpikkavum vēṇṭiya vitikaḷum muṟikaḷum virivāyccolli yirukkiṟārkaḷ. ivvintu eḻuttukkaḷum piraṇavattiṉ aintu caktika ḷeṉṟum, ivvintu caktikaḷum piraṇavatti laṭakkameṉṟum, piraṇavamē piramameṉṟum, piramamē pēraṇṭam ciṟṟaṇṭameṉṟum, piraṇavamē pirāṇaṉeṉṟum, piraṇavamē akāra, ukāra, makārameṉṟa aṭcaraṅkaḷiṉ cērkkiyāṉa ōm eṉṟum, cattu, cittu, āṉantameṉṟum, ciruṣṭi titi layameṉṉum muttoḻilkaḷāki ulakam naṭattappaṭṭu varukiṟateṉṟum, eṭṭum iraṇṭum pattumāṉa aḷavuṭaṉ viḷaṅkikkoṇṭirukkiṟateṉṟum, akāra, ukāra, makāramāṉa ravi, mati, akkiṉi eṉṟu collappaṭum kalikaḷāy uyirkaḷ tōṟum pirāṇaṉāy viḷaṅkukiṟateṉṟum, ippirāṇaṉē jīvaṉeṉṟum, āṇavam, kāmam, māyi eṉṟa mummalamaṟṟa jīvaṉē civaṉeṉṟum, jīvacceyal oḻintu civacceyalil niṟpatē tavaceṉṟum, yōkameṉṟum, mōṭcameṉṟum kaṇṭaṟintārkaḷ. itiyē pala nūlāka pakkuvarkaḷukkut takuntavitam eḻutivittārkaḷ. ivveḻuttukkaḷiṉ perumiyiyum ceyalkaḷiyum avarkaḷ eḻutiya yōka nūlkaḷilum ñāṉa nūlkaḷilum mika virivākak kāṇalām.
ōm namacivaya eṉṟa eḻuttukkaḷi nām kavaṉippōmāṉāl mūlā tāram, cuvātiṣṭāṉam, maṇipūrakam, anākatam, vicutti, ākkiṉi eṉṉum carīrattiṉ āṟu ātāraṅkaḷiluḷḷa eḻuttukkaḷākac collappaṭukiṟati aṟivōm. ivvāṟu eḻuttukkaḷum niṟkum variciyi nām kavaṉip pōmāṉāl, kīḻmuka nōkkiya mūlātārattiliruntu mēlmuka nōkkiya mūlamākiya ākkiṉā stāṉam variyum mēlnōkkic celvatāka aṟivōm. mēl mūlattil yakāra aṭcarattil viḷaṅkum ōṅkāram ciraciyum kīḻmūlattil viḷaṅkum ōṅkāram marma stāṉattiyum kuṟikkum. ivviraṇṭu iṭaṅkaḷilum aṅkattiṉ kūṟupāṭukaḷi nām kavaṉippōmāṉāl, ōm eṉṟa aṭcarattiṉ vaṭivamum oru yāṉit taliyiṉ vaṭivamum oru tavaḷikkuñcākiya arittavaḷiyiṉ vaṭivamum ottirukku meṉpati nām aṟiyalām. valatu kaṇṇil cuḻittu vaṭṭam vīci, iṭatu kaṇṇil cuḻittu vaṭṭatti irukūṟākap pirikkak kāl iḻuttu, kaṭiyil cuḻitta rakaciyattit taṉṉiyaṟinta makāṉkaḷ uṇarvārkaḷ. akāra ukāramumāy iraṇṭum makāramāṉa makimiyi maṟṟavar aṟiyāmiyiṉāl avarkaḷukku mantiramākkiṉār. mantiramāy viḷaṅkum ivvaṭcaram nēttiramāṉa iru cuḻikaḷākavum mūḷiyiṉ irupākamākavum mūḷiyiṉiṉṟu puṟappaṭṭa vāciyiṉ kālākavum cakala cittumākavum viḷaṅki niṉṟatupōlavē, kīḻmūlattilum jīvarkaḷ uṟpattiyākum jaṉaṉēntiriya avayavaṅkaḷāka ōṅkāramāy viḷaṅki niṟkiṟaJ. itu māttiramā? ivviru iṭaṅkaḷilum oṅkāratti ottirunta intac carīramāṉatu āti nātattil virutta vaṭivamuḷḷa taliyum oṭuṅkic ciṟuttavālumuḷḷa ōṅkāratti ottirukkum oru ciṟu arittavaḷik kuñcupōla iruntateṉṟu kaṟṟuṇarntōr colvārkaḷ. immutal vaṭivattiyum kīḻmēl mūlaṅkaḷil viḷaṅkum ataṉ corūpattiyum kuṟikkum ōṅkāratti mūlamantira aṭcaramākkiṉārkaḷ. ivvaṭcarattiṉ makimiyi uḷmukamāka aṟivataṟkut taṉṉi aṟitaleṉṟum, taṉṉi aṟitalē talivaṉik kāṇa vaḻiyākumeṉṟum coṉṉārkaḷ. taṉṉi aṟivataṟkāka tūla carīrattiṉ pañcīkaraṇa tattuvaṅkaḷiyum cūṭcuma carīrattiṉ pañcīkaraṇa tattuvaṅkaḷiyum mika virivākak kūṟiyirukkiṟārkaḷ.
vētānta cāstirattiṉ uṭporuḷākiya ōm eṉṟa aṭcaram tamiḻukkē uriyaJ. tamiḻ aṭcarattilēyē tūla aṭcarattiṉ vaṭivam tōṉṟukiṟatu. mantiraṅkaḷukku mutaṉmiyāṉa ōm eṉṟa piraṇava mantiram, a + u + m = ōm eṉṟu muṭinta vitattiyum ōciyiyum tūla vaṭiviyum uḷḷatu uḷḷapaṭiyē kāṭṭik koṇṭu niṟkiṟaJ. iccūṭcama aṭcaratti vēṟu entap pāṣi eḻuttum uḷḷatu uḷḷapaṭi uṭporuḷi viḷakki niṟkak kāṇōm. ōm eṉṟa mantiratti mikap piratāṉamākak koṇṭu taṅkaḷ ikapara vāḻviṟku ituvē ātiyeṉṟu aṉuṣṭittuvarum āriyar, tāṅkaḷ muttiriyāy vaḻaṅkum ivveḻuttiṟku tamiḻ eḻuttit tavira vēṟu enta eḻuttippōṭuvār? vēṟu eḻuttup pōṭṭāl uṭporuḷikkuṟikkumā? kuṟiyāta anniya eḻuttippōṭṭu jepikkalāmā?
10. pañcāṭcaraṅkaḷiṉ cērkkiyālē tūlavaṭivam tōṉṟukiṟatu.
na ma ci va ya eṉṟa aintu aṭcaraṅkaḷiṉāl kuṟikkappaṭum aintu stāṉaṅkaḷilum muṟiyē pirutivi, appu, tēyu, vāyu, ākāyameṉṟa aintu pūtaṅkaḷum pirmā, viṣṇu, rutraṉ, makēsvaraṉ, catācivaṉ eṉṟa pañcakarttākkaḷum pañca varṇaṅkaḷum nālu, āṟu, pattu, paṉṉiraṇṭu, patiṉāṟu, mūṉṟu aṭcaraṅkaḷumāṉa 51 aṭcaraṅkaḷum ivvaṭcaraṅkaḷiṉāl kuṟikkappaṭum pitur, tēvar, tēvatikaḷ caktikaḷum viḷaṅki niṉṟu ōr uṭalāy ōr jīvaṉāyt teyvapatiyāyt teyva vākaṉamāy teyva ālayamāy viḷaṅkukiṟateṉṟu mika vistāramākavum teḷivākavum colliyirukkiṟārkaḷ.
			“aimpa teḻuttē amarnta vētaṅkaḷ
			aimpa teḻuttē ākamam ātikaḷ
			aimpa teḻuttiṉ aṟivi aṟintapiṉ
			aimpa teḻuttē ainteḻuttāmē.”
eṉṟa tirumūlar tirumantirattiṉpaṭi teyvac ceyalkaḷ attaṉiyum aintu aṭcaraṅkaḷāl kuṟikkappaṭum aintu stāṉaṅkaḷilum aintu karttākkaḷā kavum caktikaḷākavumam peṟṟu teyvapatam aṭintārkaḷ. ataṉ mēṉmi yiyaṟinta periyōrkaḷ tīmikku vilaki naṉmi ceyvatiyē jīvar kaḷukkuk kaṟpikkavum taṉṉip pōl piṟaṉi nēcikkum jīvakāruṇya mākiya nirvikaṟpa camātiyil niṉṟu muttineṟi kūṭavum vēṇṭiya cātaṉaṅkaḷiyum vitikaḷiyum vētamāka eḻuti vittārkaḷ. vētattiṉ tiṟavukōlākiya muṉāṭcarattiṉ makimiyi aṉuṣṭittavarē aṟivār.
piraṇava aṭcarattiṉ uṭalākiya pañcāṭcaraṅkaḷiṉ vaṭivattik kavaṉippōmāṉāl, nakāram eṉṟa mutal eḻuttu iṭam valamāka māṟi cuvātiṣṭāṉa cakkarattil eḻuta atuvē iraṇṭu kālkaḷākavum, iraṇṭāvatu eḻuttākiya makāratti iṭam valamāka iṇittu maṇi pūrakattil aṭikka atu vayiṟākavum, cikāramākiya mūṉṟāvatu eḻutti iṭam valam iṇittu anākatac cakkarattil eḻuta iraṇṭu kikaḷākavum irattācayamākavum, vicutti stāṉattil muṉpōl nālāvatu eḻuttākiya vakārattiyeḻuta cuvācācayamākavum kaḻuttākavum, ākkiṉā stāṉattil yakāram eḻuta atu ōṅkāramākavum viḷaṅki, oru maṉuṭa carīrattiṉ amippiyum aṅkaṅkaḷiyum kāṭṭik koṇṭirukkum. ātistāṉa mākiya mēl mukkōṇattil viḷaṅkum ōṅkāra vaṭivam ciraciyum kīḻmukam nōkkiya mukkōṇamāy viḷaṅkum mūlātārattil ōṅkāra vaṭivam pirajāpatiyiyum kuṟikkum.
pirajikaḷ uṟpattikkuk kāraṇamākiya mūlātārattil uṭporuḷāy viḷaṅkum karuvum ōṅkāra corūpamāy niṟkum. ippaṭit tūla cūṭcama kāraṇam eṉṉum mūṉṟu vaḻiyilumviḷaṅki niṟkum ōm eṉṟa aṭcaramum atu viḷaṅki niṟkum tūla carīramum namacivaya eṉṟa aintu aṭcaraṅkaḷiṉālākiya carīrattil mukkiyamāka viḷaṅki niṟkiṟaJ. tūla carīrattiṉ āṟu ātāraccakkaraṅkaḷiyum eḻuti avikaḷil aṭcaraṅkaḷ porunti vaṭivuṇṭākum vitattiyum nām pūrva tamiḻ nūlkaḷil teḷivākak kāṇpōm. na ma ci va ya eṉṟa ivvintu aṭcaraṅkaḷiṉ vaṭivippōlalotta eḻuttukkaḷ maṟṟum pāṣikaḷililli yeṉpatu niccayam. mantiraṅkaḷil mutaṉmipeṟṟa immantiramum mantirattiṉ uṭporuḷkaḷik kuṟikkum aṭcaramum tamiḻ pāṣikkēyuriyavi. maṟṟavar jepittālum jepitta mantirattiṉ uṭporuḷ kāṭṭum aṭcaraṅkaḷ illātu pōṉāreṉpatu niccayam. itupaṟṟiyē, tamiḻppāṣi mikappūrvamāyuḷḷateṉṟu nām tuṇintu collalām.
11. oṉṟiliruntē ulaka muṇṭāṉatupōl tamiḻiluḷḷa ka,ca,ṭa,ta,pa eṉṟa eḻuttukkaḷiliruntē maṟṟa nālu eḻuttukkaḷ vantaṉa.
ātiyil oṉṟākavum ataṉ piṉpē iraṇṭu mūṉṟākavum viruttikku varuvatu ulaka iyaṟki. oṉṟāyirunta mutal vastuvi liruntē cakalamum uṇṭāyiṟṟu. oru pāṣiyil vaḻaṅkivarum pala ōcikaḷuṭiya iṉaveḻuttukkaḷi oṉṟākac ceyvatu maṉuṭa iyaṟki yallavē. oṉṟiliruntu pala nūtaṉaṅkaḷuṇṭākkuvatē iyalpu. iv vuṇmikkēṟpa, ka,ca,ṭa,ta,pa eṉṟa āti ōcikaḷiliruntu ovvoṉṟiṟku nannāṉku eḻuttukkaḷ vītam viruttiyāvatu iyalpē. maṉatil eṇṇum karuttukkaḷ yāviyum colvataṟku aṉukūlamāka ivveḻuttukkaḷ camaskirutattil cērttuk koḷḷappaṭṭatippaṟṟi mikavum cantōṣappaṭa vēṇṭiyatāyirukkiṟatu. maṟṟum camaskiruta eḻuttippaṟṟic colla iṅku avaciyamilliyātalāl viṭappaṭṭaJ. ivveḻuttukkaḷil cila tamiḻilum vaḻaṅkuvataṟku ilakkaṇamum collappaṭṭirukkiṟatu. āki yiṉāl tamiḻp pāṣiyum ataṉ eḻuttukkaḷum mikunta pūrvamāṉavi yeṉṟum, tamiḻppāṣi yātōṭum kalappuṟāta taṉippāṣi yeṉṟum ataṉ piṉṉē maṟṟap pāṣikaḷuṇṭā yirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟum tōṉṟukiṟatu.
tamiḻ eṉṟa patattil vaḻaṅkum  ḻa eṉṟa ciṟappeḻuttu maṟṟa entap pāṣikkārarum taṅkaḷ pāṣiyiṉ eḻuttukkaḷiṉāl eḻuti uccarikkak kūṭāta mēmpāṭuṭiya teṉpatiyum maṟantupōkakkūṭāJ.
mikat toṉmiyum taṉimiyumāṉa ippāṣi mikappūrvīkamāṉa teṉmaturiyil aracāṇṭukoṇṭirunta pāṇṭiya rājākkaḷiṉāl ātarikkappaṭṭum akkirācaṉam peṟṟum mikavum uṉṉata niliyiliruntaJ. jalappiraḷayattiṉāl aḻintupōṉa 49 tamiḻnāṭukaḷilumirunta ciṟanta vitvāṉkaḷ yāvarum oru caṅkamākac cērkkappaṭṭu ariya periya viṣayaṅkaḷi vicārittum ciṟanta nūlkaḷi araṅkēṟṟiyum vantārkaḷ.
12. teṉṉintiyāviṉ teṉpakkattiluḷḷa teṉmaturiyum, teṉmaturi aḻintapiṉ teṉṉintiyāvum caṅkītattil mikap pūrvamuṭiyavi yeṉpaJ.
itaṉ muṉṉuḷḷa ellāvaṟṟiyum nām kavaṉikkiyil, teṉṉinti yāviṉ teṉpākattil intumakā camuttirattil vicālittirunta lemūriyā eṉṉum kaṇṭam oṉṟiruntateṉṟum, atuvē jātikaḷ yāvum piṟantu vaḷarnta toṭṭilāyirunta teṉṟum, avviṭattuḷḷōr akkaṇṭam aḻintapiṉ aticcuṟṟiyuḷḷa tīvukaḷilum nāṭukaḷilum kuṭiyēṟiṉārka ḷeṉṟum terikiṟatu. avviṭattiluḷḷa maraṅkaḷ mirukaṅkaḷ maṉitarkaḷik koṇṭum, cuṟṟiṭaṅkaḷilakappaṭum maṉita elumpukaḷ pirāṇikaḷiṉ elumpukaḷik koṇṭum, pāṣikaḷil kalantirukkum vārttikaḷik koṇṭum, pūrvakālattil avarkaḷ veku nākarīkamuṭiyavar kaḷāyiruntār kaḷeṉṟum, avarkaḷ tamiḻppāṣiyiyē pēciṉārkaḷeṉṟum nīṇṭa āyuḷuḷḷavarkaḷāyum palacālikaḷāyu miruntārkaḷeṉṟum kāṇappaṭukiṟatu.
iyal ici nāṭakameṉṉum muttamiḻilum ciṟantu viḷaṅkiya tamiḻnāṭu aḻintapiṉ, ataṉ camīpattiluḷḷa teṉṉintiyāvil aṅkirun tōr aṉēkar tappi aṭikkalam pukuntirukkalāmeṉṟum, atu tamiḻnāṭṭiṉ oru pākamāyiruntataṉāl, iyal ici nāṭakameṉṉum mut tamiḻukkuriya cila amcaṅkaḷum oruvāṟu annāṭṭil vaḻaṅkivantaṉa veṉṟum nām niṉikkavēṇṭum. ataṉālēyē veku pūrvantoṭṭut teṉṉintiyāviṉ caṅkītam cāstira muṟimiyuṭiyateṉṟu yāvarālum koṇṭāṭappaṭṭu vantatu. vāḻiyaṭi vāḻiyāy vamcaparampariyāyk kōyil campaḷaṅkaḷiṉāl ātarikkappaṭṭu vanta naṭaṉamātarkaḷum, avarkaḷ aṇṇāvikaḷum, nākacurakkārarkaḷum, tavulkārarkaḷum, ōccarkaḷum, pullāṅ kuḻal vācippavarkaḷum, vīṇi vācippavarkaḷum, caṅkīta cāstirattip pēṇi nāḷatuvariyum paṭittuvarukiṟārkaḷ. ivarkaḷiṉiṉṟē teṉṉintiya caṅkītatti maṟṟavar kaṟṟukkoṇṭāreṉṟum, kaṟṟukkoḷḷukiṟārka ḷeṉṟum pirattiyaṭcamāy aṟiyalām. vīṇi vācippatilum, vāyppāṭṭup pāṭuvatilum tērnta vitvāṉkaḷukkum pāṭam collakkūṭiya nākacurakkārarkaḷ iṉṟikkum irukkiṟārkaḷeṉpati nām kāṇalām. ākavē caṅkītattit taṅkaḷ jīvaṉamākak koṇṭa oru vakuppārāl paratam, vāyppāṭṭu, vīṇi, pullāṅkuḻal, nākacuram, mēḷam mutaliya caṅkīta amcaṅkaḷ mukkiyamāyp pēṇappaṭṭum maṟṟavarukkuc collik koṭukkap paṭṭum vantirukkiṟateṉṟu nām eṇṇa iṭamirukkiṟatu. vaṭapāṣiyil tērnta vitvāṉkaḷ ērāḷamāyirukkum vaṭanāṭṭil, teṉṉāṭṭiṉ caṅkītampōl ciṟanta caṅkītamilliyeṉṟu nām kāṇpatē, caṅkīta cāstiram ātiyil tamiḻilēyē yiruntateṉpataṟkum, teṉṉāṭṭilēyē vaḻaṅkivantateṉpataṟkum teṉṉāṭṭiṟku vantapiṉpē āriyar atil tērntavarkaḷāṉār kaḷeṉpataṟkum teḷivāṉa attāṭciyākiṟaJ. mēṉmiyuṭiyatākiya teṉṉintiya caṅkīta muṟimiyi 4,440 varuṣaṅkaḷākavirunta mutaṟ caṅkattārum rājarkaḷum pirapukkaḷum vitvāṉkaḷum ātarittu arumiyāṉa nūlkaḷ eḻuti mikavum virutti nilikkuk koṇṭuvantiruntārkaḷeṉpati nām vicārittaṟintāl, intiyāviṉ caṅkītam mikap pūrvamāyuḷḷateṉpatu namakku viḷaṅkum. ākiyiṉāl teṉmaturiyilirunta mutal caṅkattiyum kapāṭapuratti lirunta iṭiccaṅkattiyum uttara maturiyilirunta mūṉṟāvatu caṅkattiyum paṟṟic collum cila kāriyaṅkaḷip pārppōm.

13. teṉmaturiyilum kapāṭapurattilum uttaramaturiyilumirunta mūṉṟu caṅkaṅkaḷippaṟṟiya cila mukkiya kuṟippukaḷ.
ittamiḻmoḻiyi virutti ceytuvanta muccaṅkaṅkaḷuḷ mutaṟcaṅkam mutal ūḻiyiṉ iṟutiyiliruntatākac collappaṭukiṟatu. im mutaṟcaṅkamirunta kālattiyum atil vaḻaṅkiya nūlkaḷiyumpaṟṟi aṟivatē namakku avaciyamām. iṟṟikkuc cumār 8,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉuḷḷa oru kalviccapiyiyum atil vaḻaṅki vanta nūlkaḷiyum annūlkaḷuḷ caṅkītattiṉ perumiyiyum aṟivatē namakku mukkiyam. ākiyāl kaṭal koṇṭa teṉmaturiyilirunta mutaṟcaṅkattippaṟṟiyum, kaṭalāl aḻikkappaṭṭa kapāṭapurattilirunta iraṇṭām caṅkattippaṟṟiyum, uttara maturiyeṉṟa taṟkāla maturiyirunta mūṉṟām caṅkattippaṟṟiyum kalvimāṉkaḷ kūṟum cila ātāraṅkaḷic caṟṟuk kavaṉippōm. iṟṟikkuc cumār 1,800 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ cēranāṭṭil aracāṇṭa ceṅkuṭṭuvaṉ eṉpavaruṭiya tampi iḷaṅkōvaṭikaḷ ‘cilappatikāram’ eṉṉum orunūl eḻutiyirukkiṟār. atil akkālattiluḷḷa rājyaṅkaḷiṉ perumi, rājākkaḷiṉtaṉmi, aracāṭci mutaliyavikaḷ teḷivāka viḷaṅkukiṉṟaṉa. katānāyakaṉākiya kōvalaṉiyum avaṉ kātalitta naṭaṉak kaṇikiyākiya mātaviyiyum paṟṟiccollumiṭattu, ivviruvarum vīṇi vācippatil mikavum pāṇṭittiyamuṭiyavarkaḷāyiruntārkaḷeṉṟum mātavi paratattilum mikat tērntavaḷāyiruntāḷeṉṟum kūṟip paratattiṉ cila mukkiya amcaṅkaḷiyum ciṟappāka eṭuttuccollukiṟār. ataṟku uriyāciriyarākiya aṭiyārkku nallār kālattil kēḷviyilum arikuṟiyāyirunta nūlkaḷilumiruntu caṅkītattippaṟṟi eḻutiya urikaḷil cila mukkiyamāṉa amcaṅkaḷ veḷiyākiṉṟaṉa. innūli veḷippaṭuttiya makā makōpāttiyāyar vē. cāminātaiyar avarkaḷāl eḻutappaṭṭa cila kuṟippukaḷi iṅkē pārppōm.
cilappatikāram, iḷaṅkōvaṭikaḷ varalāṟu,
“iḷaṅkōvaṭikaḷ kālam kaṭiccaṅkap pulavar kālameṉṟu niccayikkap paṭukiṟaJ. akkaṭiccaṅkappulavaruṭ ciṟantavarum maturik kaṇakkāyaṉār makaṉārumākiya nakkīraṉār iṟiyaṉārakap poruḷukkut tām iyaṟṟiyavuriyil innūliṟ kāṉal variyiluḷḷa “niṇaṅkoḷ,” “tuṟimēy valampuri” “nērntanaṅkātalar” “puṇartuṇi” eṉṉum pāṭalkaḷi yeṭuttu utāraṇamākak kāṭṭiyiruppatuṅ kāṇka. iḷaṅkōvaṭikaḷukkut tamiyaṉākiya ceṅkuṭṭuvaṉ pattiṉik kaṭavuḷākiya kaṇṇakiyārkkuk kōyil camittu viḻākkoṇṭāṭiya kālattu ilaṅki yaracaṉākiya kayavākuveṉpavaṉ uṭaṉiruntāṉeṉṟu varantarukātiyālum, ak kayavākuvum ilaṅkiyiṟ kaṇṇakiyārkkuk kōyil kaṭṭuvittu viḻākkoṇṭāṭiṉāṉeṉṟu innūṟpatikattic cārnta uripeṟu kaṭṭuriyālun terikiṉṟaṉa. iṟṟikkuc caṟṟēṟakkuṟiya 1,760 varuṭaṅkaḷukku muṉpu kayavākuveṉṉu maracaṉoruvaṉ iruntāṉeṉṟu ilaṅkic carittirattāṟ (makāvamcam) pulappaṭukiṉṟaJ. piṉṉum caṟṟēṟak kuṟiya 760 varuṭaṅkaḷukku muṉpu kayavāku veṉṉum maṟṟōr rājaṉiruntatākavum accarittirattāl teriyavarukiṉṟaJ. āyiṉum, innūliṟ kūṟiya vēṟu cila aracarkaḷuṭiya kālatti ārāyumpōtu in nūlāciriyar kālam iraṇṭāṅkayavākuviṉ kālattiṟku muntiyatākat terikiṉṟamiyiṉ, ivarkālam mutaṟkayavākuviṉ kālameṉṟē tuṇiyappaṭukiṉṟaJ.”
ivvacaṉaṅkaḷil makāvamcam eṉṟa ilaṅkic carittirattāl kayavākuviṉ kālamum akkālattilirunta iḷaṅkōvaṭikaḷiṉ kālamum kaṭiccaṅkattiṉ pulavaruḷ oruvarākiya nakkīraṉār kālamum oṉṟeṉṟu niṉikka iṭamirukkiṟatu. kayavākuviṉ kālam iṟṟikkuc caṟṟēṟak kuṟiya 1,800 varuṣaṅkaḷākum. mēlum kūlavāṇikaṉ cāttaṉārāl ceyyappaṭṭamaṇi mēkaliyeṉṉum nūli nacciṉārkkiṉiyaṉār ciṟappittuc colluvatik kavaṉikkumpoḻutu kūlavāṇikaṉ cāttaṉār kālamum iḷaṅkōvaṭikaḷ kālamum oṉṟeṉṟu koḷḷa iṭamirukkiṟatu. iḷaṅkōvaṭikaḷ eḻutiya cilappatikārattiṟku aṭiyārkku nallār eḻutiya uriyum kaṭiccaṅkap pulavaruḷ oruvarākiya nakkīraṉāruṭiya kālattiṟkup pintiṉatāyirukkavēṇṭumeṉṟu ūkikka iṭamirukkiṟatu. mēṟkaṇṭapaṭi 1,800 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ eḻutappaṭṭa nūlil, jalap piraḷayattil aḻintupōṉa teṉmaturiyilirunta mutaṟ caṅkattip paṟṟiyum iraṇṭāvatu caṅkamirunta kapāṭapurattip paṟṟiyum mikavum teḷivāka eḻutukiṟār.
14. mutaṟ caṅkamum teṉ maturiyic cērnta 49 nāṭukaḷum kaṭalāl aḻintatippaṟṟi.
cilappatikāram, vēṉiṟkātiyuri.
“neṭiyōṉ kuṉṟam-vēṅkaṭamali. toṭiyōḷ-peṇpāṟ peyarāṟ kumariyeṉpatāyiṟṟu. ākavē teṉpāṟ kaṇṇatōr āṟṟiṟkup peyarām. āṉāl, neṭiyōṉ kuṉṟamum toṭiyōḷ natiyumeṉṉātu puvamu meṉṟatu eṉṉi yeṉiṉ, mutalūḻiyiṟutikkaṇ teṉmaturiyakattut taliccaṅkattu akattiyaṉārum iṟiyaṉārum kumaravēḷum murañciyūr muṭināka rāyarum nitiyiṉ kiḻavaṉum eṉṟivaruḷḷiṭṭa nālāyirattu nāṉūṟṟu nāṟpattoṉpatiṉmar eṇṇiṟanta paripāṭalum mutunāriyum mutukurukum kaḷariyāviriyu muḷḷiṭṭavaṟṟip puṉintu terintu nālāyirattu nāṉūṟṟu nāṟpatiṟṟiyāṇṭu irīyiṉār. kāyciṉa vaḻutimutaṟ kaṭuṅkōṉīṟāyuḷḷār eṇpattoṉpatiṉmar; avaruṭ kaviyaraṅkēṟiṉār eḻuvar pāṇṭiyaruḷ oruvaṉ cayamākīrttiyaṉākiya nilantaru tiruviṟ pāṇṭiyaṉ tolkāppiyam pulappaṭuttu irīyiṉāṉ. akkālattu avar nāṭṭut teṉpāli mukattiṟku vaṭavelliyākiya paḵṟuḷiyeṉṉu māṟṟiṟkum kumariyeṉṉu māṟṟiṟku miṭiyē eḻunūṟṟuk kāvatavāṟum ivaṟṟiṉ nīr malivāṉeṉa malinta ēḻ teṅkanāṭum ēḻ maturināṭum ēḻ muṉpālināṭum ēḻ piṉpālināṭum ēḻ kuṉṟanāṭum ēḻ kuṇakāri nāṭum ēḻ kuṟumpaṉināṭumeṉṉum inta nāṟpattoṉpatu nāṭum kumari kolla mutaliya paṉmalināṭum kāṭum natiyum patiyum taṭanīrkkumari vaṭaperuṅkōṭṭiṉ kāṟum kaṭal koṇṭoḻitalāṟ kumariyākiya puva meṉṟāreṉṟuṇarka. iḵtu eṉṉi peṟumāṟeṉiṉ, ‘vaṭivēleṟinta vāṉpaki poṟātu, paḵṟuḷi yāṟṟuṭaṉ paṉmaliyaṭukkattuk kumarikkōṭuṅ koṭuṅkaṭal koḷḷa’ eṉpataṉālum kaṇakkāyaṉār makaṉār nakkīraṉāruritta iṟiyaṉār poruḷuriyāṉum uriyāciriyarākiya iḷampūraṇa vaṭikaḷ mukavuriyāṉum piṟavāṟṟāṉum peṟutum. aḵtu aṟṟāka, vaṭakkiṉkaṇ vēṅkaṭamali teṟkiṉkaṭ kumarikkaṭaleṉak kuṟiyāṟ kūṟiṉavar kīḻpāṉ mēlpāṟku elli kūṟātatu eṉṉiyō veṉil, neṭunticiyākiya vaṭapāṟkelli kuṉṟameṉṟum teṉpāṟ kelli kumarip puvameṉṟum kūṟiṉamiyāṉ oḻinta ticikaṭku oḻinta puvam elli eṉpatāyiṟṟu; eṉṉi? ‘vēṅkaṭaṅkumari tīmpuṉaṟ puvameṉ ṟinnāṉkelli tamiḻatu vaḻakkē’ eṉṟār cikaṇṭiyāru mākaliṉ. aṉṟiyum vaṭa ticikkaṇ vaṭukoḻinta tiripuṭi moḻi palavuḷavākalāṉ, maliyelli kūṟi oḻinta tici mūṉṟiṟkum tiripiṉmiyāṟ kaṭalelli kūṟiṉāreṉiṉumamiyum.”
ivvuriyāl teṉṉintiyāviṉ teṉpākattiluḷḷa kumari nāṭṭiṟkuk kumariyāṟu vaṭavelliyā yiruntateṉṟum kumarināṭu vari 49 nāṭukaḷum kaṭalāl aḻintupōyiṉa veṉṟum pārttōm. avvaḻintu pōṉa 49 nāṭukaḷippaṟṟiya cila viṣayaṅkaḷi itaṉ piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
15. mutal ūḻiyil 49 tamiḻ nāṭukaḷippaṟṟiya cila viparam.
tamiḻmoḻiyiṉ varalāṟu,
“puṟanāṉūṟṟiṟ pāṇṭiyaṉ palyākacāli mutukuṭumip peruvaḻutiyi neṭṭimiyār pāṭiya pāṭṭiṟ ‘paḵṟuḷiyāṟu’ vaṭimpalampa niṉṟa pāṇṭiyaṉāl uṇṭākkappaṭṭateṉpatu kuṟikkappaṭṭuḷatu; ciṉṉāṇ muṉṉar veḷippaṭṭa ‘ceṅkōṉṟaric celavu’ eṉṟatōr ciṟu nūliṉāṟ cila viṣayaṅkaḷ viḷaṅkukiṉṟaṉa. mēl aṭiyārkku nallāruriyāṉ viḷaṅkiya ēḻ teṅkāṭu mutaliya nāṭukaḷic cārntu ‘peruvaḷanāṭu’ mutaliya piṟanāṭukaḷum, ‘maṇimali’ mutaliya cila malikaḷum, ‘muttūr’ mutaliya cilavūrkaḷum, cakkarakkō, pērāṟṟu neṭuntuṟiyaṉ, iṭikkaḻic ceṅkōṭaṉ mutaliya pulavar cilaratu peyarkaḷum, ‘perunūl’, ‘iyaṉūl’ eṉac cila nūṟpeyarum annūlāṉum ataṉuriyāṉum veḷiyākiṉṟaṉa. annūl mutalūḻiyil taliccaṅkattār kālattiṟ kumariyāṟṟiṟkum paḵṟuḷiyāṟṟiṟkum iṭiyēyuḷḷa peruvaḷanāṭ ṭaracaṉākiya ceṅkōvi mutalūḻit taṉiyūrc cēntaṉ pāṭiṉāṉeṉpatu,
			
“ceṅkōṉ ṟariccelavic cēntaṉ ṟaṉiyūrāṉ
			tuṅkaṉ ṟamiḻttāp pulittoṭarā-laṅkicittāṉ
			cakkarakkō muṉṉiṉṟu cāṟṟum peruvūḻi
			yakkarakkō nāmañcu vām”
eṉṟa paḵṟuḷiyāṟṟut talippāyccal ēḻteṅkanāṭṭu muttūr akattiyaṉ kūṟiya pāṭṭiṉāṟ pulaṉākiṉṟaJ. iviyaṉittiyum uṟṟunōkkumiṭattu, eḻunūṟṟukkāvatam akaṉṟu kiṭanta nāṟpattoṉpatu tamiḻnāṭukaḷ kaṭal koḷḷappaṭṭaṉa veṉpatu pulaṉām. ikkālattu aḷaviṉpaṭi oru kāvatameṉpatu pattu milāka, eḻunūṟu kāvatamum ēḻāyira milelliyaḷavām. ‘intumakā camuttiram’ irunūṟṟimpatu lakṣam caturamiluḷḷaJ. itaṉāl atu ciṟitu kuṟiyap patiṉāṟulakṣam mil nīḷamum patiṉāṟu lakṣam mil akalamumuṭiyateṉpatu peṟappaṭum. peṟavē ippatiṉāṟulakṣam mil nīḷattil ēḻāyiram milaḷavu nilaṉāyiruntu kaṭal koḷḷappaṭṭiruttal vēṇṭum. iṉi ‘mōrīcuttīvu’kkum ‘pampāy’ nakarattukkum iṭiyiluḷḷa nīrpparavi iraṇṭāyirat tinnūṟumil nīḷamuḷḷatām. mōrīcuttīviṟkum ataṟkut teṟkiluḷḷa ‘kerkiyūlaṉ’ eṉṉun tīviṟkum iṭiyiluḷḷa nīḷamum avvaḷaviṉatēyām. ākavē nīḷattil ikkālattiluḷḷa ‘kumarimuṉi’ yiliruntu kerkiyūlaṉ tīviṉ teṟku variyilum, akalattil ‘maṭakācikartīvu’ mutaṟ ‘cumāttirā’, ‘jāvā’ mutaliyavaṟṟi yuḷḷaṭakkiya ‘cantāt tīvukaḷ’ aḷavum virintu kiṭanta kumarināṭu kaṭal koḷḷappaṭṭa teṉpatu pōtarum.
