“orē ātipāṣiyiṉiṉṟum uṟpattiyāṉa palavita iṟantupōṉa pāṣikaḷiyum upayōkattilirukkum pāṣikaḷiyum oppiṭṭup pārppōmāṉāl nām atēvita muṭivāṉa apippirāyattukkuttāṉ varuvōm. intak koḷkiyi maṉatil vittuk koṇṭu nammuṭiya iṇṭu jermāṉik (Indo-Germanic)  pāṣikaḷiṉ vamca aṭṭavaṇiyi nām cōtippōmāṉāl, ippōtu vaḻaṅkukiṟa ententa pāṣikaḷi pūrva pāṣikaḷiṉ kumāratti allatu pētti eṉṟu niṉikkiṟōmō antanta pūrva pāṣikaḷellām vekukālamāy upayōkattilillāmal iṟantu pōṉavikaḷāyt terikiṉṟaṉa. āriyōrōmāṉik (Aryo-Romanic) pāṣikaḷum, cilavō jermāṉik (Slavo-Germanic) pāṣikaḷum muḻutum kāṇāmal maṟintaṉa. appaṭiyē āriyaṉ (Aryan)  kirikkōrōmaṉ (Greco-Roman) cilavō leṭṭik (Slavo-Lettic)  pūrva jermāṉik (Germanic) mutaliya pāṣikaḷellām oḻintupōyiṉa. ivikaḷiṉ kumārattikaḷum pēttikaḷuṅkūṭa oḻintu pōyiṉa. taṟkālam vaḻaṅkum iṇṭu jermāṉik (Indo-Germanic) pāṣikaḷellām etu viṣayattil ottirukkiṉṟaṉa veṉṟāl, orē aṭiyil piṟantum pala vittiyācamāṉa piṉ cantatiyāri uṭiyavikaḷāyiruppatiltāṉ āti pāṣiyiliruntu cila anēka māṟutalkaḷaṭintum cila koñca māṟutalkaḷaṭintum kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa.
eḷitil rūpakārappaṭuttakkūṭiya inta viṣayamāṉatu itaṟku oppāṉa mutukelumpuṭiya jīvarācikaḷiṉ uṟpattiyi viḷakkakkūṭiyatāyirukkiṟatu. pala pāṣikaḷiṉ uṟpattiyik kāṭṭukiṟa pāṣā cāstiramāṉatu pala mirukaṅkaḷiṉ uṟpattiyik kāṭṭum miruka cāstirattiṟku palatta utaviyāyirukkiṟatu. āṉāl oṉṟu maṟṟoṉṟiviṭa tiṭamāy rūpakārap paṭuttum viṣayattil atika ciṟantatāyirukkiṟatu. eppaṭiyeṉṟāl pāṣā cāstirattiṟku vēṇṭiya ātāraṅkaḷ mutaliyavi, atāvatu aḻintupōṉa pāṣikaḷiṉ ciṉṉaṅkaḷ, miruka cāstira ātāraṅkaḷākiya elumpu mutaliyavikaḷiṉ kal accukaḷiviṭa tiṟamāy pātukāttu vikkap paṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉa.”
mēṟkāṭṭiya cila varikaḷi nām kavaṉikkiyil ippōtu vaḻaṅki varukiṟa ellāp pāṣikaḷum orē oru pāṣiyiliruntu uṇṭāṉavi yeṉṟu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. appāṣi lemūriyā eṉṉum paḻiya ulakattil pēcappaṭṭu vantatu. anta āti pāṣiyiṉ vārttikaḷ palavum pala pāṣikaḷōṭu kalantiruppatik koṇṭu pala jentukkaḷiṉ uṟpattiyiyum mirukaṅkaḷiliruntu uṇṭāṉa maṉuṣaṉiṉ uṟpattiyiyum tiṭṭamāyt terintu koḷvataṟku palatta utaviyāyirukkum eṉṟum tōṉṟukiṟatu. pāṣikaḷil vaḻaṅkivarum āti pāṣiyiṉ cila vārttikaḷ kaṟkaḷil tōṉṟum mirukac ciṉṉaṅkaḷip pārkkilum palatta cāṭciyāka viḷaṅki niṟkiṉṟaṉa.
32. lemūriyā nāṭṭil pēcappaṭṭu vanta pāṣi tamiḻppāṣiyē eṉpatu.
maṉita jāti uṟpattiyāṉa ātipūmi lemūriyā eṉṟu nām palavitattālum tiṭṭamāy aṟikiṟōm. lemūriyāk kaṇṭattiliruntu maṉita jātikaḷum maṉita jātikku muntiya vālillāk kuraṅkiṉamum maṟṟum cila pirāṇikaḷum tāvara varkkaṅkaḷum ataṟku cuṟṟilumuḷḷa iṭaṅkaḷiliruppatik koṇṭu teṉṉintiyāviṉ teṉpākattiliruntu aḻintu pōṉatākac collappaṭum kumarināṭē lemūriyāveṉṟu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. kumariyāṟṟiṟkum, paḵṟuḷiyāṟṟiṟkum naṭuvilirunta tākac collappaṭum mikunta nīr vaḷamuḷḷa ēḻteṅkanāṭum, ēḻ maturi nāṭum, ēḻ muṉpālināṭum, ēḻ piṉpāli nāṭum, ēḻ kuṉṟanāṭum, ēḻ kuṇakārināṭum, ēḻ kuṟumpaṉināṭum ākiya 49 nāṭukaḷaṭaṅkiya teṉpāṇṭi nāṭē maṉita jātikku āti piṟappiṭameṉṟu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. ēḻu ēḻu nāṟpattoṉpatu nāṭukaḷ eṉṟu colluvati nām kavaṉikkiyil nāval tīvu, (nāval maram) iṟalit tīvu, (ittimaram) kucit tīvu, (nāṇal) kiravuñcat tīvu, (aṉṟil) puṣkarattīvu, (yāṉi) teṅkut tīvu, kamukut tīvu eṉṟa ēḻu perum pūpākaṅkaḷum āsṭrēliyā, cumāttirā, jāvā pōla ēḻu tīvukaḷāyiruntirukkalāmeṉṟum avikaḷ ov voṉṟum evvēḻu nāṭukaḷākap pirikkappaṭṭirukkalāmeṉṟum tōṉṟukiṟatu. teṉṉintiyāvil iyaṟkiyāyk kāṇappaṭum nāval, itti, nāṇal, aṉṟil, yāṉi, teṅku, kamuku mutaliya maraṅkaḷum jīvarācikaḷum ēḻu tīvukaḷilum mikutiyāy iruntatākat terikiṟatu. maṟṟa iṭaṅkaḷi liruppatāka nām kāṇamāṭṭōm. capta curaṅkaḷum ivvēḻu tīvukaḷilēyē yiruntu uṇṭāṉatāka pūrva nūlkaḷil collappaṭukiṟatu. inta nāṟpat toṉpatu nāṭum kaṭalāl aḻikkappaṭumuṉ mikunta ceḻippuṭiyatāyum iyaṟki amippiṉ vaḷaṅkaḷ mikuntatāyum pāṇṭiya rājarkaḷāl āḷappaṭṭu vantatāyum nām itaṉmuṉ pārttōm. kaṭalāl aḻinta innāṭṭiṟku teṉmaturi talinakarāyiruntaJ. itil tamiḻiyē pēci vantārkaḷ. ittamiḻ moḻiyē pala iṭaṅkaḷilum pala pāṣikaḷilum vaḻaṅki varukiṟateṉṟu itaṉmuṉ vistāramākap pārttōm. tamiḻp pāṣiyil eḻutappaṭṭa pala ariya viṣayaṅkaḷum nūlkaḷum piraḷayattāl aḻikkappaṭṭapiṉ pāṣiyiṉ mikavum coṟpamāṉa pākamāttiram miñci niṉṟaJ. pūrvamuḷḷa nūlkaḷil vaḻaṅki varum cila vārttikaḷ iṉṉum arttan teriyāmal appaṭiyē niṟkiṉṟaṉa. anēka arum pataṅkaḷ vaḻaṅkāmal oḻintaṉa. ippaṭi nūlkaḷum nūlkaḷiṉ poruḷkaḷum poruḷkaḷi viḷakkum arumpataṅkaḷum oḻintapiṉ, neyyariyil miñciya ili ciṟuvar vāy vantatu pōla nāṭōṭiya vaḻakkattilirukkum vārttikaḷē tamiḻil vaḻaṅki varukiṉṟaṉa. ittamiḻmoḻi pūrvamāy lemūriyāvil pēcappaṭṭu vantateṉpatiyum atu pala pāṣikaḷil kalantirukkiṟateṉpatiyum iyaṟki amippiṉ ōcikaḷukku mikunta poruttamuṭiya tāyirukkiṟateṉpatiyum eḻuttukkaḷiṉ culapamāṉa vaṭiviyum eḻuttut tavaṟāta uccarippiyum koṇṭu tamiḻē ātipūrva pāṣiyeṉṟu tiṭṭamākat terikiṟatu. 49 tamiḻ nāṭukaḷaṭaṅkiya lemūriyā aḻinta kālattil tamiḻarum, tamiḻil eḻutappaṭṭa kaliñāṉaṅkaḷum aḻintu pōṉatiṉāl, tamiḻi aṟpamāka niṉittu alaṭciyam ceyyum kēvala nilikku vantatu. peruṅkāyamirunta pāttirattil eñci niṉṟa vācaṉipōla mikac coṟpamāṉa pākam iraṇṭāñ caṅka kālattil nūlkaḷāka viḷaṅkiṉa. tolkāppiyattiyum maṟṟum cila nūlkaḷiyum nām uṟṟu nōkkiṉāl vittiyam, vātam, yōkam, ñāṉam, cōtiṭam, cittiram, caṅkītam mutaliya 64 kalikaḷum tamiḻnāṭṭil mikunta perumi peṟṟu vaḻaṅki vantaṉaveṉṟu aṟivōm. mutaṟ caṅkamirunta teṉ maturiyil kāyciṉa vaḻuti mutal kaṭuṅkōṉ īṟāyuḷḷa 89 pāṇṭiyarkaḷ aracāṭci ceytu koṇṭiruntārkaḷ. avaruḷ ēḻu pāṇṭiya rājarkaḷ kaviyaraṅkēṟiṉārkaḷ. avarkaḷuḷ cayamākīrttiyaṉākiya nilantarutiruviṟ pāṇṭiyaṉ kālattil eḻunūṟṟukkāvatam paraviyirunta 49 pāṇṭiya nāṭum kaṭalāl aḻintaṉa.
33. teṉṉāṭṭip paṟṟiyum teṉṉāṭṭiṉ pāṣiyip paṟṟiyum rājāṅkattip paṟṟiyum collum apippirāyatti ottuppārttal.
tamiḻ vaḻaṅkum teṉṉāṭṭilēyē tamatu jīviya nāḷellām taṅkiyiruntu tamiḻp pāṣiyit tīra vicāritta kālṭvel (Caldwell) attiyaṭcar avarkaḷ tamiḻiliruntu camaskirutattilum, epiru pāṣiyilum, āṅkilō cākcaṉ pāṣikaḷilum, cīttiya pāṣikaḷilum, maṟṟum cila pāṣikaḷilum vaḻaṅki varum vārttikaḷi vevvēṟākap pirittuc colvati itaṉ muṉ pārttōm. atikkoṇṭu maṟṟa ellāp pāṣikaḷukkum tamiḻp pāṣiyē āti ūṟṟāyirukkalāmeṉṟu itaṉ muṉ nām eṭuttuk kāṭṭiyirukkiṟōm. tamiḻp pāṣiyiṉ vārttikaḷ pala pāṣikaḷilum kalantiruppatik koṇṭu ātiyil tamiḻ makkaḷ pala nāṭukaḷukkup pōyirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟāvatu allatu maṟṟavar tamiḻ nāṭṭiṟku vantu kalantu uṟavāṭiyirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟāvatu eṇṇa iṭamirukkiṟatu. āṉāl ekkēl (Haeckel) eṉṉum tattuvacāstiriyār colluvati nām kavaṉippōmāṉāl teṉṉintiyāviṉ teṉ pākattilirunta lemūriyā eṉṉum kaṇṭam mikappūrvamāyuḷḷa paḻiya ulakameṉṟum, atuvē jaṉaṅkaḷ mutal mutal uṟpattiyāṉa paratīcāyiruntateṉṟum collukiṟār. pāṣikaḷ pala māṟi māṟi putitākkappaṭṭu vantālum avikaḷil kalantirukkum vārttikaḷik kavaṉittāl avikaḷ mikappūrvamāṉa oru pāṣiyiṉiṉṟē vantirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟum avvārttikaḷ maṉuṣa uṟpattiyiyum avarkaḷ uṟpattiyāṉa nāṭṭiyum annāṭṭil vaḻaṅki vanta pāṣiyiyum kaṇṭupiṭippataṟku mukkiya utaviyāyirukkumeṉṟum collukiṟār.
tamiḻ vārttikaḷ pala pāṣikaḷil kalantiruppatik koṇṭu tamiḻppāṣi taṉitta pāṣiyāyirukkalāmeṉṟu eṇṇum kālṭvel attiyaṭcar avarkaḷum teṉṉintiyāviṉ teṉpākattiliruntu aḻintupōṉa lemūriyāvē jaṉaṅkaḷ uṟpattiyāṉa paratīcu eṉṟu collum ekkēl tattuvacāstiriyārum iṟṟikkuc cumār 50 varuṣaṅkaḷukku uṭpaṭṭavarkaḷeṉṟu nām aṟivōm. āṉāl iṟṟikku cumār 1,800 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ iṟiyaṉārakap poruḷukku uri yeḻutiya nakkīrarum, akkālattirunta rājākkaḷ, tēcaṅkaḷ, kalikaḷ mutaliyavikaḷi oruvāṟu aṟintu koḷḷaṟkētuvāyirukkiṟa cilappatikāram eḻutiya iḷaṅkōvaṭikaḷum teṉṉintiyāviṉ teṉ pakkattiluḷḷa kumariyāṟṟiṟkum kumariyāṟṟiṉ ēḻunūṟṟuk kāvataṅkaḷukku appuṟamuḷḷa paḵṟuḷiyāṟṟiṟkum iṭiyilirunta 49 tamiḻ nāṭukaḷ aḻintu pōṉatākac collukiṟārkaḷ. annāṭukaḷukku teṉmaturi rājatāṉiyāka iruntateṉṟum, atil 89 pāṇṭiyarkaḷ 4,440 āṇṭu parampariyāy aracāṭci ceytum tamiḻccaṅkatti ātarittum vantārkaḷeṉṟum collukiṟārkaḷ. ivarkaḷ colliyiruppavi itaṉmuṉ pāṣiyip paṟṟiyum lemūriyāvip paṟṟiyum eḻutiya kālṭvel attiyaṭcar avarkaḷukkum ekkēl eṉṉum tattuva cāstiriyārukkum terintirukku māṉāl tamiḻ nāṭṭip paṟṟiyum tamiḻp pāṣiyip paṟṟiyum pāṇṭiya rājyattip paṟṟiyum atika nuṭpamāṉa viṣayaṅkaḷic colli yiruppārkaḷ.
immūṉṟiyum cīr tūkkip pārppōmēyāṉāl oruvar tamiḻp pāṣiyiṉ pūrvattiyum ataṉ mēṉmiyiyum maṟṟoruvar tamiḻp pāṣi vaḻaṅki vanta lemūriyā nāṭṭiyum ataṉ iyaṟkiyiyum paṟṟic collukiṟār. maṟṟavar, lemūriyā eṉṟu piṟar collum aṭiyāḷaṅkaḷōṭukūṭiya 49 tamiḻ nāṭukaḷiyum cila ūrkaḷiyum, malikaḷiyum, āṟukaḷiyum, rājarkaḷiyum, caṅkap pulavarkaḷiyum, akkālattu vaḻaṅki vanta nūlkaḷiyum cayamākīrttiyaṉākiya nilan tarutiruviṟ pāṇṭiyaṉ kālattil kaṭalāl aḻintupōṉatiyum collu kiṟārkaḷ. ivarkaḷ iṟṟikku 1,800 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉuḷḷavarkaḷ. itilum muṉṉatāka iṟṟikku cumār 8,700 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ teṉmaturiyil aracāṇṭu koṇṭirunta nilantaru tiruviṟpāṇṭiyaṉ aviyattil ataṅkōṭṭācāṉ muṉṉiliyil tolkāppiyam araṅkēṟṟiyateṉṟu tolkāppiya pāyirattil collappaṭuvatiyum nām kavaṉippōmāṉāl taṉittut taṉittu vicāritta palaruṭiya apip pirāyaṅkaḷ lemūriyā eṉṟu aḻikkappaṭum pūrva tamiḻnāṭākiya teṉpāṇṭi nāṭṭiṟkē poruntumeṉṟu teḷivākat terikiṟatu. itōṭu teṉmaturiyilum kapāṭapurattilum uttaramaturiyilum cumār 13,000 varuṣaṅkaḷāka iṟṟikku cumār 700 varuṣaṅkaḷ variyum pāṇṭiya rājarkaḷē aracāṭci ceytu koṇṭu vantirukkiṟārkaḷeṉpatip pūrva tamiḻ nūlkaḷiṉālum, ataṉpiṉ stalapurāṇaṅkaḷiṉālum teḷivāka aṟikiṟōm. cāṉṟōr kulattavarāṉa ivarkaḷ nāṭaṉ, nāṭāṉ, pāṇṭiyaṉ, teṉṉavaṉ, tamiḻnāṭaṉ eṉap pala pulavarkaḷāl pukaḻap peṟṟu vantārka ḷeṉṟum nām aṟikiṟōm. ivvaḷavu kālam āṇṭu koṇṭirunta rāja vamcattavarkaḷ ippōtu illāmal pōṉārkaḷāveṉṟu niṉikka nēriṭum. mūṉṟāvatu caṅkattiṉ kaṭiciyil pāṇṭiya rājaṉukkum caṅkap pulavarkaḷukkum cila maṉavaruttam nēriṭa caṅkaṅkalintu tamiḻippaṟṟi vicārikkum ūkkam kuṟintu tamiḻ aracarkaḷi aṟpamāy niṉikkum kaṣṭakālam ārampittaJ. ataṟkuppiṉ cumār āyira varuṣaṅkaḷāka aracāṭciyiruntālum pala uḷ kalakattiṉāl aracāṭci muṭintaJ. ataṉ piṉ vanta makammatiyarkaḷum, nāyakkarkaḷum maṟṟum evarum cāṉṟōr kulattavarukku catturukkaḷ āṉārkaḷ. ataṉāl avarkaḷ varavara pūmi urimi iḻantu coṟpattil jīvaṉam ceyyumpaṭiyāṉa nilikku vantārkaḷ. avarkaḷuḷ nāḷatu variyum nilittirukkum teyvapakti, rājapakti, tiriyam, uṇmi, muyaṟci, poruḷīṭṭal, aṭakkam mutaliya uttama kuṇaṅkaḷiyum avarkaḷukkuḷ vaḻaṅkivarum kulappeyarkaḷiyum paṭṭap peyarkaḷiyum kavaṉikkum aṟivāḷikaḷ intiyāviṉ teṉ pākattil mikuntu vacikkum tamiḻ makkaḷākiya cāṉṟōr kulattavarē pūrva pāṇṭiyarāja vamcattavarkaḷeṉṟu kāṇpārkaḷ.
uṇmiyuḷḷa tēvapaktaṉāyirunta tāvītu aracaṉum mikunta ñāṉi eṉṟu eṇṇappaṭum avaṉ kumāraṉ cālōmōṉ aracaṉum pakkattu tēcattu rājākkaḷ kappaṅkaṭṭa pirapalamāy aracāṭci ceytu koṇṭirunta yūtēyā rājyam aḻiya, tirumpa avvirājyattukku varum eṇṇamaṟṟa ēḻikaḷāyppōṉa yūtarkaḷiyum, oru kālattil mikavum pirapalamāṉa rājākkaḷāyiruntu ippōtu rājyam iḻantu celvamum iḻantu vaṟiñarāy cañcarikkum, amerikkāviluḷḷa cikappuniṟa intiyarkaḷiyum (Red Indians), āppirikkāvil oru kālattil mikunta celvākku peṟṟu neṭunāḷ aracāṭci ceytu koṇṭiruntu piṟaku avi yāvum iḻantu mikattāḻnta jātiyār eṉṟu eṇṇappaṭum kēvala niliyil taṟkālam irukkum cūlu, kāvpar (Zulu, Kaffir) eṉṉum teṉ āppirikka jātiyāriyum aṟivāḷikaḷ aṟivārkaḷ. itupōlavē pūrva tamiḻ aracarkaḷāy viḷaṅkiya pāṇṭiya rājākkaḷum avarkaḷ aracāṭciyum pōṉapiṉ avarkaḷ piṉṉaṭiyārum mika ēḻikaḷākit tamiḻ nāṭṭiṉ ōr pākamākiya teṉṉintiyāviṉ teṉ pakkattil nāḷatuvariyum jaṉapperukkuṭaṉ nilittu viruttiyaṭintu varukiṟārkaḷ eṉpatiyum aṟivāḷikaḷ aṟiyāmaṟpōkārkaḷ.
mēṣattil uccaṉāyirunta cūriyaṉ paṭippaṭiyāyk kuṟintu tulā rāciyil nīcaṉākiṟatu pōlavum piṉ paṭippaṭiyāy vaḷarntu mēṣattil uccaṉākiṟatu pōlavum, oru kālattil taliyāyiruntatu, maṟṟoru kālattil vālākavum, vālāka iruntatu taliyākavum māṟum irutali maṇiyaṉip pōlavum ituvumākiyatu ulaka iyaṟkitāṉē. mēl kīḻākavum kīḻ mēlākavum ākuṅkālattil ataṟkēṟpa naṭantu koḷvatu maṉuṣa iyaṟki. rājarkaḷ muṟiyaṭikkappaṭṭu tam kuṭikaḷē tamakkuc catturukkaḷākuṅ kālattil kāṭukaḷil maṟintu kāy kaṉikaḷit tiṉṟu viṟaku veṭṭi jīvaṉam paṇṇuvatiyum nām kēḷvippaṭṭirukkiṟōm.
oru rākattil vaḻaṅkum curaṅkaḷ mūṉṟu stāyikaḷilum ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇa katiyāy cañcāram peṟṟu vāti camvāti vitippaṭi uṇṭākum pala pirastārattiṉāl āṉantam uṇṭākkuvatupōla aṇṭa puvaṉa carācaraṅkaḷukkuk karttaṉāṉa teyvattiṟku ituvum oru tiruviḷiyāṭṭē.
ippaṭi pāṇṭiyarājyam aḻikiyil caṅkīta nūlkaḷ palavum aḻintu pōyiṉa. atil tappip piḻittavar pala tēcattil ceṉṟu kuṭiyēṟiṉārkaḷ. avarkaḷ vaḻaṅki vanta tamiḻppāṣi kālañcellac cella muṟṟilum māṟi vēṟu pāṣiyāyiṟṟu. appaṭiyāṉālum tamiḻp pāṣiyiṉ cātāraṇa coṟkaḷ palavum appāṣikaḷil vaḻaṅki varukiṉṟaṉa. atupōlavē teṉṉintiya caṅkītattiṟkuriya cila amcaṅkaḷ māṟit tēcattukkut tēcam kāṉam vēṟupaṭṭālum, teṉṉintiya caṅkītattil vaḻaṅki vanta mukkiya curaṅkaḷē māṟāmal vaḻaṅki varukiṉṟaṉa. curutikaḷ ittaṉiyirukkalā meṉṟu vēṟu cilar collik koṇṭiruntālum, avikaḷi upayōkikkum vaḻiyum vāttiyattiṉ utaviyumillāmal vāyccollōṭu māttiram niṟkiṟārkaḷ. āṉāl teṉṉintiyāvilō paṉṉiraṇṭu curaṅkaḷiyum avikaḷiṉiṭiyē vaḻaṅkum nuṭpamāṉa curutikaḷiyumuṭiya kāṉam ceytukoṇṭu varukiṟārkaḷ. taṅkaḷ aṉupōkattilirukkum ammēṉmiyāṉa kāṉam pūrva tamiḻ nūlkaḷil collappaṭṭiruppaviyeṉṟu aṟiyārkaḷ. avikaḷ cērum muṟi iṉṉateṉṟum atuvē caṅkītattiṟku piratāṉa meṉṟum niṉikka maṟantu pōṉārkaḷ. parampariyāyuḷḷa pāṭamē taṟkālam varikkum uṇmiyik kāppāṟṟik koṇṭu niṟkiṟaJ. teṉṉintiya caṅkītattil vaḻaṅki varum rakaciyaṅkaḷi vaṭa pāṣiyil eḻutiyavarkaḷ ivviṣayattil tavaṟip pōṉārkaḷeṉpati itaṉpiṉ pārppōm. caṅkītattukkuriya peyarkaḷiyum irākaṅkaḷiṉ peyarkaḷiyum muṟṟilum māṟṟip pūrvat tamiḻppeyarkaḷ illāmal camaskirutattil pustakaṅkaḷ eḻuti vittārkaḷ. ivvuṇmiyi itaṉpiṉ aṟivōm. ataṉ muṉ intiya caṅkītattippaṟṟiyum atil vaṭatēcattu caṅkītattip paṟṟiyum intustāṉ caṅkītattippaṟṟiyum teṉṉintiya caṅkītattip paṟṟiyum maṟṟavar collum cila apippirāyaṅkaḷi nām kavaṉippatu nallateṉṟu niṉikkiṟēṉ.
V. intiya caṅkītattip paṟṟip palar collum vevvēṟu apippirāyam
1. intiya caṅkītattip paṟṟiya potuvāṉa apippirāyam.
teṉṉintiyāviṟkut teṟkēyirunta lemūriyā eṉṟu taṟkālattil aḻikkappaṭum ātipāṇṭināṭu nāṟpattoṉpatum kaṭalāl aḻikkap paṭṭapiṉ, pūrvattil atu aṭintirunta ellā nākarikamum muṟṟilum aḻintaṉa. atippaṟṟip piṟar collit terintu koḷḷavāvatu ōlic cācaṉaṅkaḷ ceppup paṭṭayaṅkaḷ kalveṭṭukaḷ mutaliyavikaḷiṉāl aṟintu koḷḷavāvatu ētuvillāmal pōyviṭṭatu. iruntālum, aṅkaṅkē citaṟik kiṭanta cila nūlkaḷiṉālum iṭiccaṅkattilum kaṭiccaṅkattilu mirunta vitvāṉkaḷiṉāl collappaṭṭa coṟpa ātāraṅkaḷiṉālum koñcam terikiṟatēyaṉṟi muṟṟum teriyavilli. āṉālum parampari yāyk kēḷvi mūlamāyk kāppāṟṟappaṭṭu varum caṅkītattik koṇṭum cila kittoḻilkaḷik koṇṭu ippaṭit tērcciyuḷḷa oru kāla miruntatu eṉṟu yāvarum niṉikka iṭamirukkiṟatu. ippaṭi niṉippavarkaḷiṉ apippirāyam oṉṟukkoṉṟu cila pākaṅkaḷil vittiyācamāyiruntālum pūrvīkattilirunta uṇmiyi oppuk koḷvataṟkup pōtumāṉaviyeṉṟē niṉikkiṟēṉ. kiṟistu paramāttumā ulakil avatarikkum kālattil kuruccantirayōkamum cantira maṅkaḷayōkamum peṟṟa caṉi mīṉattil niṟka oru periya vāṉajōti tōṉṟumeṉṟum, accōti tōṉṟum rāci pākikaḷukku ottatāṉa pūpākattil mika makattuvamuḷḷa oru avatāra puruṣaṉ piṟappāreṉṟum, lakkaṉātipati cūriyaṉ nīca paṅkarājayōkamum putaṉ uccamum puttira stāṉattil rākuvum niṟpatikkoṇṭu avar irāṭca´ aippaci mātam 18m tēti 483/4 nāḻikikku jaṉaṉamāvār eṉṟum kaṇitattāl aṟintu avar piṟakkiṟa kālattil avarik kaṇṭu kāṇikkikaḷuṭaṉ taricikka vēṇṭu meṉṟu vekutūram yāttiri ceytu taricitta kiḻakku tēcattu cāstirikaḷiṉ kaṇitattiṉ nuṭpamum vāṉa cāstirattiṉ niccayamum avatāra puruṣarkaḷit taricikka virumpiya avarkaḷ paktiyum nām pārttu āccariyappaṭa vēṇṭiyatāyirukkiṟatu. ivarkaḷ tamiḻ mūvaracarkaḷā yirukkalāmeṉṟu ūkikkappaṭukiṟārkaḷ. kaṇitattil mikunta nuṭpamāṉa piṉṉa pākaṅkaḷukkum 36 stāṉaṅkaḷ vari periya lakkaṅkaḷukkum peyar vittu aḻikkiṟa vaḻakkamāṉatu mikavum koṇṭāṭakkūṭiyatē. appaṭiyē ciṟpa cāstirattilum caṅkītattilum mikunta pāṇṭittiya maṭin tiruntārkaḷ. pattukkuppattu caturamāyuḷḷa oru melliya cālviyi oru kikkuḷ aṭaṅkumpaṭiyākac ceytiruppārkaḷāṉāl avarkaḷ kit toḻiliṉ tiṟamiyip paṟṟic collavum vēṇṭumā? oru nelliṉ umiyi iraṇṭu pākamākap pirittu, uḷḷirukkum ariciyil vikṉēsparar corūpamum cuppiramaṇiyar corūpamum enta amcattilum kuṟivupaṭāmal ceytu, paḻiyapaṭi atil vittu mūṭit taṅkaḷ kittoḻil cāmarttiyattik kāṭṭakkūṭiyavarkaḷum, mikunta palamuṭiyaṉavum catturukkaḷ toṭuṅkālattil toṭṭa anēkari atam paṇṇip pōṭakkūṭiyaṉavumāṉa poṟikaḷiyum patumikaḷiyum ceyyak kūṭiyavarkaḷum anēkariruntārkaḷ. ippaṭik kittoḻil, cittiram, caṅkītam, yōkacātaṉi, vittiyam mutaliyavikaḷ intiyāvil oru kālattil aṅkaṅkēyiruntu vantaṉaveṉṟu yāvarum colvārkaḷ. āṉāl ivvariya vittikaḷ taṅkaḷ pirāṇaṉ pōkiṟa varikkum taṅkaḷ contap piḷḷikaḷukkuṅkūṭac colli vikka maṉamillātavarkaḷiṉāl aṅkaṅkē maṟintu pōyviṭṭaṉa. avikaḷ pūrvacarittiraṅkaḷi aṟiya virumpum cilarukku vicāraṇiyiṉmēl kiṭikkak kūṭiyaṉavākavum ūkikkak kūṭiyaṉavākavu mirukkiṉṟaṉavēyoḻiya, tiṭṭamāy varakkūṭiyavi mikac cilavē. iruntālum, carittira ārāyccikkārar avarkaḷukkuk kiṭittatākac collum kuṟippukaḷil cila kavaṉikkat takuntavi.
Hindu Music and the Gayan Samaj Part II. P. 8.
“But upon the testimony of works of great antiquity lying around us (some 4,000 to 8,000 years old), we can safely affirm that Hindu music was developed into a system in very ancient times, in times of which we have no genuine records, in times when all other nations of the world were struggling with the elements for existence, in times when Hindu Rishis were enjoying the fruits of civilization and occupying themselves with the contemplation of the mighty powers of the eternal Brahma.”
“nammiccuṟṟi nām pārkkum 8,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉuḷḷa paḻamiyāṉa nūlkaḷiṉ cākṣiyatti vittuk koṇṭu intiya caṅkītamāṉatu ātikālattilēyē oru muṟiyāka stāpaṉamāṉateṉṟu nām tiriyamāyc collalām. ippaṭic caṅkītam uṇṭāṉa kālamāṉatu namakku atippaṟṟittiṭṭamāṉa carittira cāsaṉaṅkaḷ illātakālam. antakkālamāṉatu ulakattiṉ maṟṟellā jātiyārum tiṭa nilimikku vēṇṭiya ētukkaḷkūṭa illāmal tattaḷittuk koṇṭirunta kālam. antak kālamāṉatu intu makariṣikaḷ nākarīkamaṭintatiṉāl uṇṭākum palaṉkaḷip pūraṇamāy aṉupavittuk koṇṭu pirammattiṉuṭiya aḷavilaṭaṅkā vallamiyi eppōtun tiyāṉittuk koṇṭu taṅkaḷ vāḻnāḷi celavaḻitta kālam.”
itil iṟṟikku 8,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉum intiyāvil caṅkītam iruntati paḻimiyāṉa nūlkaḷ collukiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu collukiṟār. caṅkītattil māttiramalla, necavilum, niliyuḷḷa cāyaṅkaḷ pōṭuvatilum, vitamvitamāṉa vāttiyak karuvikaḷ ceyvatilum tērcciyaṭin tiruntārkaḷeṉṟu piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil terintu koḷḷalām.
Mill’s History of Br. India, Vol 1, Page II.
“Of the exquisite degree of perfection to which the Hindus have carried the productions of the loom, it would be idle to offer any description.
Among the arts of the Hindus, that of printing and dyeing their cloths has been celebrated; and the beauty and brilliancy as well as durability of the colours they produce are worthy of particular prise.”
Dr. Tennant says, “If we are to judge merely from the number of instruments and the frequency with which they apply them, the Hindus might be regarded as considerable proficients in music.”
“tariyupayōkittal, tuṇi neytal ākiya vittikaḷil intukkaḷ ati campūraṇa tērcciyaṭintiruntavarkaḷākiyāl atippaṟṟi iṅku vistarippatu acāttiyamāṉa kāriyam. intukkaḷ tērcciyaṭinta vittikaḷuḷ tuṇikaḷil cittiraṅkaḷi acciṭuvatum avikaḷukku cāyam tōyppatum veku vicēṣittavi. antac cāyaṅkaḷ aḻakilum, pakaṭṭilum, nīṭittu nilittiruppatilum vicēṣittiruntaṉavākac collappaṭukiṉṟaṉa.
intukkaḷ upayōkappaṭuttukiṟa vāttiyaṅkaḷiṉ ilakkattik kavaṉikkiṟapōtum avarkaḷ aṭikkaṭi avikaḷi upayōkikkiṟa viṣayattik kavaṉikkiṟapōtum avarkaḷ caṅkītattil vekutūram tērcciyaṭintiruntārkaḷ eṉṟu teḷivākiṟatu, eṉṟu ṉuca. kūnanyēvē collukiṟār.”
mēlum āriyarkaḷ intiyāvil paṭiyeṭuttu varuvataṟku muṉṉēyē intiyāvilirunta pūrva kuṭikaḷ mikunta nākarīkamuṭiyavarkaḷā yiruntārkaḷeṉṟu piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
India through the ages. Steels P.I.
“Even if we go so far back as B.C. 2,000, the voices of men who have lived and died are still to be heard in the earlier hymns of the Rig-Veda.”
“These same hymns incidentally tell us that the Aryan invaders found a people in India civilised enough to have towns and disciplined troops, to have weapons and banners, women whose ornaments were of gold, poisoned arrows whose heads were of some metal that was probably iron.”
“intutēca carittirattil rikku vētattil kaṭavuḷukkuc collappaṭum pirārttaṉikaḷik kavaṉittup pārppōmāṉāl antak kālattil  atāvatu ēṟakkuṟiya 4,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉirunta maṉitariṉ paḻakka vaḻakkam mutaliyavikaḷ namakku naṉṟāyt teriya varukiṟatu. atē pirārttaṉikaḷiliruntu nām maṟṟum kāriyaṅkaḷiyum aṟikiṟōm. ateṉṉaveṉil, āriyarkaḷ intiyāvil paṭiyeṭuttu vantapōtu aṅkirunta jaṉaṅkaḷ nākarīkattukkuriya anēka amcaṅkaḷi uṭiyavarkaḷā yiruntatik kaṇṭārkaḷ. avi yāviyeṉil, intukkaḷ nakaraṅkaḷil vacittārkaḷ. naṉṟāyp payiṟṟuvikkappaṭṭa cēṉikaḷ avarkaḷukku iruntaṉa. avarkaḷuṭiya stirīkaḷ taṅka nakikaḷiyaṇintiruntārkaḷ. nuṉiyil viṣamēṟṟappaṭṭa irumpup pūṇkaḷiyuṭiya ampukaḷi upayōkittiruntārkaḷ eṉpavikaḷē.”
mēṟkaṇṭa varikaḷi nām kavaṉikkiyil āriyar intiyāviṟku paṭiyeṭuttu vantapōtē teṉṉintiyāviluḷḷa jaṉaṅkaḷ rājya pārattiṟkuriya kōṭṭi kottaḷaṅkaḷuṭiya nakaraṅkaḷil yuttap payiṟcipeṟṟa cēṉikaḷuṭaṉum āṭi āparaṇaṅkaḷiṉ aḻakōṭum viṣam ēṟṟappeṟṟa āyutaṅkaḷuṭaṉum vacittu vantārkaḷeṉṟu collukiṟār. tāṅkaḷ kuṭiyirukkum tēcattiṉ cītōṣṇa stitiyum pulpūṇṭu tāvaram tāṉiyaṅkaḷiṉ kuṟiviyum aṟintu tāṅkaḷum taṅkaḷ āṭu māṭukaḷum piḻippataṟkēṟṟa vacatiyāṉa iṭam tēṭi vanta āriyar, yākañ ceytum cōmaraca pāṉañ ceytum vantārkaḷeṉṟu vētattiṉālēyē teḷivāka aṟikiṟōm. ivarkaḷ varuvataṟkumuṉ teṉṉintiyāvil uyirkaḷik kollā viratattiyum māmicam pucikkāta civaneṟiyiyum mēṉmiyākak koṇṭoḻukiyavar mikutiyāyiruntārkaḷeṉṟu pūrva tamiḻ nūlkaḷ mūlamāy aṟikiṟōm.
nāḷaṭivil avarkaḷ taṅkaḷ vētattiluḷḷa pirārttaṉikaḷ collum caṅkīta muṟiyi viṭṭut teṉṉintiyāviṟkuriya kāṉa muṟiyi mikutiyāyp payiṉṟu varukiṟārkaḷ eṉpatiyum nām iṅkē maṟantu pōkakkūṭāJ.
caṅkītamāṉatu intiyarkaḷuṭiya nākarīkatti virutti paṇṇiṉa cāstiraṅkaḷil oṉṟāyiruntateṉṟum iṟṟikkuc cumār 2,300 varuṣaṅkaḷukkumuṉ pāṇiṉiyiṉ kālattilēyē oḻuṅkuṭaṉ amikkap paṭṭiruntateṉṟum intiyāviliruntē ekiptu (Egypt) pāracīkam (Persia) arēpiyā (Arabia) kirēk (Greece) mutaliya tēcaṅkaḷukkuk koṇṭu pōkappaṭṭateṉṟum piṉvarum cila vākkiyaṅkaḷāl kāṇalām.
W.W. Hunter’s The Indian Empire P. 110-112.
INDIAN MUSIC.
“The Indian art of Music (Gandharva Veda) was destined to exercise a wider influence. A regular system of notation had been worked out before the age of Panini (350 B.C.), and the seven notes were designatred by their initial letters. This notation passed from the Brahmans through the Persians to Arabia and was thence introduced into European music by Guido L. Arezzo at the beginning of the 11th Century. Some indeed, suppose that our modern word gamut comes not from the Greek letter gamma, but from the Indian gama in Prakrit; (in Sanskrit, Grama) literally a musical scale”
“kāntarva vētam eṉṟaḻikkappaṭṭa intiya caṅkītamāṉatu intiyaruṭiya nākarīkatti virutti paṇṇiṉa anēka karuvikaḷil cirēṣṭamāṉaJ. ki.mu.  350 varuṣattukku muṟpaṭṭa pāṇiṉi (ñayanini) muṉivar kālattilēyē, intiya caṅkītamāṉatu oruvita oḻuṅkuṭaṉ amikkappaṭṭu, irākaṅkaḷi curappaṭuttum muṟi, sapta curaṅkaḷiyum avikaḷit toṭaṅkum eḻuttukkaḷāl aḻittal mutaliya oḻuṅkukaḷuṭaṉum iruntatākat terikiṟatu. irākaṅkaḷic curaṅkaḷāka eḻutum immuṟi yāṉatu pirāmaṇarkaḷiṭamiruntu pāracīkar mūlamāy arēpiyāvukkuk koṇṭu pōkappaṭṭu, appuṟam avarkaḷiṭamiruntu 11vatu nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ tuvakkattil, Guido L. Arezzo  eṉpavarāl airōppiya caṅkītattil amikkappaṭṭaJ. taṟkālam caṅkītattil vaḻaṅki varum ‘‘Gamut’’ eṉṉum patamāṉatu kirēkku eḻuttākiya ‘Gamma’ eṉpatiliruntu uṇṭāṉatu eṉṟu cilar apippirāyappaṭuvatupōl niṉiyāmal intiya patamākiya ‘kirāmam’ (atāvatu ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇam) eṉṉum patattiliruntu uṇṭāṉatākac cilar niṉikkiṟārkaḷ.”
kiṟistuvukkumuṉ 2,500kkum 1,400kkum naṭuviluḷḷa pirāmaṇiya kālam (kirantaṅkaḷ uṇṭāṉa kālam) eṉṟu aḻikkappaṭum 4,400 ām varuṣattilēyē intiya caṅkītattukkuriya ārōkaṇa avarō kaṇaṅkaḷiruntaṉaveṉṟum iṉṉum nuṭpamāy vicārittāl intiya caṅkītattiṉ kālam mikappūrvamāṉateṉṟu aṟiyalāmeṉṟum caṅkīta cāstirattiṉ anēka pākaṅkaḷ intiyāvilēyē jeṉittaṉaveṉṟum piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷāl aṟiyalām.
Hindu Musical Scale and 22 Srutis By K.B. Deval Page I.
“It might be stated here at the outset that the Hindu musical scale dates as far back as the Brahman Period which is calculated, according to modern researches, to extend from 2,500 B.C. to 1,400 B.C. It is possible that further researches might modify this date or might, perhaps carry it still farther back. But we may be certin that our scale dates farther back than the Greek scale which is acknowledged to be the parent of modern European scales. Capt. Day in his ‘Music of Southern India; observes :- The Historial Strabo shows that the Greek influence extended to India, and also that Greek musicians of a certin school attributed the grater part of the science of music to India.”
“ārampattil ippōtu nām avaciyamāyc colla vēṇṭiyateṉṉa veṉṟāl intiya caṅkītattil vaḻaṅkum ārōkaṇaṅkaḷ ‘pirāmaṇiya kālam’ eṉṟu collappaṭṭa atāvatu ki.mu.  2,500kkum 1,400kku iṭiyiluḷḷa kālattilēyē upayōkattiliruntaṉa eṉṟu taṟkāla cāstira ārāycciyiṉ mūlamāyt teriya varukiṟatu. iṉṉum itilum anēka vicēṣamāṉa ārāyccikaḷ naṭakkumāṉāl intiya caṅkīta kālam iṉṉum muṉṉukku uḷḷatāyt teriya varalām. āṉāl nām stiramāyc collakkūṭiyatu, taṟkālam airōppāvil vaḻaṅkum ārōkaṇaṅkaḷukkut tāyāka eṇṇappaṭum kirēkka ārōkaṇaṅkaḷukku muṉṉāṉa kālattilum intiya ārōkaṇaṅkaḷ iruntaṉa eṉpatām. ‘teṉṉintiya caṅkītam’ eṉṟa nūlil Captin Day  eṉpavar ‘carittira cāstiriyākiya Strabo collukiṟapaṭi kirēkkaruṭiya caṅkītattiṉ pirakācam intiyā variyil eṭṭiyatu eṉṟum kirēk vitvāṉkaḷil oru cārār caṅkīta cāstirattiṉ atikamāṉa pākam intiyāviliruntu jeṉittatu eṉṟu oppuk koṇṭārkaḷ eṉṟum carittirakkārarākiya ḷuvacayabṭi collukiṟār’ eṉṟu kūṟukiṟār.”
intiya caṅkītam iṟṟikkuc caṟṟēṟakkuṟiya 3,000 - 4,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ pirāmaṇiya kālattiluṇṭāṉatākavum kiṟistuvukku 350 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉuḷḷa pāṇiṉi eṉpavar kālattiliruntatākavum pirāmaṇarkaḷiṭamiruntu maṟṟa tēcattārukkup paraviṉatākavum mēṟkaṇṭa varikaḷil kāṇkiṟōm.
atil iṉṉum vicēṣamāṉa ārāycci ceytāl itaṟku veku kālattiṟku muṉṉālēyē intiyāvil caṅkītamiruntateṉṟu collalām eṉkiṟār. tolkāppiyattil nālvaki nilaṅkaḷiṉ karupporuḷkaḷ colla vanta iṭattil nālvakiyāṉa yāḻkaḷiyum paṟṟi viparañ collukiṟār. atil marutam, kuṟiñci, neytal, pāli eṉṟa nālu yāḻkaḷiṉ curutimuṟikaḷik kavaṉippōmāṉāl ṣaṭjamam, mattimam, pañcamam, niṣātam ākiya nālu curaṅkaḷi curutiyāka vittuk koṇṭu kāṉampaṇṇappaṭuvateṉṟu itaṉ piṉviparamāy aṟivōm. tolkāppiyar taṉakku muṉ vaḻaṅki vanta caṅkīta nuṭpattiyē aṅkē coṉṉār. itippaṟṟi akastiyarum nāratarum virivāka nūl eḻutiyiruntatākavum terikiṟatu. itu teṉmaturiyil caṅkamirunta kālamām. caṅkam uṇṭāvataṟku muṉpē caṅkītamiruntirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟu nām tiṭṭamākac collalām. itteṉmaturi aḻikiyil iṅkiruntōr muṉṉamē tām kaṟṟukkoṇṭa ciṟ cila curaṅkaḷit taṅkaḷ tēcattil virutti ceytirukkalāmeṉṟum paḻiyapaṭi teṉṉintiyāviluḷḷa kāṉattik koṇṭē taṅkaḷ kāṉatti virutti ceytu koṇṭārkaḷeṉṟum nām aṟiya vēṇṭum. iti aṟivuḷḷa evarum oppukkoḷvārkaḷ. teṉṉintiyāviṉ caṅkītattil vaḻaṅki varum curaṅkaḷi pitākaras (Pythagoras) eṉṉum kirēkka tattuva cāstiri 2/3, 3/4 eṉṟa aḷaviṉ mūlamāyk koṇṭu pōṉār eṉpati itaṉpiṉ aṟivōm. curaṅkaḷiluḷḷa veku nuṭpamāṉa kūṭutal kuṟitali aṟiyāmal palavitamāṉa cantēkaṅkaḷ jaṉittirukkiṟateṉṟu piṉ pārppōm.
intiyāvil yākañ ceyyumpoḻutu iraṇṭu pirāmaṇarkaḷ vīṇi vācikka, maṟṟoru pirāmaṇar vētaṅkaḷip pāṭikkoṇṭirukka vēṇṭum. appaṭippaṭṭa kāṉamillāta oru yākam pirayōcaṉamilli yeṉṟu piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Hindu Music and the Gayan Samaj, Part I. P. 21.
“The system of instrumental music was in practice in the earliest times of the history of our land and it was held that sacrifical rites (yagams) had no efficacy unless two Brahmans played upon the Vina in concert with a third Brahman singing.”
“nam nāṭṭiṉ āti carittira kālan toṭaṅkic caṅkīta vāttiyaṅkaḷi upayōkappaṭuttum muṟi ēṟpaṭṭiruntirukkiṟaJ. yākam ceyyumpōtu iraṇṭu pirāmaṇarkaḷ vīṇiyil vācikka mūṉṟāvatu pirāmaṇar oruvar cērntu pāṭiṉāloḻiya anta yākattil oru pirayōcaṉamumilli eṉṟu koḷḷappaṭṭaJ.”
intiya caṅkītam maṉatic cāntappaṭuttit teyvattiṉiṭattil maṉati niliniṟkac ceykiṟateṉṟu piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil pārkkalām.
Hindu Music and the Gayan Samaj, Part II, P. 30.
“Music is one of the most innocent and elevating indoor amusements. It affords pleasure to all and delights specially those who cultivate and develop a taste for it. It softens and refines the mind and elevates its devotion to the Creator of the Universe. Relying upon the testimony only of works of great antiquity lying around us some 4,000 to 8,000 years old, we can safely affirm that Hindu music is of very ancient origin, and was developed into a system and science when Hindu Rishis resided and meditated in the primoeval forests, and inugurated civilization.”
“caṅkītamāṉatu kālatti nalvaḻiyil celavaḻikkak kūṭiyatum, maṉacip paravacappaṭuttak kūṭiyatumāṉa ōr nalla paḻakkam. atu yāvarukkum cantōṣattit tarattakkatum, mukkiyamāy atil viruppamuṭiyavarāy atik kaṟṟōrukku atika āṉantattit tarattakkatumāyirukkiṟatu. atu maṉatic cāntappaṭuttic cuttappaṭutti ulakip paṭitta ciruṣṭikariṭattil ammaṉati vacappaṭuttukiṟaJ. 4,000 varuṣa mutal 8,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉē atu ēṟpaṭṭatu eṉṟu nammic cuṟṟiyirukkum paḻamiyāṉa cāstira nūlkaḷ mūlamāy aṟinta nām intiya caṅkītamāṉatu veku paḻamiyāṉateṉṟum, intiya makā riṣikaḷ kāṭukaḷil vacittu tapacu ceytu vanta kālattilēyē oru cāstira muṟiyāy ēṟpaṭuttappaṭṭirunta teṉṟum, cakala nākarīkattukkum atu āti uṟpattiyāyiruntateṉṟum taṭiyillāmal collalām.”
mēṟkaṇṭa varikaḷi nām kavaṉikkiyil caṅkītamāṉatu intiyāvil mikap paḻamiyāṉa cāstirameṉṟum tapōtaṉarkaḷāl appiyācikkap paṭṭum cāstiramāy eḻutappaṭṭum vantateṉṟum cakala nākarīkattiṟkum atu āti uṟpattiyāyiruntateṉṟum collukiṟār. ulakiṉ uṟpattikku nātamē ātikāraṇamā yiruntatatupōla oru maṉuṣaṉiṉ nākarīkattiṟkum paktikkum avaṉ uḷḷattilirun tuṇṭākum nātamē mutalāyirukkiṟatu. eṅkumuḷḷatāy viḷaṅkum oruvaṉē ellā jīvappirāṇikaḷiṉ uḷḷattilumirukkiṟāṉ. avvuḷḷam ammeypporuḷi (uṇmiyi) yuṭiyatā yirukkumāṉāl ellāmuḷḷatākavum atu kuṟiyak kuṟiyap paṭippaṭiyāy oṉṟumillātatākavum ākiṟati nām aṟivōm. nalla uḷḷam illātavaṉ nalla vārttikaḷi yillātavaṉākiṟāṉ. uṇmi avaṉ vāyiṉiṉṟu varukiṟatilli eṉpatiyum nām aṟivōm. oruvaṉ coṉṉa iṉiya vacaṉaṅkaḷum iṉpamāṉa ōcikaḷum coṉṉa avaṉiṭattilēyē tirumpap pōyc cērukiṉṟaṉa. ippaṭi māṟimāṟi uṇṭākum ceyalē oruvaṉil nilittirukkumpoḻutu avaṉ āṉanta muṭiyavaṉākiṟāṉ. uṇmiyāṉa āṉantattiṟku uṇmiyāṉa nātamē ātikāraṇam. ivvuṇmi viruttikku mūlakāraṇattiyē ātmam eṉṟum param eṉṟum aṟivuṭiyōrkaḷ colvārkaḷ. uṇmi yiṉiṉṟu tōṉṟum āṉantamum āṉantattiṉiṉṟu tōṉṟum kītamum evviṭatti liruntu uṇ ṭāyiṟṟō anta iṭattiyē alaṅkārappaṭuttukiṟaJ. atāvatu atil uṇmiyāy viḷaṅkum teyvattiṉiṭattil nilikkumpaṭic ceykiṟaJ. teyvattilēyē nilittirunta tapōtaṉarkaḷ caṅkītatti utaviyākak koṇṭu taṅkaḷ tapacic ceytu vantārkaḷ. jīvakāruṇyam, aṭakkam, poṟumi, kīḻppaṭital, aṉpu mutaliya uttama kuṇaṅkaḷ viḷaṅkit teyva puttirarāka ulakattil pirakācittārkaḷ. itaṟku māṟāka atāvatu uṇmikku māṟākac ceyyum kāṉaṅkaḷ ceytavaṉip pala allal ceytu poyyaṉākkumeṉpatu niccayam.
vētamuṇṭāṉa kālattilēyē avikaḷ rākattōṭu pāṭappaṭṭa teṉṟum oru curattik koṇṭum iraṇṭu curattik koṇṭum mūṉṟu curattik koṇṭum kāṉam paṇṇik koṇṭiruntārkaḷeṉṟum ataṉpiṉ aintu, āṟu, ēḻu eṉṉum curaṅkaḷiyum antanta curaṅkaḷukkup poruttamuḷḷa mēl curaṅkaḷiyum cērttu utātta, aṉutātta, svaritam eṉṟu vaḻaṅki vantārkaḷeṉṟum piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷāl terikiṟatu.
Hindu Music and the Gayan Samaj, P. 4.
“As has been already observed, our Rishi ancestors, in very early times, had been chanting vedic hymns and setting them to music, and mention of this fact in the vedas is frequently made in the Rigveda, as for instance, in such assertions as Archino Gayanti, Ganthino Gayanti and Samino Gayanti. Agin, in later times Panini and other acharyas or teachers describe the science, and all this goes to show distinctly that music was cultivated among our ancestors to a large extent, and whith great assiduity and taste. The Arka system of music, it is sid, was based upon only one note, the Gathika system upon two, and the Samika upon three, and to these was subsequently added another system termed the swarantara (another note) based upon four notes. There was thus vital difference between the system adopted by the Rishis and those adopted by the Acharyas; and Panini, to make up the difference, while regarding in his vyakarana Sutras, the three swaras” (Udatta, Anudatta and Swarita), as the min notes, points out in his (Siksha), the connection between the system by three and those by seven notes thus :
(Udatta) includes (ni and ga)
(Anudatta) includes (ri and dha) and
(Swarita) includes (sa, ma, pa)
“nām muṉṉē coṉṉapaṭi, nammuṭiya muṉṉōrkaḷāyirunta riṣimārkaḷ ātikālattilēyē vētattiluḷḷa pirārttaṉikaḷip pāṭi curappaṭutti vittirukkiṟārkaḷ. itippaṟṟi rikku vētattil anēka iṭaṅkaḷil aṭikkaṭi collappaṭukiṟatu. utāraṇamāka, arcciṉōkāyanti, kāyantiṉōkāyanti mutaliya muṟikaḷ collappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. piṟkālaṅkaḷil pāṇiṉi mutaliya āciriyarkaḷum vitvāṉkaḷum inta vittiyi vistarittuc colliyirukkiṟārkaḷ. iviyellām nām kavaṉikkumpōtu, caṅkītamāṉatu namatu muṉṉōrāl veku cirattiyōṭum piriyattōṭum virutti paṇṇappaṭṭu vantatu eṉṟu niccayamāyc collalām. ‘arkkā’ eṉṉum muṟiyāṉatu orē curattiyum ‘kātikā’ eṉpatu iraṇṭu curattiyum, ‘cāmikā’ eṉpatu mūṉṟu curattiyum uṭittāyiruntatu eṉṟum, ivikaḷōṭukūṭa  nālu curattiyuṭiya ‘svarāntam’ eṉṉum vēṟoru muṟi uṇṭāyiṟṟu eṉṟum terikiṟatu. ākiyāl, riṣikaḷiṉ muṟikkum ācāriyariṉ muṟikkum aṉanta vittiyācamiruntatāl inta iraṇṭu muṟiyiyum campantappaṭuttum paṭiyāka eṉṉa ceytāreṉṟāl, tammuṭiya ‘viyākaraṇa cūttirattil’ mūṉṟu curaṅkaḷākiya utāttam, aṉutāttam, svaritam eṉpavikaḷi mūla curaṅkaḷāka vittuk koṇṭum, tammuṭiya “ciikṣa”yil anta mūṉṟu curaṅkaḷukkum captacuraṅkaḷukkumuḷḷa campantatti piṉvarumāṟu kāṇpikkiṟār”
“utāttam niṣātattiyum kāntārattiyum” “aṉutāttam riṣapattiyum tivatattiyum” svaritam caṭja mattima pañcamaṅkaḷiyum uḷḷaṭakkiyirukkiṉṟatu eṉkiṟār.”
ivvākkiyaṅkaḷik kavaṉikkiyil vaṭamoḻi ilakkaṇam coṉṉa pāṇiṉi (Panini) muṉivariṉ kālattil sapta curaṅkaḷum caṅkītattiṉ cila mukkiya amcaṅkaḷum collappaṭṭiruntatākat terikiṟatu. vaṭatēcatti liruntu vanta āriyar mutal mutal oṉṟu, iraṇṭu, mūṉṟu curaṅkaḷōṭu utātta aṉutāttamākak kāṉam ceytu vantārkaḷeṉṟu teḷivāka aṟikiṟōm. āṉāl intiyāviṉ teṉpākattiluḷḷa rāvaṇēsvaraṉ eṉṉum rājaṉ cāmavētatti sapta curaṅkaḷiṉālum pāṭiṉāṉeṉṟum ataṉ piṉpē sapta curaṅkaḷum avaṟṟiṉ vikruti cuvaraṅkaḷum cāma vētattil paḻakkattiṟku vantaṉaveṉṟum eṇṇa iṭamirukkiṟatu. iṭic caṅkattiṉ tuvakkattilirunta tolkāppiyariṉ kālattiṟku muṉṉālēyē caṅkīta cāstiram mika uṉṉata niliyiliruntateṉṟu nām muṉṉē pārttōm. itu pāṇiṉiyiṉ kālattiṟku anēka āyira varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉuḷḷaJ. pāṇiṉi rāvaṇaṉuṭiya kālattiṟkum tolkāppiyariṉ kālattiṟkum mikap pintiṉavareṉṟum iḷaṅkōvaṭikaḷ kālattiṟkuc camīpittavar eṉṟum colla vēṇṭum. pāṇiṉiyiṉ kālattiṟku muṉṉā lēyē cāma vētam pāṭum vitam rāvaṇēsparaṉāl kaṟpikkappaṭṭa teṉṟum ataṟku muṉṉālēyē ici nūl akattiyarālum nāratarālum collappaṭṭiruntateṉṟum nām aṟiya vēṇṭum. rāvaṇēsparaṉāl caṅkīta vāttiyam ceyyappaṭṭatāka piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
History of Music by Hunt P. 141.
“The family of stringed instruments played with a bow has been a very numerous one. The most ancient Viol on record appears to be the ravenstrom (or ravanstrom), still played in India by the mendicant monks of Buddhah. Tradition says that this primitive instrument was invented by one of the kings of Ceylon, but the date assigned to this monarch is somewhat about five thousand years before Christ. It sid, that the ravenstrom was the precursor of the gondok, or Russian Fiddle; and the Welsh crwth, which had six strings strung across a flat bridge, and was played partly with the bow, and partly by plucking with the fingers.”
“villiṉāl vācikkappaṭum tantivāttiyaṅkaḷiṉ toki aṉantam. avikaḷil veku pūrvamāyuḷḷatākac collappaṭum (Viol) kiṉṉaram eṉṟa vāttiyamāṉatu, taṟkālattilum intiyāvil puttamata canniyāci kaḷāl upayōkappaṭuttappaṭṭu varukiṟa rāvaṇēsvaram eṉṉum vāttiyamē. inta ātivāttiyamāṉatu ilaṅkiyi āṇṭa aracarkaḷil oruvaṉāl uṇṭākkappaṭṭatākac collappaṭukiṟatu. āṉāl anta aracaṉ aracāṇṭa kālam kiṟistuvukku muṉ 5,000 varuṣaṅkaḷ eṉṟu parampariyāyc collappaṭukiṟatu. curaḷaḷayin kuṉiṉaṭana allatu ḻuṭiṉēṭima eṉṉum vāttiyamāṉatu, inta irāvaṇēsvaram eṉṉum vāttiyattiliruntu uṇṭāṉatu eṉpataṟkut taṭiyilli. vēls tēcattil upayōkap paṭuttappaṭum ūcaṟavā eṉṉum vāttiyamum itiliruntu uṇṭāṉatutāṉ. ataṟku ēḻu tantikaḷ uṇṭu. atu villiṉāl vācikkappaṭalām, allatu viraliṉāl mīṭṭavum paṭalām.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷik kavaṉikkumpoḻutu rāvaṇēsvaram nām cātāraṇamāy teruvil pārkkum akappik kiṉṉariyip pōlirukku meṉṟu niṉikka ētuvirukkiṟatu. iti allatu itaṟku caṟṟup peritā yuḷḷatum villiṉāl vācikkakkūṭiyatumāṉa oru vāttiyatti rāvaṇēsvaraṉ ceytāṉeṉṟu colvati nampa iṭamilli. avaṉuṭiya cāstira vallamikkēṟpac caṅkītattil tērnta āriyarkaḷ kaṇṭu piṭikkāta curaṅkaḷiyum kaṇṭupiṭittu atil cāmavētattip pāṭip paramacivaṉ kirupiyip peṟutaṟkut takuntatāṉa uyarvuṭiya vāttiyamoṉṟu ceytiruppāṉē yoḻiya, kuṟavar ceytu āṟu picāvukku viṟṟu jīvaṉampaṇṇum oru akappik kiṉṉariyic ceytāṉeṉṟu colvatu muṟṟilum ovvāJ. rāvaṇaṉ āṇṭa tīvukaḷil tēṅkāy peritāyiruntatiṉāl akkoṭṭāṅkaccikaḷik koṇṭu akappik kiṉṉari ceytu ciṟuvarkaḷukkum coṟpattil jīvaṉam paṇṇum paratēcikaḷukkum viḷiyāṭṭup poruḷāka viṟṟu vantārkaḷ. itaṟku rāvaṇēsvaram eṉṟu peyar vantirukkalāmeṉṟu niṉikkiṟēṉ. 10,417 culōkaṅkaḷiyuṭiya rikku vētamē mikappūrvamāyuḷḷaJ. ivvētam ecur, cāmam, atarvaṇam eṉṟa mūṉṟu vētaṅkaḷiyum taṉṉilaṭakkik koṇṭiruntatu. rāvaṇēsvaraṉ rikku vētatti naṉṟāyt terintavaṉā yiruntatiṉāl, avikaḷuḷ paramacivaṉi tōttarikkak kūṭiyaṉavāyirunta cila culōkaṅkaḷit taṉ kālattil paḻakkamāyirunta teṉṉāṭṭuc caṅkīta muṟippaṭi sapta curaṅkaḷiyumuṭiyatākiya iṉiya paṇṇil kāṉam paṇṇiṉāṉeṉṟu viḷaṅkukiṟaJ. ikkāṉattil mikap pirītiyuṭiyavarāy rāvaṇaṉ piḻiyi maṉṉittatiṉ nimittam civaṉi cāmakāṉappiriyar eṉṟu nāḷatu variyum collappaṭṭu varukiṟatu. cāma, pēta, tāṉa, taṇṭam eṉṉum rāja tantiram nāṉkiyum aṟinta rāvaṇēsvaraṉ, pakavāṉ taṉṉi maṉṉikkumpaṭiyāka rikkuvētattiṉ cila pākatti vīṇiyuṭaṉ kāṉam paṇṇiṉāṉ. cāmam + vētam = cāmavētam; cāmam = camātāṉam paṇṇutal. taṉ mēl koṇṭa kōpam taṇintu camātāṉamākumpaṭi pirārttaṉi paṇṇiṉatiṉāl ikkītattiṟkuc cāmakāṉam allatu cāmavētam eṉṟu peyar vantirukkalām.
cōmayākañ ceyyuṅkālattil teyvattit tōttarikkum pāṭalkaḷukkuc cāmakāṉam eṉṟu peyar vaḻaṅkukiṟaJ. cōmam eṉpatu oru virukṣattiṟkum oru ceṭikkum peyarāka vaḻaṅki varukiṟateṉṟu terikiṟatu. cōma virukṣam kāṭāyiruntateṉṟum cōmak koṭi (cōmalati)yi koṇṭu vantāṉeṉṟum collappaṭukiṟatu. cōmacceṭi yiliruntuṇṭākum oruvita kuṭinīr āṉantatti viḷivittu mayakkatti uṇṭākkum vastuvāyiruntateṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu. yākattiṟkuriya patārttaṅkaḷi pākañceytu avaravarkaḷ yākapākam peṟṟukkoṇṭapiṉ taṅkaḷukkup pōtumāṉa aḷavu cōmaracapāṉam paṇṇum poḻutu cantira lōkattilirukkiṟatāka niṉikkum taṅkaḷ pitirkaḷukkāka cōmaṉi allatu cantiraṉip pārttu kāṉam paṇṇuvārkaḷ eṉṟu piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Music of Hindustan by Fox Strangways P. 249-250.
“Samaveda. The symbol round which the elaborate ritual of the Samaveda gathers is that sacrifice of which the drinking of the juice of the Soma plant was the central point. The virtues of this juice are recapitulated in the ninth book of the Rig Veda, from which minly the words for the Saman chants are taken. Soma is translated moon plant; and the Samaveda is specially connected with the worship of ancestors, whose abode was the moon. Great care was taken not to deviat from the original melody-types and rhythms and the religous efficacy of the hymns was held to depend largely on the right application of directions contined in the Brahmanic explanations (Brahmana not later than the sixth century B.C.) of the Vedic text “Samhita.” The expense of the full cermonical was not small; the Soma sacrifice involved days in performance and months in preparation. A full description of its elaborate and gorgeous ritual is to be found in the Aitareyabrahmana of the Rig Veda translated into English by Martin Haug, 1863, and its close connection with the fire-worship of the Zorastrins is there detiled.”
“cāmavētam. cāmavētattil collappaṭṭa atimēṉmiyāṉa caṭaṅ kācāraṅkaḷ ellāvaṟṟilumukkiyamāṉatu eṉṉaveṉṟāl cōmalatiyiṉ racattik kuṭippatākiya paliyē. ivviracattiṉ perumiyippaṟṟi rikkuvētattiṉ oṉpatāvatu atikārattil vistāramāyp pārkkalām. cāma vētattiṉ culōkañ collutalum itiliruntutāṉ eṭukkappaṭṭirukkiṟaJ. cōma eṉṟāl cantiracceṭi. cāmavētattiṟkum pitir vaṇakkattiṟkum campantam uṇṭu. ateṉṉaveṉṟāl appitirkkaḷ cantiraṉil vacikkiṟārkaḷ eṉṉum nampikkiyē. intac culōkaṅkaḷ ātiyil eḻutap paṭṭa irākamēriyilum tāḷattilum ippōtu collappaṭukiṟatā eṉpatippaṟṟi atika kavaliyuḷḷavarkaḷā yiruntārkaḷ. vētaṅkaḷic cērnta pirāmaṇaṅkaḷil caṅkītattippaṟṟic colliyirukkum vitikaḷiṉpaṭi inta cāmavēta culōkaṅkaḷ collappaṭāviṭṭāl avikaḷil yātoru payaṉumilli yeṉṟu koṇṭārkaḷ. (pirāmaṇaṅkaḷ ki.mu.  āṟāvatu nūṟṟāṇṭil uḷḷavi). caṭaṅkā cāraṅkaḷukkuc ceṉṟa celavāṉatu koñcamalla. cōmaraca pali oṉṟic celuttuvataṟku anēka nāḷ piṭittaṉa. ataṟku vēṇṭiya āyattaṅkaḷ muṭivu peṟa anēka mātaṅkaḷ ceṉṟaṉa. intap paliyiṉ atikāmpīramāṉa caṭaṅkukaḷip paṟṟiyaṟiya vēṇṭumāṉāl rikku vētattiluḷḷa āyttariya pirāmaṇattil pārkkalām. inta nūl āyacavin ḻayarapa eṉpavarāl 1863l iṅkilīṣil moḻi peyarkkappaṭṭaJ. intac caṭaṅkukaḷukkum pārcikaḷāl ceyyappaṭum akkiṉi vaṇakkattiluḷḷa caṭaṅkukaḷukkum uḷḷa neruṅkiya campantam itil naṉṟāy veḷippaṭum.”
cōmaṉip pārttup pirārttikkum kāṉattiṟkuc cōmakāṉam allatu cāmakāṉam eṉṟum cōmaracam kuṭittuc collum pāṭṭiṟkuc cāmakāṉameṉṟum peyar varalām. āṉāl rāvaṇēsvaraṉ paramacivaṉi iṉṉiciyuṭaṉ pāṭiyapiṉpē ataṟku cāmakāṉam eṉṟu peyar vantiruppatāka nām niṉikka vēṇṭum. rikkuvētattiluḷḷa culōkaṅkaḷē rāvaṇaṉāl kāṉam ceyyappaṭṭapiṉ cāmavētameṉṟu aḻikkappaṭṭaṉa eṉṟu nāḷatu variyum ulaka vaḻakkattil collappaṭukiṟatu.
mēṟkaṇṭa vākkiyaṅkaḷil kaṇṭa carittiratti nām itaṉ muṉ kēḷvippaṭṭirukkiṟōm. iccarittiratti ñāpakappaṭuttuvataṟkāka maturi ālayattil ceyyappaṭṭirukkum corūpattiyum pārttirukkiṟōm. itil rāvaṇēsvaraṉ oru vīṇiyi vittukkoṇṭu taṉ irupatu kikaḷiṉālum vācikkiṟatākak kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. mēlum maturikkuc camīpattiluḷḷa āviṭiyārkōyililum maṟṟiya civastalaṅkaḷilum rāvaṇa vākaṉam (kilācavākaṉam) eṉṟa veḷḷi vākaṉattilum rāvaṇaṉ vīṇi vācikkiṟatākavē tōṉṟukiṟatu. cumār 2,000-3,000, varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉuḷḷa ālayattiṉ cittirattiyum vākaṉattiṉ cittirattiyum kavaṉikkiyil rāvaṇēsvaram oru vilvāttiyam eṉṟu niṉikka iṭamilli. āṉāl intu caṅkītattiṉ ati pūrvamāyuḷḷatum taṟkālattil illātatum āyiram tanti pūṭṭiyatumākiya pēriyāḻ eṉpati rāvaṇaṉ ceytu taṉ 20 karaṅkaḷiṉālum avikaḷi mīṭṭi vācittāṉeṉṟu colvatu mikavum poruttamāyirukkum eṉṟu tōṉṟukiṟatu. akkālattiṟku muṉṉamēyē caṅkītam pūraṇa tērcciyaṭintirunta teṉṟu nām niccayikkalām. akkālattil pēriyāḻ, cakōṭayāḻ, makarayāḻ, ceṅkōṭṭiyāḻ mutaliya vīṇikaḷiruntaṉavākak kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉavē yoḻiya inta akappik kiṉṉari iruntatāka ōriṭattum collak kāṇōm. vāttiyaṅkaḷiṉ utaviyik koṇṭu oruvaṉāyt taṉittup pāṭum vaḻakkam, intiya caṅkītattiṟku mikap pūrvamākavēyuḷḷatu eṉṟu piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil kāṇappaṭukiṟatu.
H.M. Scale & Srutis, By K.B. Deval P. 46.
“The essential basis of music is melody and this is contined admittedly in the Hindu scale to its full extent. This has been the min charm of the Hindu system of music for thousands of years in the past and will continue to remin so for a number of years in the future.”
“ellāc caṅkītattukkum ātāram taṉiccuraṅkaḷi oṉṟiṉpiṉ oṉṟāyc colluvatutāṉ (melody). itu intiya caṅkītattil pūraṇa aḷavāy irukkiṟatu eṉṟu ellōrum oppuk koḷḷukiṟārkaḷ. anēka āyira varuṣaṅkaḷāy intiya caṅkītam iṉimiyāyiruppataṟkuk kāraṇam ituvē. itu kāraṇam paṟṟiyē varappōkiṟa varuṣaṅkaḷilum atu veku piracittamāyirukkum eṉpataṟkuc cantēkamilli.”
ivvākkiyaṅkaḷil rākam pāṭuvatu mika iṉimiyuṭiyateṉṟu collukiṟār. iṉi varappōkuṅ kālaṅkaḷilum itu mikavum mēṉmi yuṭiyatāka eṇṇappaṭumeṉṟum niccayañ collukiṟār. uṇmi yilēyē avikaḷ mēṉmiyuṭiyavikaḷā yiruppataṟkuk kāraṇam piṉvarum vākkiyattil kāṇalām.

The Indian Empire by W.W. Hunter. P. III.
“It is, indeed, impossible to adequately represent the Indian system by the European notation; and the full range of its effects can only be rendered by Indian instruments a vast collection of sound-producers, slowly elaborated during 2,000 years to suit the special requirements of Hindu music. The complicated structure of its musical modes (rags) rests upon three separate systems, one of which consists of five, another of six, and the other of seven notes. It preserves in a living state some of the early forms which puzzle the student of Greek music, side by side with the most complicated developments.”
 “intiya irākaṅkaḷi airōppiya caṅkītattil vaḻaṅki varum kōṭukaḷ nōṭṭukkaḷ mūlamāy curappaṭuttuvatu kūṭātakāriyam. anta irākaṅkaḷiṉ muḻu iṉimiyiyum pirastārattiyum aṟiya vēṇṭu māṉāl intiya caṅkīta vāttiyaṅkaḷ mūlamāyttāṉ aṟiyalām. inta vāttiyaṅkaḷ oṉṟiraṇṭalla, nēṟṟu muṉṟānāḷ uṇṭāṉaviyumalla. avikaḷiṉ toki atikam. aviyuṇṭāṉa kālamum 2,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉākum. avi orē kālattil uṇṭāṉaviyumalla. antantak kālattukkum avaciyattukkum takkapaṭi paṭippaṭiyāy 2,000 varuṣaṅkaḷākac cōtikkappaṭṭum pirastārattukkuk koṇṭuvarappaṭṭavi. intiya caṅkītattukku ātāramāyirukkappaṭṭa ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇaṅkaḷ mūṉṟu vakiyuḷḷaṉa. oṉṟu 5 curaṅkaḷum, oṉṟu 6 curaṅkaḷum, oṉṟu 7 curaṅkaḷumuḷḷaṉa. ātiyil ēṟpaṭṭuḷḷa inta ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇaṅkaḷ avvaḷavu cikkumukkāy iruppatāl kirēkka caṅkītam aṟintavarkaḷkūṭa avikaḷik kaṇṭāl yāteṉṟaṟiyāmal tikippārkaḷ.”
ivvacaṉaṅkaḷi kavaṉikkiyil iti eḻutiyavar intiya caṅkīta cāstirattiṉ nuṭpaṅkaḷi naṉṟāy aṟintavareṉpatu viḷaṅkukiṟaJ. mutal mutal airōppiyac curattik kōṭukaḷil kuṟikkum vaḻakkampōla kuṟikkak kūṭāta vitamāka nuṭpamāṉa curaṅkaḷirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu collukiṟār. annuṭpamāṉa curaṅkaḷiyum intiya caṅkīta vāttiyaṅkaḷiṉ mūlamāyttāṉ aṟiyalāmeṉṟum annuṭpamāṉa curaṅkaḷē intiya rākaṅkaḷiṉ iṉimiyuṭiyaṉaveṉṟum collukiṟār. innuṭpamāṉa curamē intiya caṅkītatti alaṅkarittuk koṇṭirukkiṟateṉṟu aṟintālum ilakuvāyc collikkāṭṭavum iṉṉiṉṉa aḷavil varukiṟateṉṟu eḻutikkāṭṭavum aṟiyātirukkiṟārkaḷ. mēlum intiyāvil vaḻaṅki varum vāttiyak karuvikaḷ 2,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉṉālēyē uṇṭākkap paṭṭaviyeṉṟum oḷaṭava, cāṭava, campūraṇam eṉṉum ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇaṅkaḷil pāṭum oru rākam maṟṟavarkaḷ aṟintukoḷḷak kūṭāta avvaḷavu tērcci peṟṟaviyeṉṟum collukiṟār. mēṟṟici caṅkītattiṟkum kīḻttici caṅkītattiṟkumuḷḷa vittiyācattiyum taṉittuppāṭum intiya caṅkītattiṉ mēṉmiyiyum atu viruttiyaṭiyāmal pōṉataṟkuk kāraṇattiyum Capt. Daypiṉvarumāṟu collukiṟār.
Vide Hindhustani Sangita Paddhati. P. 329, 330.
“The wide divergence of thaste in the matter of music between European and Asiatic nations has doubtless arisen from the fact that while the western nations gradually discarded the employment of mode, and clothed the melody with harmony, the Eastern nations in this respect made little or no progress; and now, in India, the employment of authentic modes and melody types (or ragas) is still jealously adhered to.
Speaking of this, Capt. Willard remarks “To expect an endless variety in the melody of Hindustan would be an injudicous hope as their authentic melody is limited to a certin number, sid to have been composed by professors universally acknowledged to have possessed not only real merit but also the original genius of composition, beyond the precinets of whose authority it would be criminal to trespass. What the more reputed of the moderns have done is that they have adopted them to their own purposes, and found others by the combination of two or more of them. Thus far they are licensed, but they dare not proceed a step further. Whatever merit an entire modern composition might possess, should it have no resemblance to the established melody of the country, it would be looked upon as spurious. It is implicitly believed that it is impossible to add to the number of these one single melody of equal merit. So tenacious are the natives of Hindustan of the ancient practices.”
The continued employment of mode combined with the almost entire absence of harmony, has prevented Indian music from reaching any higher pitch of development such as has been attined elsewhere. It stands to reason also that this is the chief cuse of the monotony which cuses Indian music to be little appreciated by, if not repellent to, European ears.
Since the early periods of India history, music would seem to have been cultivated more as a science than an art. More attention seems to have been pid to elaborate and tedious artistic skill than to simple and natural melody. Hence arose technical rules that marred the pristine sweetness of melody, the very life of all real music. To a great extent this must be attributed to the art falling into the hands of illiterate “Virtuosi.” Their influence which cused music to suffer both in purity of style and simplicity is being felt less and less. The great aim of music - “Rakti” or the power of affecting the heart-now asserts itself more and more, and is slowly but surely bringing about a return to the early type of sweet, simple melody.”
“airōppiya jātiyārukkum āciyāviluḷḷa jātiyārukkum caṅkīta viṣayattil irukkum vitti yācattiṟkuk kāraṇameṉṉa veṉṟāl, mēṟṟici jātiyār varavara ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇa viṣayamāy ēṟpaṭum irākaṅkaḷi viṭṭuviṭṭu ēka kālattil pala aṉucuraṅkaḷi amittup pāṭuvatiyē vicēṣamāka virutti ceytārkaḷ. kīḻtticiyār itu viṣayattil yātoru muyaṟciyum ceyyavilli. taṟkālattilum intiyāvil (authentic modes) ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇaṅkaḷiyum irākap pirastāraṅkaḷiyumviṭa caṅkītam vēṟilli eṉṟa koḷki mēmpāṭuṭiyatāyirukkiṟatu.
itu viṣayattippaṟṟi Captin Willard eṉpavar colluvatu yāteṉil, intu tēcattiluḷḷōr upayōkikkum ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇaṅkaḷ (authentic modes) coṟpamāṉaviyātalāl, intiyaruṭiya irākavittiyācaṅkaḷ kaṇakkilaṭaṅkātaviyāyirukkum eṉṟu eṇṇuvatu picaku. appaṭi eṇṇuvatum niyāya virōtamāyirukkum. inta ārōkaṇaṅkaḷ (authentic modes) caṅkītattil vitvat tiṟamiyiyum uruvākkum tiṟamiyiyum uṭittāyirunta pala vitvāṉkaḷāl amikkappaṭṭaviyeṉṟu yāvarum oppukkoḷvatāl,avarkaḷuṭiya variyik kaṭantu nām celvatu kuṟṟamāyirukkum. taṟkāla vitvāṉkaḷ anta ārōkaṇaṅkaḷit tāṅkaḷum aṅkīkarittu upayōkattukkuk koṇṭu vantatumallāmal avikaḷil iraṇṭoṉṟi oṉṟu cērttatāl putitāṉa cilavaṟṟik kaṇṭupiṭittu mirukkiṟārkaḷ. ivvaḷavu tūram ivarkaḷ ceyyalāmē yoḻiya itaṟku mēl nūtaṉamāy oṉṟiyum avarkaḷ ceyya atikāramēyilli. taṟkālam uṇṭu paṇṇappaṭṭa caṅkītamāṉatu evvaḷavu matippi uṭittāyiruntapōtilum, tēcattil parampariyāy vaḻaṅkappaṭum ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇaṅkaḷukku ottirāviṭṭāl kuṟṟameṉṟu koḷḷappaṭum.  ippōtu irukkiṟa rākaṅkaḷōṭu putitāy ētāvatu oṉṟi yāvatu cērppatu kūṭāta kāriyam eṉṟu intu vitvāṉkaḷ niccayamāy nampukiṟārkaḷ. paḻamiyi viṭāppiṭiyāyp piṭittal eṉkiṟa vaḻakkam ivarkaḷukkuḷ iruppatāl ippaṭi niṉikkiṟārkaḷ. taṉi curaṅkaḷālāṉa irākaṅkaḷiyē virutti ceytu pala aṉucuraṅkaḷi ēka kālattil cērttuc colvatik (Harmony) kaṭṭōṭē viṭṭuviṭṭatutāṉ intiya caṅkītamāṉatu maṟṟa tēca caṅkītam viruttiyaṭintatupōla aṭiyātataṟkuk kāraṇam. airōppiyaruṭiya kātukku intiya saṅkītam iṉimiyāyirāmal orē rītiyuṭiyatāyk kāṇappaṭuvataṟkum ituvē kāraṇam.
intutēca carittirattiṉ ārampakāla mutal intiya caṅkītamāṉatu cāstira campantamāṉa viṣayattil tērcciyi aṭintiruntatēyoḻiya pāṭutal, vāttiyaṅkaḷil vācittal mutaliya viṣayaṅkaḷil atika virutti yaṭintiruntatākat teriyavilli. irākaṅkaḷic cuttamāy yāvarukkum viḷaṅkakkūṭiya muṟiyil colvatip periyatāy niṉikkāmal irākaṅkaḷik kaṣṭamāy ākkik kāṇpippatē vittiyeṉa niṉittārkaḷ. ākiyāltāṉ putitu putitāṉa muṟikaḷ tōṉṟi ātiyil irākaṅkaḷukku irunta aḻakik keṭuttuviṭṭaṉa. caṅkītattiṉ aḻaku yāvarukkum eḷitil viḷaṅkum atiṉ lēcāṉa muṟiyaṉṟō? ippaṭic caṅkītam keṭṭuppōṉataṟku mukkiyak kāraṇam yāteṉṟāl, paṭippaṟiyāta pāṭakar, vāttiyakkārar mutaliyavarkaḷ kiyil caṅkītam akappaṭṭuk koṇṭatutāṉ. āṉāl caṅkītamāṉatu ippaṭic cuttamiṉṟik kalappāvatiṉālum lēcāṉa muṟikaḷi viṭṭu viṭuvatiṉālum keṭṭup pōvataṟkuk kāraṇamāyiruntavarkaḷ ippōtu varavara oḻintupōkiṟārkaḷ. ākiyāl ippōtu caṅkītamāṉatu muṉṉirunta ātit taṉinilimikkut tirumpi varukiṟatu eṉpataṟkuc cantēkamilli. ellāc caṅkītattiṉuṭiya mukkiya amcam eṉṉaveṉṟāl, kalliyum kariyappaṇṇumpaṭiyāṉa avvaḷavu iṉimiyik koṭuppatutāṉ. taṟkāla caṅkītam ivvaḻiyil tirumpukiṟatu eṉkiṟataṟkum pōtumāṉa ātāraṅkaḷ uṇṭu.”
itil mēṟṟiciyār oru ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇattil ēṟpaṭum rākam oṉṟi ataṟkuriya aḻakōṭu pāṭuvati viṭṭuviṭṭu ṣaṭjamam, kāntāram, pañcamam, mēlṣaṭjamam mutaliya aṉucuraṅkaḷi vittuk koṇṭu nālu pākamākap paṭikkum aḻakiyē virutti ceytārkaḷeṉṟum, intiyarkaḷ appaṭic ceyyāmal rākattiyē paṭippatu mēleṉṟu niṉittārkaḷeṉṟum collukiṟār. ivvuṇmiyi nāmum aṅkīkarikka vēṇṭiyatē. pūrvattil iraṇṭu mūṉṟu nālu curaṅkaḷōṭu vētattik kāṉam ceyta kālattil, cilar ṣaṭjamattilum cilar mēl ṣaṭjamattilum tuvakkip pāṭuvatu vaḻakkam. ivvaḻakkam vētapārāyaṇam paṇṇum ovvoru kūṭṭattārukkum nāḷatuvari paḻakkamāyirukkiṟateṉṟum ituvē palar cērntu pāṭum muṟiyāyiruntateṉṟum nām aṟiya vēṇṭum. ivvaḻakkattiṉiṉṟē maṟṟa tēcattār cērntu pāṭum muṟiyi viruttikkuk koṇṭu vantārkaḷeṉṟum, āṉāl immuṟi mikap pūrvamāy intiyāvilēyē vaḻaṅki vantateṉṟum ovvoru intiyaṉum perumip pārāṭṭakkūṭiyatākavumirukkiṟaJ. uṟcava kālaṅkaḷil svāmi veḷippuṟappaṭumpoḻutu svāmiyiṉ piṉṉāl vētakāṉam paṇṇuvati nām kavaṉittāl ivvuṇmi viḷaṅkum. āṉāl sapta curaṅkaḷu muṇṭāki avikaḷiṉ pirastārapētattiṉāl anēka rākaṅkaḷ uṇṭāṉati aṟinta piṉ, oṉṟu cērntu pāṭum vaḻakkattil taṉittup pāṭum aḻaku uṇṭākamāṭṭāteṉṟu aṟintārkaḷ. ṣaṭjamattil ārampittuc caṅkarāparaṇa rākañcollum oruvarukku, kāntārattil ārampittuppāṭuvatu aṉumattōṭi rākamākavum, pañcamattil ārampittup pāṭum maṟṟavarukku harikāmpōti rākamākavum varum. āka mūṉṟu rākaṅkaḷiyum oṉṟu cērppatiṉāl kātukku iṉimiyiyum maṉatukku oṭukkattiyum tarāJ. āṉāl muṭiyum iṭaṅkaḷil allatu cila curaṅkaḷ oṉṟu cērumiṭattil caṟṟu iṉimiyāyirukkum. mey maṟanta uṉṉata nilikku aṉucuraṅkaḷ cērnta caṅkītam utaviyāyirukkamāṭṭāJ. āṉāl maṉatip palavaḻippaṭuttic carīratti uṟcākappaṭutti vikkum. iv vuṇmiyi aṟintē taṉittup pāṭum muṟiyi intiyāvil taṉittut tapacu ceytu koṇṭirunta periyōr virutti ceytu koṇṭu vantārkaḷeṉṟu niccayamāyc collalām. intiyāvip pōlotta uṣṇa piratēcattiluḷḷōr karuvi, karaṇaṅkaḷ oṭuṅki niṟka nīṇṭa jīvaṉiyum virintu niṟkak kuṟukiya āyuḷiyum uṭiyavarāvārkaḷ. āṉāl airōppāvip pōṉṟa kuḷirnta piratēcaṅkaḷiluḷḷōr karuvi, karaṇaṅkaḷ oṭuṅki niṟkil kuḷiriṉāl viṟittu āyuḷ kuṟukip pōvārkaḷ. taṅkaḷi uṣṇappaṭuttuvataṟku vēṇṭiya ākāramum, uṭiyum aṉalum, āṭṭamum avarkaḷukku mikavum avaciyamē. itaṟkēṟpa uṣṇap piratēcattil kuḷirnta nīrūṟṟukkaḷum cītaḷap piratēcattil ven nīrūṟṟukkaḷum amintiruppatu iṅkuk kavaṉikkattakkaJ. ivviyaṟkiyi aṉucarittē ellōrum pāṭavum āṭavum kūṭiyavitamāka kuḷirnta tēcattiṉ caṅkītam amintirukkiṟaJ. intiyāvil pakirmukappaṭṭa maṉati oruvaḻippaṭuttit tiyāṉa nilikkuk koṇṭu varakkūṭiya vitamākavum, neṭunēram camāti kūṭum vitamāy rākavistāramuṭiyatākavum mikavum ciṟanta amippuṭiyatākavum aḻakuṭiyatākavum ceyyappaṭṭirukkiṟaJ. āṭavum pāṭavum virumpuvōṉ mēṟṟicic caṅkītattil piriyappaṭuvāṉ. amintu maṉamaṭaṅki tiyāṉa niliyil niṟka virumpuvōṉ teṉṉintiya caṅkītattiṉ oru rākatti āṟumātamāṉālum toṭarntu pāṭikkoṇṭum kēṭṭuk koṇṭumiruppāṉ. itu iyaṟki amippē. kuḷir kālamākiya mārikkālattil kāytta kāykaḷ tittippillāmalum paḻukkāmalum neṭunāḷ marattil niṟpatiyum, puṣpaṅkaḷ vācaṉiyiṉṟic cilanāḷ utirāmaliruppatiyum nām aṟivōm. āṉāl uṣṇa kālattil veku cīkkirattil kāykaḷ atika maturamuṭiyaviyāki viṭuvatiyum puṣpaṅkaḷ atika vācaṉiyuṭiyaṉavāy cīkkiram utirntu viṭuvatiyum nām kāṇalām. atikak kuḷircciyāṉa tōppukaḷuḷḷa āṟṟōraṅkaḷilum malikaḷiṉ kukikaḷilum tapacu ceytu koṇṭirunta periyōr taṅkaḷ tapacukkaṉukūlamāka vīṇi eṉṉum ciṟanta vāttiyatti vittukkoṇṭu oru rākatti anēka kālam appiyācittu ālāpaṉi ceytu taṉittup pāṭum vaḻakkatti virutti ceytu vantirukkiṟārkaḷ eṉṟu nām aṟiya vēṇṭum. mālikkālattil utayamum utayakālattil māliyum iruppatupōl uṇarakkāṭṭum rākattiṉ aḻaku, aṉucuraṅkaḷic cērttup pāṭuvatiṉāl uṇṭākamāṭṭāteṉpatu tiṇṇam. ākiyiṉāl ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇattil oḷaṭava, cāṭava, campūraṇam eṉṉum curappaṭi vakkira varjiya kālakkiramam aṟintu oru rākattippāṭi ataṉ iṉimi terintu koṇṭavarkaḷ atiyē virumpuvārkaḷ eṉpatu niccayam.
paḻamiyi viṭāppiṭiyāy piṭikkiṟa vaḻakkam intukkaḷuk kuḷḷiruppatāl putitāka oru rākam uṇṭākkuvatu kūṭāta kāriyameṉṟu caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷ niccayamāy nampukiṟārkaḷ eṉkiṟār. ivviṣayattil ivar apippirāyatti muṟṟilum oppukkoḷḷukiṟōm. pūrvattil intiyā vilirunta periyōr ovvoru rākattiyum vistāram ceyvataṟku vēṇṭiya kirāmatti kītamākac ceytu vittārkaḷ. akkītamē taṟkālattiluḷḷa vitvāṉkaḷukku rākatti vistāram paṇṇuvatil ūṉṟu kōl pōl utavukiṟaJ. ippaṭippaṭṭa kītam ceyyakkūṭiya vitimuṟikaḷ aḻintupōyiṉa. ākiyiṉāl muṉṉōr ceytu vitta kītaṅkaḷ ettaṉiyō attaṉi rākaṅkaḷ māttirantāṉ ippōtu pāṭappaṭṭu varukiṉṟaṉa. putitāka oru ārōkaṇa avarōkaṇattil oru rākam oruvar pāṭuvārāṉāl, atil anēka tappitaṅkaḷ ēṟpaṭṭu itip pāṭāmal viṭṭuviṭuvatē nallateṉṟu avarukkē tōṉṟum. tiṟavu kōlillāta pūṭṭit tiṟakkavum piṉ pūṭṭavum muṭiyātirukkiṟatu eppaṭiyō appaṭiyē intiya caṅkītattiṉ niliyumirukkiṟaJ. itu tavira “cāstira campantamākat tērcci yaṭintiruntārkaḷēyoḻiya, pāṭuvatil avvaḷavu tērcciyaṭiyavilli”  eṉṟu collukiṟār. aṉupōkam vēṟu col vēṟāyirunta cila caṅkīta cāstiraṅkaḷiṉāl ippaṭic colla nēriṭukiṟatē yoḻiya cāstirattiṉ marmam teriyumāṉāl ivvitam niṉikka māṭṭōm. mēlum avar colliyatu pōlavē, iḻivāṉa toḻil uḷḷavarkaḷālum aṟiyāmi yuḷḷavarkaḷālum caṅkītam taṉ matippi iḻantateṉṟu nāmum collukiṟōm. ātmalāpam peṟa uṇṭāṉa kītam artta nimittam viṟkappaṭuvati aṟinta periyōr aruvaruppatu niyāyantāṉē.
kirīṭātipatikaḷālum avatāra mūrttikaḷālum riṣikaḷālum āṭik koṇṭāṭappaṭṭu vanta caṅkītam, kaḻikkūttāṭikaḷālum kūttāṭukiṟavar kaḷālum naṭaṉañceypavarkaḷālum ari kuṟiyāy appiyācikkappaṭṭum, caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷeṉṟu taṅkaḷik kāṭṭik koḷḷukiṟavarkaḷāl taṟ kālattil perumpālum paṭikkappaṭṭum varuvatē caṅkītam kuṟivāṉa nilikku vantataṟku kāraṇameṉṟu colla iṭantarukiṟaJ. intiya caṅkītattiṟku mukkiyamāṉa mārkkaviti tavaṟi, perumpālum tēcikamāṉataṟkuk kāraṇam intiya tēcattup pirapukkaḷiṉ kavaṉakkuṟivē yoḻiya vēṟalla. intiyāvil caṅkītam mika uyarvākak koṇṭāṭappaṭṭa teṉṟum vāymoḻiyāl parampariyāyp pāṭappaṭum kītaṅkaḷi nām ātāramākak koḷḷa vēṇṭumeṉṟum piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil terikiṟatu.
Doctor Coomarasawmy’s Foreword to “The Study of Indian Music” by E. Clements.
“Long anterior to this, however, music was a most highly cultivated-perhaps the most highly cultivated of India arts, and to the present day it has remined the most continuously vital and most universally appreciated art of India.”
“kāḷitācaṉ paratar kālattukku muṉṉēyē caṅkītamāṉatu ellā intiya cāstira vittikaḷilum atikamāyp payilappaṭṭu vantatu eṉṟum ātikālamutal taṟkālam vari toṉṟutoṭṭu uyiruḷḷatāyum, intiyāvil ellā vittikaḷilum cirēṣṭamāṉatāyum koṇṭāṭap paṭṭateṉṟum teriya varukiṟatu.”
“It is far better that the method of oral transmission should be mintined.”
“vāymoḻiyāl parampariyāy varum muṟiyē vicēṣamākak koḷḷappaṭa vēṇṭum.”
vāymoḻiyāy parampariyāy varum muṟiyiyē vicēṣamāyk koḷḷa vēṇṭum. ivvārttiyi nām naṉṟāy iṅkē kavaṉikka vēṇṭum. intiyāviṉ mukkiyamāṉa vaḻakkam oṉṟu itaṉāl aṟivōm. intiyāviṉ arumiyāṉa anēka kalikaḷum toḻilkaḷum takutiyuḷḷa oruvaṉukkuk karṇaparampariyāy atāvatu taṉ antiya kālattil veku rakaciyamāy kātil collappaṭṭu vantatāka aṟivōm. cila mantiraṅkaḷiṉ rakaciyaṅkaḷum vētāntattiṉ rakaciyamum vātattiṉ rakaciyamum muppūviṉ muṟiyum pōṉṟa cila arumiyāṉa vittikaḷ oṉṟu allatu iraṇṭu vārttikaḷāl pūrttipeṟakkūṭiyaviyāyiruntaṉa. appaṭippaṭṭa rakaciya moḻi allatu kurumoḻiyiṉṟi eḻutappaṭṭa pustakaṅkaḷ ērāḷamā yiruntum oru pirayōjaṉattiyum tarāJ. itōṭu cila ariya vittikaḷ toṭṭukkāṭṭa atāvatu ceytu kāṭṭa vēṇṭiyavikaḷākavum iruntaṉa. icceymuṟi toṭṭukkāṭṭāta variyum viḷaṅkuvatu kaṭiṉamākum. vittiyam vātam mutaliya toḻilkaḷil varum iṭipākam pañca pūta iṉapākamum vāṉa cāstiram yōka cāstiram mutaliyavikaḷil varum kaṇitamuṟikaḷum vētānta cāstirattil varum pañcīkaraṇa tattuvac ceyalkaḷum mukkuṇa tattuvac ceyalkaḷum pōṉṟa anēka rakaciyam collappaṭāmal cāstiraṅkaḷ eḻutappaṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉaveṉṟu nām aṟivōm. “ellārkaṇ muṉṉiṟkum eṭutturikkum kuru aruḷillāmaṟ pōṉāl collālum vārātu” eṉṟa vākkiyattiṉpaṭi mika virivāṉa nūlkaḷukkum periyōr toṭṭukkāṭṭa vēṇṭiya cila rakaciyaṅkaḷ vitteḻutiyirukkiṟārkaḷ. akkurumoḻiyillāmal pōṉāl cāvi yillāta oruvaṉ pūṭṭiya vīṭṭi eppaṭic cuṟṟic cuṟṟi mayaṅkuvāṉō appaṭiyē kurumoḻiyillāta cāstiraṅkaḷum viḷaṅkāmal aṉarttam viḷivikkum. oru neruppukkucciyiṉ veḷiccam eppaṭi iruṭṭiṉ antakāratti nīkki uḷḷatikkāṭṭukiṟatō appaṭippōl periyōrkaḷ collum oru vārttiyiṉ piṉ vācikkum oru cāstiram mikat teḷivāka arttamākiṟaJ. ippaṭiyē teṉṉintiya caṅkīta cāstirattiṉ curuti muṟiyilum oru rakaciyam maṟikkappaṭṭirukkiṟa teṉpati itaṉpiṉ pārppōm.
ic curuti muṟikkuriya tiṟavukōl (rakaciyam) teriyumāṉāl teṉṉintiya caṅkītam māttiram cāstira yuktamuṭiya teṉṟum maṟṟavi kuṟivuḷḷaviyeṉṟum nām ilakuvāy aṟintu koḷvōm. teṉṉintiyāvil pāṭappaṭṭu varum rākaṅkaḷum avikaḷukkuriya curaṅkaḷum aṇuppiramāṇamum tavaṟāmal parampariyāy nāḷatu variyum kōyil ūḻiyakkārarkaḷāl pēṇappaṭṭu vantirukkiṟateṉṟum atuvē karnāṭaka caṅkītam cuttamāyirukkiṟataṟkuk kāraṇam eṉṟum nām aṟiya vēṇṭum. ikkarnāṭaka muṟiyiṉ rakaciyam terintu koḷḷāmi yiṉālēyē curutiyip paṟṟip palar palavāṟāka pustakaṅkaḷ eḻutavum vātañ ceyyavum nēriṭṭatu.
mēl kāṟṟiṉāl kiḻakkē pōṉa paṭcikaḷum carukukaḷum kīḻ kāṟṟiṉāl maṟupaṭi mēṟkē pōvatupōla maṟṟavar kītamum kālatarumat tiṟkut takuntapaṭi appaṭiyum ippaṭiyum pala māṟutalkaḷiyaṭintālum tamiḻnāṭṭiṉ caṅkītam oṉṟu māttiram māṟāmal niṟkiṟateṉṟu aṟivōm. tamiḻ nāṭṭil vaḻaṅki varum caṅkītattiṟkuriya pūrva nūlkaḷ muṟṟilum aḻintu pōṉālum parampariyāy vaḻaṅki varum pāṭamuṟikaḷ caṅkītattiṉ rakaciyam yāvum aṟintu koḷvataṟkup pōtumāṉavi yeṉṟum ammuṟiyik koṇṭu caṅkīta cāstirattiṉ mukkiyamāṉa tattuvaṅkaḷik kaṇṭupiṭikkavum atupōlavē maṟṟoṉṟu nūtaṉamāy uṇṭākkavum kūṭiya vitamāyirukkiṟateṉṟu nām niccayam colluvōm. rākaṅkaḷip paṟṟi eḻutik koṇṭirukkum iraṇṭām puttakattil itaṉ viparam yāvum teḷivākak kāṇalām. oru collāl virinta pala col maṟinta oru colli viḷakki niṟpatu pōla caṅkītattil virinta pala uruppaṭikaḷ collāmal viṭṭa curuti rakaciyatti viḷakkik kāṭṭik koṇṭirukkiṉṟaṉa. karṇa parampariyāy vaḻaṅki varum karnāṭaka rākaṅkaḷiṉ aḻakē avvirākaṅkaḷiṉ rakaciyattiyum kāṭṭik koṇṭu niṟkiṟateṉṟu itaṉpiṉ pārppōm. erikiṟa viḷakki viṭṭil pūcci viḻuntu keṭuttut tāṉum keṭṭatupōlākāmal putitāṉa tēcika rākaṅkaḷik kēṭṭu cila avāntara curaṅkaḷi karnāṭaka caṅkītattil kalantu vaḻaṅkum keṭutalkaḷi nīkkik karnāṭaka muṟippaṭi taṅkaḷ kāṉattik kāppāṟṟik koḷḷumpaṭi karnāṭaka caṅkītam payilum kaṉavāṉkaḷ yāvariyum mikavum kēṭṭuk koḷḷukiṟēṉ.
piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil intiya caṅkītattiṉ iṉimiyi maṟṟavarkaḷ aṟintu koḷḷātataṟkuk kāraṇam kūṟukiṟār.
The Indian empire by W.W. Hunter. P. III.
“Melodies which the Indian composer pronounces to be the perfection of harmony, and which have for ages touched the hearts and fired the imagination of Indian audiences, are condemned as discord by the European critic. The Hindu ear has been trined to recognise modifications of sound which the European ear refuses to take pleasure in. Our ears on the other hand, have been tught to expect harmonic combinations of its own. The Indian musician declines altogether to be judged by the few simple Hindu airs which the English ear can appreciate.”
“irākaṅkaḷil atika iṉimiyuṭiyaviyeṉṟum, cirēṣṭa curap poruttamuḷḷaviyeṉṟum, nīṇṭa kālaṅkaḷāka intiyaruṭiya maṉati urukac ceytu paravacappaṭuttiṉavi eṉṟum yāvarālum oppukkoḷḷap paṭṭavikaḷ, curapporuttam aṟṟaviyeṉṟu airōppiyarāl taḷḷiviṭap paṭukiṉṟaṉa. intiyaruṭiya kātukku iṉimiyeṉṟeṇṇappaṭum nuṭpamāṉa ciṟu curaṅkaḷ airōppiyaruṭiya kātukaḷukku veṟuppāyirukkiṉṟaṉa. nammuṭiya kātukaḷō airōppiyaruṭiya curaṅkaḷukku māṟupaṭṭa oṟṟumic curaṅkaḷiyē kēṭṭu āṉantikkum paḻakkatti yaṭintirukkiṉṟaṉa. intu caṅkītattiṉ cila ilēcāṉa tuṇṭukaḷi airōppiyar iṉimiyāyiruntatāka oppuk koḷvārkaḷ. āṉāl intu vitvāṉkaḷ intu caṅkītattiṉ iṉimikku intac ciṟu tuṇṭukaḷ tāṉ cāṭci eṉṟu oppuk koḷḷat tayārāyilli.”
intiya caṅkītam vekunāḷikku muṉṉuḷḷateṉṟum ovvoru curamum mummūṉṟu pirivuṭiya tāyiruntateṉṟum teṉṉintiya caṅkītam vētaṅkaḷi ōtuvataṟku mika aṉukūlamāy irukkiṟateṉṟum piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Hindu Music and the Gayan Samaj, P. ii P. 36 (Kunte)
“It is now positively established by documentary evidence that at least 7,000 years before Christ. India had developed a system of musical notation, that the seven notes were scientifically arranged, each note being divided into three the sharp, flat, and the proper note itself. All the inscriptions as yet discovered, and the Sanskrit literature that has been brought to light, place this statement beyond doubt. This fact has a scientific side. Though all the world over, notes, the elements of music, are seven only, yet the great variety of modes and melodies differ in European and Indian music, which is either ancient or modern, Southern or Northern. The Southern or the Dravidian system is more Vedic than the Northern or Hindustani Dhanga. There is what is called a constant mode in Maharastra. This is the remnant of the system of singing vedic psalms. It consists of opening modulation, soft, steady and slow in its progress. this is followed by notes the pitch of which is high, the modulation is strong, varied and rapid in its flow. This is followed by a combination of both leading to agreeable cadences. In a treatise on music, which is at any rate as ancient as the third century before Christ, a connection between physiological condition of human blood in the course of a day, and the changes of temper which these conditions necessitate are explined.”
“kuṟintapakṣam kiṟistu piṟakka 7,000 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ intiyāvil caṅkīta curaṅkaḷ eḻutum muṟi uṇṭāyiruntateṉṟum capta curaṅkaḷum cāstira vitippaṭi oḻuṅkāyk kaṇṭupiṭikkap paṭṭiruntaṉa eṉṟum, ovvoru curamum mūṉṟu pirivuḷḷatāyiruntatu. atāvatu curamum curattukkuk koñcam kūṭiya curutiyum curattukkuk koñcam kuṟinta curutiyumāka mūṉṟu eṉṟum eḻuttu ātāraṅkaḷ mūlamāy nirūpikkappaṭṭirukkiṟatu. ituvariyum kaṇṭupiṭikkappaṭṭirukkiṟa cila eḻuttukaḷum, kiranta nūlkaḷum iti oṭṭiyē stiramāyp pēcukiṉṟaṉa. intak koḷkiyāṉatu cāstira campantamuḷḷatāyum irukkiṟaJ. ulakameṅkum kāṉattukku ātāramākiya curaṅkaḷ ēḻu eṉṟa cattiyam vaḻaṅki vanta pōtilum, intiya caṅkītattilum airōppiya caṅkītattilum vaḻaṅkappaṭum ārōkaṇaṅkaḷum irākaṅkaḷum anēkaṅkaḷāyum oṉṟukkoṉṟu aṉanta vittiyācamuḷḷavi kaḷāyum irukkiṉṟaṉa. itu viṣayattil, ātimuṟikaḷō, taṟkāla muṟikaḷō, vaṭatēcamuṟikaḷō, teṉtēcamuṟikaḷō eviyāṉālum oṉṟukkoṉṟu vittiyācaṅkaḷ anēkamuḷḷavikaḷā yirukkiṉṟaṉa. teṉtēca allatu tirāviṭa caṅkītamāṉatu, vaṭatēca intustāṉiṭaṅkāviviṭa vētaṅkaḷi ōtuvataṟku atika pirayōcaṉamuḷḷatāyirukkiṟatākat terikiṟatu. makārāṣṭirattil māṟāta ārōkaṇam (constant mode) eṉṟa ōr muṟiyuṇṭu. vētattiluḷḷa caṅkītaṅkaḷi ōtum muṟiyiṉiṉṟu mīnta ōr muṟiyām iJ. atu ārampattil metuvāṉa ēṟṟat tāḻcciyāṉa pētaṅkaḷāy ārampittu varavara kampīrittuppōvatāṉa ōrvita kōṣṭikāṉam. piṟaku varavara uyarnta curaṅkaḷukkup pōy atituritamāka vittiyācam vittiyacamāṉa cura pētaṅkaḷuḷḷa peruṅkāṉamāki viṭukiṟaJ. ataṟkup piṉ iraṇṭuvita kāṉaṅkaḷum oṉṟu cērntu oru aṭi kāṉa amitalāṉa muṭivukku varukiṟatu. kiṟistuvukku muṉ mūṉṟāvatu nūṟṟāṇṭil eḻutappaṭṭa ōr caṅkīta nūlāṉatu ōr nāḷiṉ pala nimiṣaṅkaḷuḷ maṉita tēkattil irukkum iratta ōṭṭattiṉ nilikkum itaṉ mūlamāy maṉitakuṇam māṟum nilikkum campantappaṭuttik kāṇpikkiṟaJ.”
itil nām kavaṉikka vēṇṭiya toṉṟuṇṭu. sapta curaṅkaḷum mummūṉṟu pirivukaḷuṭiyaṉa vāyiruntaṉaveṉṟum, atāvatu curamum, curattiṟkuk koñcaṅkūṭiya curutiyum, curattiṟkuk koñcam kuṟinta curutiyumāka mūṉṟu eṉṟum collukiṟār. itik kavaṉippōmēyāṉāl, evvaḷavu kūṭiyirunta teṉṟum evvaḷavu kuṟintiruntateṉṟum oru kēḷviyuṇṭākum. curutikaḷip paṟṟiya cantēkam iṉṉum atikappaṭumēyoḻiya oru niccayattiṟkum varātu. curaṅkaḷ ēḻukkum mummūṉṟāka 21 curutikaḷākiṉṟaṉa. 22 eṉṟu coṉṉa curutikaḷukku iru virōtappaṭumē. ippaṭi niccayamillāta vārttikaḷic colluvatē curutikaḷip paṟṟip palapala apippirāyaṅkaḷ uṇṭāvataṟkuk kāraṇam. mēlum teṉtēca allatu tirāviṭa caṅkītamāṉatu vētaṅkaḷi ōtuvataṟku atikap pirayōcaṉa muḷḷatāyirukkiṟatu eṉkiṟār. vaṭatici cāstira viṟpaṉṉar oruvar teṉṉintiyāviṉ caṅkītam vētam ōtuvataṟku ēṟṟatāyirukkiṟateṉṟu colvati nām iṅkē kavaṉikka vēṇṭum. teṉṉintiyāvil rāvaṇēsvaraṉ sapta curaṅkaḷik koṇṭum cāmavētattik kāṉam paṇṇum muṟikku muntiṉavaṉāyiruntāṉeṉṟum avaṉ teṉṉintiyāviṉ camīpattiluḷḷa ilaṅkikkaracaṉeṉṟum nām aṟivōm. sapta curaṅkaḷōṭu cērntu vaḻaṅkiya pala curutikaḷum pūrvamuḷḷa tamiḻ nūlkaḷil collappaṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉa. itik koṇṭu, pūrvamāṉa vētakāṉattiṟkum maṟṟum kāṉattiṟkum teṉṉintiyāvē muntiṉatu eṉṟu colla niyāyamirukkiṟaJ.
2. teṉṉintiya caṅkītattil tēcikak kalappu vanta vitam.
teṉṉintiya caṅkītamāṉatu vaṭatēcattiṟkup palarālum koṇṭu pōkappaṭṭateṉṟum, maṟṟavarkaḷāl atu kiyāḷappaṭuṅ kālattil avaravarkaḷ pāṣikkum tēcattukkum takuntapaṭi pala pētaṅkaḷiyum aṭintateṉṟum piṉvarum vacaṉaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.
Oriental Music, P. 82 P. 30. Chinnaswami Moodr., M.A.
“Considering the prodigious number of nationalities and the diversity of provincial dialects in existence throughout the length and breadth of the Indian Empire, it should be no matter for astonishment if there be found any number of heterogeneous systems, as well as incongruous classifications in standard works forming the musical literature of the land. The primary distinction is into two classes, Marga (celestial) and Desi (terrestrial); the latter is now broadly divided into Hindustani and Karnata, the former representing the school established by Hanuma, and the latter the much more ancient and authentic system introduced by Narada, the inventor of all arts and sciences. It is clear however that local tastes and methods of trining have considerably upset the theories originally propounded.
In the extreme north, there is a system of six Ragas and thirty-six Raginis which are grouped together diferently by different authors; in the west of India the divergencies are still wider, through the origin is traceable to the same source; in the extreme south only thirty-two are recognised as principal Ragas, of which 8 are classed as Purusha and 24 as Stri, while a few more are designatred by other fanciful names. Another classification is into 32 ancient and 42 recent Ragas. All these are manifestly incomplete. Of late the Hindustani element (which has itself much deteriorated owing to foreign admixture) has been ingrafted on the Dravidian modes to an alarming extent, so that it is a matter of no small difficulty to distinguish the purely classical from the adulterated systems. The tendency is at present to demolish all system and to sil clear of all trammels rules and regulations imposed by the ancient framers of the science; but it is evident that this is not the proper method of effecting reform or insuring progress. Each system should be taken up separately by itself, and while its true original and individual character is jealously mintined, it should be divested of all useless encumbrances and incrustations which obstruct or retard improvement.”
kīḻttici caṅkītattiṉ vicēṣa amcaṅkaḷum atippaṟṟiya pala apippirāyaṅkaḷum.
“intiya rājyamāṉatu anēka jātiyār niṟintuḷḷatāyum, mākāṇaṅkaḷ tōṟum vevvēṟu pāṣikaḷ vaḻaṅkuvatāyum iruppatāl, caṅkīta cāstira viṣayattil palavita vitatiyācaṅkaḷuḷḷa palamuṟikaḷum caṅkīta cāstirap pustakaṅkaḷi aṭṭavaṇi muṟiyāy oḻuṅkupaṭutta muyaṉṟa mukkiya nūlkaḷil anēka vittiyācaṅkaḷum ēṟpaṭṭu yāvum kuḻappamāyk kāṇappaṭuvatu avvaḷavu āccariyamāṉa kāriyamalla. ātiyil caṅkītamāṉatu iraṇṭu pirivuḷḷatākak kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. avi mārkkam allatu tēvakāṉam, tēcikam allatu ivvulaka kāṉam eṉpavikaḷām. ivikaḷil tēcikamāṉatu intustāṉi, karnāṭakam eṉṟa iraṇṭu pirivukaḷākak kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. avaṟṟuḷ muntiṉatu haṉumārāl ēṟpaṭuttappaṭṭa muṟiyeṉṟum, pintiṉatu ellā cāstirattukkum āti kāraṇarākiya nāratarāl ēṟpaṭuttappaṭṭa muṟiyeṉṟum koḷḷappaṭukiṟatu. intap pintiṉamuṟi muntiṉativiṭa pūrvīkameṉṟum, muṟṟilum cariyeṉṟu nampakkūṭiyateṉṟum koḷḷappaṭukiṟatu. āṉālum, ātiyil stāpitta muṟikaḷ, iṭapētattiṉālum collik koṭukkum muṟi pētattiṉālum atika vittiyācamuḷḷavikaḷāyp pōyiṉa eṉṟu naṉṟāy veḷiyākiṟatu.
vaṭatēcattil, āṟu irākaṅkaḷum 36 rākiṉikaḷum aṭaṅkiya orumuṟi iruppatākavum, avi pala vitavāṉkaḷāl palavitamāy aṭukkic collappaṭuvatāyum terikiṟatu. mēṟkut tēca caṅkītamō itē muṟiyiliruntu uṇṭāṉa pōtilum, vittiyācaṅkaḷ iṉṉum aṉantam uṭiyaṉa. teṉṉāṭṭil tāy rākaṅkaḷ 32 tāṉ eṉṟum, avikaḷil 8 puruṣarākaṅkaḷeṉṟum, 24 stirī rākaṅkaḷeṉṟum, colvatuṭaṉ maṟṟavikaḷukku anēka vēṭikkip peyar koṭuttum aḻikkiṟārkaḷ. iṉṉoru muṟiyeṉṉaveṉṟāl, pūrvarākaṅkaḷ 32 eṉṟum, taṟkāla rākaṅkaḷ 42 eṉṟum pirippatu. āṉāl iviyellām muṭivaṟṟa vakuppukaḷēyeṉṟu pariṣkāramāyt terikiṟatu.
taṟkālattil vēṟṟuc caṅkītattiṉ kalappāl atika vittiyācamaṭintirukkiṟa intustāṉi caṅkītam tirāviṭa caṅkītattōṭu aparimitamāyk kalantiruppatāṉatu, tirāviṭa caṅkītattukku nēriṭakkūṭiya apāyattiyum kalappillāta pūrvacaṅkītattukkum kalappuḷḷa taṟkāla caṅkītattukkumuḷḷa vittiyācattiyum kaṇṭupiṭikka muṭiyāmikku ētuvāyirukkiṟatu. ivvitak kaṣṭaṅkaḷiruppatāl taṟkālattil yāvarum ceykiṟateṉṉaveṉṟāl, ellā muṟiyiyum tavirttu muṟkālattu vitvāṉkaḷāl ēṟpaṭuttappaṭṭa oḻuṅkukaḷ ellāvaṟṟiyum oḻittu viṭuvatē. āṉāl ippaṭic ceyvatu cīrtiruttañ ceytalukkum virutti ceytalukkum aḻakalla. āṉāl ceyya vēṇṭiyatu eṉṉaveṉṟāl, ovvoru muṟiyiyum taṉittaṉiyē eṭuttuk koṇṭu, ataṉuṭiya vicēṣitta āti amcattiviṭṭu nilipeyara viṭāmal, ataṉ viruttikku iṭiyūṟāyirukkakkūṭiya pirayōcaṉamaṟṟa vaḻivakikaḷ yāviyum vilakki, avikaḷic cīrtirutta vēṇṭiyatēyām.”
itik kavaṉikkiyil mārkkam tēcikam eṉkiṟa pirivukaḷ iraṇṭum caṅkītattiliruntateṉṟum mārkkam eṉpatu tēvakāṉameṉṟum, tēcikam eṉpatu intustāṉi karnāṭakameṉṟa iraṇṭu pirivukaḷuḷḷateṉṟum, muntiṉarākiya intustāṉi aṉumārāl ceyyappaṭṭateṉṟum, pintiṉatākiya karnāṭakam nāratarāl ēṟpaṭuttappaṭṭateṉṟum collukiṟār. ellāc cāstiraṅkaḷukkum ātikāraṇarākiya nāratarāl stāpikkappaṭṭa muṟi cariyāṉateṉṟum, pūrvīkamāṉateṉṟum, iṭapētaṅkaḷiṉāl māṟutal aṭintateṉṟum collukiṟār. āṉāl mārkkam eṉṟa muṟiyē riṣikaḷāl uṇṭākkappaṭṭateṉṟum, ataṉiyē anumār, nāratar, rāvaṇēsparaṉ mutaliyavarkaḷ kāṉam ceytateṉṟum, atuvē cariyāṉa muṟiyeṉṟum, teṉṉintiyāviṉ caṅkītamākiya karnāṭakam cuttam eṉṟu aḻikkappaṭuvataṟkut takutiyuḷḷateṉṟum nām aṟiya vēṇṭum. anumārāl intustāṉi uṇṭāyiṟṟeṉṟu colluvatu muṟṟilum ovvāJ. nāratarāl ceyyappaṭṭa mārkka muṟi vaṭa tēcattil ceṉṟu tēcaṅkaḷtōṟum vevvēṟu curaṅkaḷ kalakkap peṟṟatiṉāl tēcikameṉṟu peyar peṟṟatu. curañāṉamillāta ciṟuvarkaḷum stirīkaḷum piṟarum tēcika muṟiyi aṉucarikkalāmeṉṟum collappaṭukiṟatu. tēcikam eṉpatu caṅkīta ilakkaṇam tavirntu vaḻaṅkiya vaḻuvamitiyāṉa oru muṟiyeṉṟu nām ñāpakattil vikka vēṇṭum. mēlum teṉṉāṭṭil pūrvakālattilēyē 11,991 paṇkaḷiruntatākac cilappatikārattiṉ paḻiya urikārar colliyirukkiṟār. teṉṉāṭṭil piratimattimattōṭu pāṭappaṭum kalyāṇi vaṭa nāṭṭiṟkup pōy kiramam mīṟi piratimattimattōṭu vēṟu iraṇṭu mattimaṅkaḷiyum appaṭiyē kāntāra niṣātaṅkaḷilum iraṇṭu iraṇṭu curaṅkaḷiyum cērttuk koṇṭu teṉṉāṭṭukkuvara, teṉṉāṭṭiluḷḷōr ati mikavum aḻakāyirukkiṟateṉṟu paḻakukiṟārkaḷ. ippaṭiyē āṉanta piravi, kāmpōti, tōṭi mutaliya rākaṅkaḷum varavarak kalappuṟṟatāy mārkka muṟiyi iḻantu, muṟṟilum tēcikamāki, karnāṭaka caṅkītattiṉ uyarvik keṭukkum nilikku vantirukkiṉṟaṉa. ippaṭic curaṅkaḷ kalakkak kalakka, karnāṭaka rākaṅkaḷ yāvum orē rākam pōl tōṉṟumpaṭiyākiviṭum. intustāṉi mutaliya kītaṅkaḷil taṟkālattil nām collum kuṟikaḷiyē karnāṭaka caṅkītattiṟkum colla nēriṭum.
karnāṭaka caṅkītam mikavum oḻuṅkuḷḷa mēlāṉa caṅkītameṉṟuma, putta caṅkītamum makammatiya caṅkītamum tēcikameṉṟu vaḻaṅki vantaṉaveṉṟum piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil kāṇalām.


Hindu Music and the Gayan Samaj, P. I. P. 8.
“The Margi System, although preserved still in Sanskrit works on Music, owing to want of cultivation, political influence, and other adventitious circumstances has almost become extinct. Desi with its numerous ramifications is the system now obtining in India. Music is divided into Nibadha and Anibadha, that set in words and that not, the former being Margi and the latter Desi. The Desi System first acquired importance from the Buddhist musicians, and received fuller development from Mussulmans who introduced khyal from the Hindu Dhruvapada system and from that the Tappa. Besides these, there is the southern Indian system, distinct in itself, and constituting an important section of the Indian musical system, termed the Carnataka system.”
“mārkkam’ eṉṉum muṟiyāṉatu, caṅkīta kiranta nūlkaḷil innāḷ varikkum kāṇappaṭṭa pōtilum, appiyācam, turittaṉattāriṉ ātarippu mutaliya viruttikkuriya ētukkaḷ illāmal perumpālum arukip pōyviṭṭatu. āṉāl tēcikamum atil kiḷitta anēka muṟikaḷuntāṉ taṟkālam intiyāvil vaḻaṅkappaṭṭu varukiṉṟaṉa. caṅkītamāṉatu nipatam, anipatam eṉṟu iruvakippaṭum. nipatam cākittiyamuṭiyaJ. anipatam cākittiyamaṟṟaJ. muntiṉatu mārkkam. pintiṉatu tēcikam. tēcikamuṟiyāṉatu mutal mutal putta caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷāl perumiyaṭintu piṟaku makamatiyarāl pūraṇam peṟṟatu. makammatiyar intu turuvapata muṟiyiṉiṉṟu kiyāl eṉṉappaṭṭa muṟiyiyum, kiyāl eṉṉum muṟiyiṉiṉṟu ṭappā eṉṉum muṟiyiyum ēṟpaṭuttiṉārkaḷ. inta muṟikaḷiyallāmal teṉṉintiya karnāṭaka muṟiyeṉṟu oṉṟum uṇṭu. itu maṟṟavika ḷōṭu kalavāmal intiya caṅkīta muṟikaḷil veku mukkiyamāṉa ōr muṟiyāy viḷaṅkik koṇṭirukkiṟaJ.”
mēṟkaṇṭa vacaṉaṅkaḷik kavaṉikkiyil, mārkkam eṉṉum muṟi appiyācippārillāmal nūl aḷavākavē niṉṟuviṭṭateṉṟum, tēcikam palavāṟākak kiḷittu viruttiyāki viṭṭateṉṟum teriya varukiṟatu. teṉṉintiya caṅkītattip pūrṇamāyaṟiyāta puttarumavarkkup piṉ makammatiyarum tēcikattip paṭittu vantārkaḷ. curañāṉamillāmal pala curaṅkaḷ kalanta tēcikam atikamāy vaḻaṅkum vaṭanāṭṭiluḷḷa oruvar, maṟṟavikaḷōṭu kalavāta karnāṭaka caṅkītam eṉṉum oru mukkiya muṟiyirukkiṟateṉṟu colluvati nām kavaṉikka vēṇṭum. mēlum, avarkaḷ vaḻaṅki varukiṟa turpat, tillāṉā, kiyāl, ṭappā, ṭōmari mutaliya kīta muṟikaḷ mikac culapamāṉaviyeṉṟu nām aṟivōm. āṉāl karnāṭakattil vaḻaṅki varum kītam, tāṉavarṇam, cukkavarṇam, kīrttaṉam, pallavi, rākamāliki mutaliyavi, caṅkīta cāstirattukkup poruntumpaṭi ceyyappaṭṭu mikunta tāḷa amippuṭaṉ tērcciyaṭintiruppavi eṉṟu nām aṟivōm.
makammatiyarāl viruttikkuk koṇṭu varappaṭṭa intustāṉi eṉṉum tēcikamuṟiyāṉatu, teṉṉintiya caṅkītattiṉiṉṟē uṇṭāṉa muṟiyeṉṟum, teṉṉintiyāviliruntu vaṭanāṭṭukkup pōṉateṉṟum, teṉṉintiya caṅkīta vitvāṉkaḷ allāvuṭīṉ eṉpavarāl ciṟikaḷāka vaṭanāṭṭukkuk koṇṭu pōkappaṭṭārkaḷeṉṟum, avvāṟu koṇṭu pōkappaṭṭavarkaḷil takṣaṇattic cērnta caṅkīta vitvāṉ nāyak kōpāl eṉpavar oruvareṉṟum piṉvarum vākkiyaṅkaḷil kāṇkiṟōm. itil kaṇṭa nāyak kōpāl eṉpavar cumār 400 varuṣaṅkaḷukku muṉ tāḷārṇavam, rākakatampam, pirapantam mutaliya nūlkaḷ ceytavareṉṟu cāraṅka tēvar nūlukku uriyeḻutiya kallinātar collukiṟāreṉṟu cupparāmatīkṣatar kūṟukiṟār. ivar curutiyip paṟṟi naṉṟāyt terintavareṉṟu caturtaṇṭi pirakācikiyeḻutiya veṅkaṭamaki collukiṟār. ivar tīpakam eṉṉum rākam pāṭi oruvarum ēṟṟāmalē oru viḷakki eriyumpaṭic ceytāreṉṟum collappaṭṭirukkiṟatu.
Universal History of Music, P. 84.
“The Mahomedans as a ruling nation came in contact with the people of India for the first time in the 11th century, and since then a change has been worked into the music system of the country. The Mahomedans did not encourage the theory of the art, but they patronized practical musicians and were themselves instrumental in composing and introducing several styles of songs or devising new forms of musical instruments. It is related by Mahomedan historians of the period that when Dacca was invaded by Alludin in 1294 and the conquest of the south of India was completed (1310) by his Mogul general Malik Kafer, music was in such a flourishing condition, that all the musicians and their Hindu preceptors were taken with the armies, and settled in the North. It is sid that the celebrated Persian poet and musician Amir Khosru came to India during the rule of Alludin and defeated in a contest the musician of the South, Nayak Gopal, who had come to Delhi with a view to challenge the musicians of the court. Amir Khosru is reported to have given the name of Safar to the Tritantri Vina of the classic days and to have divided the Rags into twelve Mokams which were subsequently subdivided by other Mahomedan musicians into 24 Sobha and 48 Guswas.”
